Exponential Mixing of Vlasov equations under the effect of Gravity and Boundary
Jiaxin Jin & Chanwoo Kim

Abstract
. In this paper, we study exponentially fast mixing induced/enhanced by gravity and stochastic boundary in the kinetic theory of Vlasov equations. We consider the Vlasov equations with and without a vertical magnetic field inside a horizontally-periodic 3D half-space equipped with a non-isothermal diffusive reflection boundary condition of bounded continuous boundary temperature at the bottom. We construct both stationary solutions and global-in-time dynamical solutions in L ∞ L^{\infty} . We prove that moments of a dynamical fluctuation around the steady solutions decay exponentially fast in L ∞ L^{\infty} . As a key of this proof, we establish a uniform bound of so-called residual measures independently of the bouncing number of stochastic characteristics, by constructing a continuous stationary outgoing boundary flux which is strictly positive almost everywhere.



I
NTRODUCTION

Dynamics of molecules under gravity has been an important object in various mathematical studies from microscopic to macroscopic description
[
11
,
23
,
26
,
30
]
. In this paper, we consider a many body problem of molecules without intermoleculer interaction which are contained in a horizontally-periodic three dimensional half-space
\Omega=\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}
and subjected to the gravity (positive gravitational constant
g>0
) with/without a vertical magnetic field. A governing kinetic model of the system is the Vlasov equations:

\partial_{t}F+v\cdot\nabla_{x}F+(v\times B-\nabla\phi-g\mathbf{e}_{3})\cdot\nabla_{v}F=0\ \ \text{in}\ \ (t,x,v)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}.

(0.1)

Such equations are used in the kinetic model of solar wind (see
[
5
]
and the references therein).

At the bottom of domain, the molecules interact with the boundary thermodynamically via a
diffusive reflection
boundary condition

F(\cdot,x,v)=\mu_{\Theta}(x,v)\int_{n(x)\cdot v^{1}>0}F(\cdot,x,v^{1})\{n(x)\cdot v^{1}\}\mathrm{d}v^{1}\ \ \text{for}\ \ (x,v)\in\gamma_{-}:=\{x\in\partial\Omega\ \text{and}\ v_{3}>0\},

(0.2)

such that an outgoing distribution is proportional to the thermal equilibrium of boundary temperature
\Theta(x)>0
:

\mu_{\Theta}(x,v)=\frac{1}{2\pi\Theta(x)^{2}}e^{-\frac{|v|^{2}}{2\Theta(x)}}\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \text{(wall Maxwellian)}.

(0.3)

Due to the normalization

\int_{n(x)\cdot v^{1}>0}\mu_{\Theta}(x,v^{1})\{n(x)\cdot v^{1}\}\mathrm{d}v^{1}=1
,
we have a null flux at the boundary and enjoy the conservation of total mass. Throughout this paper we assume the boundary temperature
\Theta(x)
is

continuous
on
\partial\Omega
, so that

0<a\leq\Theta(x)\leq b<\infty,\ \text{for all}\ x\in\partial\Omega.

(0.4)

Main Theorem.
The main interest in this paper is to study a long-time behavior of solutions to the Vlasov equations for both a case without magnetic field

\begin{split}B=\vec{0},\ \ \text{and}\ \ \|\nabla_{x}\phi\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\leq g/2,\ \|e^{\varrho_{1}x_{3}}\nabla_{x}^{2}\phi\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\leq\varrho_{2}\ll 1,\ \phi(x)|_{x_{3}=0}\equiv 0;\end{split}

(0.5)

and a case with vertical magnetic field

\begin{split}B=(0,0,B_{3})\ \text{with a constant}\ B_{3}\geq 1,\ \ \text{and}\ \ \nabla\phi\equiv 0.\end{split}

(0.6)

First of all, it is well-known that stationary solutions to (
0.1
)-(
0.2
) are neither given by explicit formulas nor are equilibria (local Maxwellian) in general, if they exist. We refer to
[
27
,
22
,
23
]
for special cases in which a simple form of stationary solutions are available. In this paper, we construct a unique stationary solution by establishing
L^{\infty}
-bound using stochastic cycles and a natural
L^{1}
-bound from mass. This method is direct/robust and in particular does not rely on the dynamical stability. Second, we prove exponentially fast asymptotic stability of such stationary solutions. Gravity plays an important role to enhance the convergence speed as only polynomial decay
[
31
,
2
,
16
,
24
]
is possible without the gravity due to possible slow particles. For the other mixing mechanisms, we refer to
[
8
,
32
]
in the active transport equations,
[
1
,
25
,
15
]
for the Landau damping, and
[
21
,
3
,
18
,
19
]
for the Boltzmann equation. Precise statements and remarks follow.

Theorem 1
.

We consider both (
0.5
) and (
0.6
). We assume a continuous boundary temperature
\Theta(x)
satisfies (
0.4
). Given any
\mathfrak{m}>0
, there exists a unique solution
F_{s}(x,v)
to the stationary Vlasov equation

v\cdot\nabla_{x}F_{s}+(v\times B-\nabla\phi-g\mathbf{e}_{3})\cdot\nabla_{v}F_{s}=0,\ \ \text{in}\ \Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3},

(0.7)

with either (
0.5
) or (
0.6
); and the diffusive reflection boundary condition (
0.2
)
such that

\iint_{\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}F_{s}(x,v)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}v=\mathfrak{m}.

(0.8)

Moreover, we have, for
b
in (
0.4
),

\left\|e^{\frac{1}{2b}\big(\frac{|v|^{2}}{2}+\Phi(x)\big)}F_{s}(x,v)\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\lesssim\mathfrak{m}.

(0.9)

Theorem 2
.

We consider both (
0.5
) and (
0.6
). Assume all conditions in Theorem
1
. Let
F_{s}(x,v)
be a stationary solution in Theorem
1
. Consider the initial data
F_{0}(x,v)=F_{s}(x,v)+f_{0}(x,v)
such that
\iint_{\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f_{0}(x,v)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}v=0
.
\|e^{\theta^{\prime}(|v|^{2}+2\Phi(x))}f_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}<\infty
for
0<\theta^{\prime}<\frac{1}{2b}
with
b
defined in (
0.4
), and
\|e^{\delta(|v|^{2}+2\Phi(x))^{1/2}}f_{0}\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}<\infty
with
0<\delta<\infty
. There exists a unique global-in-time solution

F(t,x,v)=F_{s}(x,v)+f(t,x,v)\geq 0

(0.10)

to (
0.1
) with either (
0.5
) or (
0.6
) and the boundary condition (
0.2
) with the initial condition
F(t,x,v)|_{t=0}=F_{0}(x,v)
in
\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
,
such that

\iint_{\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f(t,x,v)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}v=0,\ \ \text{for all }t\geq 0,

(0.11)

\sup_{t\geq 0}\|e^{\theta^{\prime}(|v|^{2}+2\Phi(x))}f(t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\lesssim\|e^{\theta^{\prime}(|v|^{2}+2\Phi(x))}f_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}.

(0.12)

Moreover, for all
t\geq 0
and
0\leq\theta<\theta^{\prime}
,

\begin{split}\sup_{x\in\bar{\Omega}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{\theta(|v|^{2}+2\Phi(x))}|f(t,x,v)|\mathrm{d}v\lesssim_{\theta}e^{-\Lambda t},\end{split}

(0.13)

where
\Lambda
is defined in (
4.69
).

Difficulties and Ideas.
The main/basic idea of to construct stationary solutions and prove its stability is our novel
L^{1}-L^{\infty}
bootstrap, which allow us to transfer a velocity mixing of the diffusive reflection boundary condition to a spatial mixing through the characteristics of
the stochastic cycles
:

Definition 1

(Stochastic Cycles)
.

Define the backward exit time
t_{\mathbf{b}}
and the forward exit time
t_{\mathbf{f}}
,

\begin{split}&t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v):=\sup\{s\geq 0:X(t-\tau;t,x,v)\in\Omega,\ \forall\tau\in[0,s)\},\ x_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v):=X(t-t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v);t,x,v),\\
&t_{\mathbf{f}}(x,v):=\sup\{s\geq 0:X(t+\tau;t,x,v)\in\Omega,\ \forall\tau\in[0,s)\},\ x_{\mathbf{f}}(x,v):=X(t+t_{\mathbf{f}}(x,v);t,x,v).\end{split}

(0.14)

We define the stochastic cycles:

t^{1}(t,x,v)=t-t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v),\ x^{1}(x,v)=x_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)=X(t^{1},t,x,v),\ v_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)=V(t^{1},t,x,v),

\begin{split}&t^{k}(t,x,v,v^{1},...,v^{k-1})=t^{k-1}-t_{\mathbf{b}}(x^{k-1},v^{k-1}),\ t_{\mathbf{b}}^{k}=t_{\mathbf{f}}^{k+1}=t^{k}-t^{k+1},\\
&x^{k}(t,x,v,v^{1},...,v^{k-1})=X(t^{k};t^{k-1},x^{k-1},v^{k-1}),\ v_{\mathbf{b}}^{k}=V(t^{k+1};t^{k},x^{k},v^{k}),\end{split}

(0.15)

where we define
v^{j}\in\mathcal{V}_{j}:=\{v^{j}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}:n(x^{j})\cdot v^{j}>0\}
with the measure

\mathrm{d}\sigma_{j}=\mathrm{d}\sigma_{j}(x^{j})
on
\mathcal{V}_{j}
which is given by

\mathrm{d}\sigma_{j}:=\mu_{\Theta}(x^{j+1},v_{\mathbf{b}}^{j})\{n(x^{j})\cdot v^{j}\}\mathrm{d}v^{j}.

(0.16)

Here,
n(x)
is the outward normal at
x\in\partial\Omega
.

We encounter a difficulty in the presence of magnetic field as the change of variable
v\mapsto(t_{\mathbf{b}},S_{x_{\mathbf{b}}})
becomes degenerate:

\mathrm{d}v=5B^{2}_{3}(2-2\cos(B_{3}t^{i}_{\mathbf{b}}))^{-1}\mathrm{d}t_{\mathbf{b}}\mathrm{d}S_{x_{\mathbf{b}}}.

(0.17)

Note that the denominator
2-2\cos(B_{3}t^{i}_{\mathbf{b}})
can be very small when
B_{3}t^{i}_{\mathbf{b}}
approaches
2k\pi
with
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
.
Thus, we split velocity
v
into the following two parts:

\begin{split}&\mathcal{V}^{G_{\mathbf{\varepsilon}}}(x):=\{v\in\mathcal{V}:\frac{\varepsilon}{B_{3}}+\frac{2k\pi}{B_{3}}\leq t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)\leq\frac{2\pi-\varepsilon}{B_{3}}+\frac{2k\pi}{B_{3}}\ \text{with}\ k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}\},\\
&\mathcal{V}^{B_{\mathbf{\varepsilon}}}(x):=\{v\in\mathcal{V}:-\frac{\varepsilon}{B_{3}}+\frac{2k\pi}{B_{3}}\leq t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{B_{3}}+\frac{2k\pi}{B_{3}}\ \text{with}\ k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}\}.\end{split}

(0.18)

Although the Jacobian is large when
v\in\mathcal{V}^{B_{\mathbf{\varepsilon}}}
, we can control the integration of such part small inside the stochastic cycles (see Lemma
11
). This is because when
v\in\mathcal{V}^{B_{\mathbf{\varepsilon}}}
, the size of range of
t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)
in each period
[\frac{2k\pi}{B_{3}},\frac{2(k+1)\pi}{B_{3}}]
with
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
is
\frac{2\varepsilon}{B_{3}}
. On the other hand, we deduce the upped bound when
v\in\mathcal{V}^{G_{\mathbf{\varepsilon}}}
since the denominator in Jacobian is away from zero (see Lemma
13
and Proposition
5
). We crucially use this
L^{1}-L^{\infty}
bootstrap to construct stationary solutions based on a pointwise estimate boundary outgoing flux for
x\in\partial\Omega
,

\mathfrak{J}(x)=\int_{n(x)\cdot v>0}F_{s}(x,v)\{n(x)\cdot v\}\mathrm{d}v.

(0.19)

The proof of dynamical stability is based on 1) the Doeblin-type pointwise lower bound with a unreachable defect (Proposition
8
)

f(NT_{0},x,v)\geq\mathfrak{m}(x,v)\Big\{\iint_{\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f((N-1)T_{0},x,v)\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x-\iint_{\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{f}}(x,v)\geq\frac{T_{0}}{4}}f((N-1)T_{0},x,v)\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x\Big\};

(0.20)

2) weight energy estimate to control the unreachable defect (Theorem
7
). In contrast to no-gravity case of
[
16
]
, we are able to employ an exponential weight
\varphi(\tau)=e^{\delta\tau}

{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|f\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}:=\|f\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}+\Big(1+\frac{1}{\delta T_{0}}\Big)\frac{4\mathfrak{m}_{T_{0}}}{\varphi(\frac{T_{0}}{4})}\|\varphi(t_{\mathbf{f}})f\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}};

(0.21)

3) prove a decay of an exponential moments in
L^{\infty}
using
L^{1}-L^{\infty}
bootstrap.

Regarding the first component, we carefully split the characteristics in the presence of magnetic field as the crucial mixing by diffuse reflection boundary operator is degenerate. About the second ingredient, a key observation is that gravity control the vertical trajectory so that
t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v),t_{\mathbf{f}}(x,v)\leq|v_{3}|
, which allows
\varphi
of an exponential growth can make

\int_{n(x)\cdot v<0}\varphi(t_{\mathbf{f}})\mu_{\Theta}(x,v)|n(x)\cdot v|\mathrm{d}v<\infty.

(0.22)

We prove an exponential decay in
L^{1}
using an energy estimate with an exponential weight.

Finally, a major difficulty arises at the last ingredient when we control the
L^{\infty}
-norm of the fluctuation
f(t,x,v)
via the stochastic cycles (see (
4.5
)-(
4.7
) for details). It turns out crucial to control so-called
i
-residual measure
uniformly-in-
i
:

\int_{\prod_{j=0}^{i-1}\mathcal{V}_{j}}\mathbf{1}_{0<t_{i}\leq t}\ \mathrm{d}\sigma_{i-1}\cdots\mathrm{d}\sigma_{1}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{0}.

(0.23)

We note that
\mathrm{d}\sigma_{j}=\mu_{\Theta}(x^{j+1},v_{\mathbf{b}}^{j})\{n(x^{j})\cdot v^{j}\}\mathrm{d}v^{j}
is not a probability measure in general. Therefore, we could experience an exponential growth of
i
-residual measure where
i
is proportional to the time
t
. We overcome this difficulty by constructing a continuous outgoing boundary flux
\mathfrak{J}(x)
and bound the
i
-residual measure using an upper/lower bound of
\mathfrak{J}(x)
(see the strategy described at the beginning of Section
5
). The downward gravity is crucial to prove
\mathfrak{J}(x)
continuous. The continuity of
\mathfrak{J}(x)
closely relates to continuity/regularity of solutions to the transport equation (
[
17
,
6
,
11
,
20
]
).

1.
Background

1.1.
Characteristics and basic properties

For the zero-magnetic field case of (
0.5
)
, the characteristics of (
0.1
) are determined by the Hamilton ODEs

\begin{cases}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}s}X(s;t,x,v)=V(s;t,x,v),\\
\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}s}V(s;t,x,v)=-\nabla\Phi(X(s;t,x,v)),\end{cases}

(1.1)

for
-\infty<s,t<\infty
and
\Phi(x)
defined in (
0.5
) with
(X(t;t,x,v),V(t;t,x,v))=(x,v)
.

For the nonzero-magnetic field case of (
0.6
),
the characteristics of (
0.1
) solve

\begin{cases}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}s}X(s;t,x,v)=V(s;t,x,v),\\
\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}s}V(s;t,x,v)=V(s;t,x,v)\times B-\nabla\Phi(X(s;t,x,v)),\end{cases}

(1.2)

where
B,\Phi(x)
take a form of (
0.6
) with
(X(t;t,x,v),V(t;t,x,v))=(x,v)
.

We list some properties that both zero-magnetic (
1.1
) and nonzero-magnetic field cases (
1.2
).

Lemma 1

(
[
3
,
9
]
)
.

For any
g
and
(X,V)
in (
1.1
) (resp. (
1.2
)), we have

\displaystyle\int_{\gamma_{+}}\int_{0}^{t_{-}}g(t,X(t,t+s,x,v),V(t,t+s,x,v))|n(x)\cdot v|\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}S_{x}=\iint_{\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}g(t,y,v)\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}v,

(1.3)

\displaystyle\int_{\gamma_{-}}\int_{0}^{t_{+}}g(t,X(t,t-s,x,v),V(t,t-s,x,v))|n(x)\cdot v|\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}S_{x}=\iint_{\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}g(t,y,v)\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}v,

(1.4)

\displaystyle\int_{\gamma_{\pm}}g(t,x_{\mp}(x,v),v_{\mp}(x,v))|n(x)\cdot v|\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}S_{x}=\int_{\gamma_{\mp}}g(t,y,v)|n(y)\cdot v|\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}S_{y}.

(1.5)

Here, for the sake of simplicity, we have abused the notations temporarily:
t_{-}=t_{\mathbf{b}},x_{-}=x_{\mathbf{b}}
and
t_{+}=t_{\mathbf{f}},x_{+}=x_{\mathbf{f}}
.

Proof.

The proof is similar to the proof for Lemma 3 in
[
3
]
. For the completeness of the paper, we only sketch the proof.

First we consider zero-magnetic case. To derive (
1.3
) and (
1.4
), we consider for fixed t, the map

(x,v)\in\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\mapsto(t-t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v),x_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v),v_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v))\in\mathbb{R}\times\gamma_{-}.

We can check that this map is one-to-one, and we compute the determinate of the matrix from the change of variables. Applying the following the equality

\begin{split}&\nabla_{x,v}x_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)=\nabla_{x,v}t_{\mathbf{b}}v_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)+\nabla_{x,v}X(t-t_{\mathbf{b}};t,x,v),\\
&\nabla_{x,v}v_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)=-\nabla_{x,v}t_{\mathbf{b}}\nabla\Phi(x_{\mathbf{b}})+\nabla_{x,v}V(t-t_{\mathbf{b}};t,x,v),\end{split}

and the matrix transformation, the Liouville theorem that
\det[\frac{\partial(X,V)}{\partial(x,v)}]=1
, we derive

\big|\det\Big[\frac{\partial(t-t_{\mathbf{b}},x_{\mathbf{b}},v_{\mathbf{b}})}{\partial(x,v)}\Big]\big|=\frac{1}{|v_{\mathbf{b}}\cdot n(x_{\mathbf{b}})|}.

Then we show (
1.4
). Similarly, we can derive (
1.3
) if we consider the map

(x,v)\in\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\mapsto(t+t_{\mathbf{f}}(x,v),x_{\mathbf{f}}(x,v),v_{\mathbf{f}}(x,v))\in\mathbb{R}\times\gamma_{+}.

Next, for (
1.5
), we consider the map

(t,x,v)\in\mathbb{R}\times\gamma_{+}\mapsto(t-t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v),x_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v),v_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v))\in\mathbb{R}\times\gamma_{-}.

Again we can get the map is one-to-one, and compute the determinate of the matrix from the change of variables. From the matrix transformation and the fact
t_{\mathbf{b}}
is independent of
t
, we derive

\big|\det\Big[\frac{\partial(x_{\mathbf{b}},v_{\mathbf{b}})}{\partial(x,v)}\Big]\big|=\big|\det\Big[\frac{\partial(t-t_{\mathbf{b}},x_{\mathbf{b}},v_{\mathbf{b}})}{\partial(t,x,v)}\Big]\big|=\frac{|v\cdot n(x)|}{|v_{\mathbf{b}}\cdot n(x_{\mathbf{b}})|}.

which implies (
1.5
).

The proof for the magnetic case can be done in a parallel way of zero-magnetic proof as the Liouville theorem shows
\det[\frac{\partial(X,V)}{\partial(x,v)}]=1
from
\nabla_{v}(V\times B-\nabla\Phi(X))=0
.
∎

Following Lemma
2
, we derive the stochastic cycles

Lemma 2

(
[
3
,
9
]
)
.

Suppose
F
solve (
0.7
) and (
0.2
) (resp. (
0.1
) and (
0.2
)) with
0\leq t_{*}\leq t
, then for
k\geq 1
,

\displaystyle F(x,v)=

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}_{t^{1}<t_{*}}F(X(t_{*};t,x,v),V(t_{*};t,x,v))

(1.6)

\displaystyle+\mu_{\Theta}(x^{1},v_{\mathbf{b}})\int_{\prod_{j=1}^{i}\mathcal{V}_{j}}\sum\limits^{k-1}_{i=1}\Big\{\mathbf{1}_{t^{i+1}<t_{*}\leq t^{i}}F(X(t_{*};t^{i},x^{i},v^{i}),V(t_{*};t^{i},x^{i},v^{i}))\Big\}\mathrm{d}\Sigma_{i}

(1.7)

\displaystyle+\mu_{\Theta}(x^{1},v_{\mathbf{b}})\int_{\prod_{j=1}^{k}\mathcal{V}_{j}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{k}\geq t_{*}}F(x^{k},v^{k})\mathrm{d}\Sigma_{k},

(1.8)

where

\mathrm{d}{\Sigma}_{i}:=\frac{\mathrm{d}\sigma_{i}}{\mu_{\Theta}(x^{i+1},v_{\mathbf{b}}^{i})}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{i-1}\cdots\mathrm{d}\sigma_{1}
, with
\mathrm{d}\sigma_{j}=\mu_{\Theta}(x^{j+1},v_{\mathbf{b}}^{j})\{n(x^{j})\cdot v^{j}\}\mathrm{d}v^{j}
in (
0.16
), and
v_{\mathbf{b}}^{j}=v_{\mathbf{b}}(x^{j},v^{j})
defined in (
0.15
).
Here,
(X,V)
in (
1.1
) (resp. (
1.2
)).

Proof.

We will prove this lemma by induction for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
.
When
n=1
, then it has two cases
t^{1}<t_{*}\leq t
and
t_{*}\leq t^{1}\leq t
. For
t^{1}<t_{*}\leq t
, we have

F(x,v)=F(X(t_{*};t,x,v),V(t_{*};t,x,v)).

For
t_{*}\leq t^{1}\leq t
, we get

F(x,v)=F(x^{1},v_{\mathbf{b}})=\mu_{\Theta}(x^{1},v_{\mathbf{b}})\int_{\mathcal{V}_{1}}F(x^{1},v^{1})\mathrm{d}\Sigma_{1}.

Combining the above two cases, we derive that

F(x,v)=\mathbf{1}_{t^{1}<t_{*}}F(X(t_{*};t,x,v),V(t_{*};t,x,v))+\mu_{\Theta}(x^{1},v_{\mathbf{b}})\int_{\mathcal{V}_{1}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{1}\geq t_{*}}F(x^{1},v^{1})\mathrm{d}\Sigma_{1},

so the equality (
1.6
)-(
1.8
) is true for
n=1
.

Let
k>1
and the equality (
1.6
)-(
1.8
) holds true for
n=k
. Then we obtain

\begin{split}F(x,v)=&\mathbf{1}_{t^{1}<t_{*}}F(X(t_{*};t,x,v),V(t_{*};t,x,v))\\
&+\mu_{\Theta}(x^{1},v_{\mathbf{b}})\int_{\prod_{j=1}^{i}\mathcal{V}_{j}}\sum\limits^{k-1}_{i=1}\Big\{\mathbf{1}_{t^{i+1}<t_{*}\leq t^{i}}F(X(t_{*};t^{i},x^{i},v^{i}),V(t_{*};t^{i},x^{i},v^{i}))\Big\}\mathrm{d}\Sigma_{i}\\
&+\mu_{\Theta}(x^{1},v_{\mathbf{b}})\int_{\prod_{j=1}^{k}\mathcal{V}_{j}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{k}\geq t_{*}}F(x^{k},v^{k})\mathrm{d}\Sigma_{k}.\end{split}

(1.9)

Now considering
t^{k+1}
, we have two cases
t^{k+1}<t_{*}\leq t^{k}
and
t_{*}\leq t^{k+1}\leq t^{k}
.
For
t^{k+1}<t_{*}\leq t^{k}
, we have

F(x^{k},v^{k})=F(X(t_{*};t^{k},x^{k},v^{k}),V(t_{*};t^{k},x^{k},v^{k})).

For
t_{*}\leq t^{k+1}
, we get

F(x^{k},v^{k})=F(x^{k+1},v_{\mathbf{b}}^{k})=\mu_{\Theta}(x^{k+1},v_{\mathbf{b}}^{k})\int_{\mathcal{V}_{k+1}}F(x^{k+1},v^{k+1})\{n(x^{k+1})\cdot v^{k+1}\}\mathrm{d}v^{k+1}.

Replacing
F(x^{k},v^{k})
in (
1.9
) with the above, we derive that

\begin{split}F(x,v)=&\mathbf{1}_{t^{1}<t_{*}}F(X(t_{*};t,x,v),V(t_{*};t,x,v))\\
&+\mu_{\Theta}(x^{1},v_{\mathbf{b}})\int_{\prod_{j=1}^{i}\mathcal{V}_{j}}\sum\limits^{k-1}_{i=1}\Big\{\mathbf{1}_{t^{i+1}<t_{*}\leq t^{i}}F(X(t_{*};t^{i},x^{i},v^{i}),V(t_{*};t^{i},x^{i},v^{i}))\Big\}\mathrm{d}\Sigma_{i}\\
&+\mu_{\Theta}(x^{1},v_{\mathbf{b}})\int_{\prod_{j=1}^{k}\mathcal{V}_{j}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{k+1}<t_{*}\leq t^{k}}F(X(t_{*};t^{k},x^{k},v^{k}),V(t_{*};t^{k},x^{k},v^{k}))\mathrm{d}\Sigma_{k}\\
&+\mu_{\Theta}(x^{1},v_{\mathbf{b}})\int_{\prod_{j=1}^{k+1}\mathcal{V}_{j}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{k+1}\geq t_{*}}F(x^{k+1},v^{k+1})\mathrm{d}\Sigma_{k+1}.\end{split}

Thus, the equality (
1.6
)-(
1.8
) holds for
n=k+1
.
By induction, we prove the Lemma.
∎

Remark 1
.

From the Lemma
3
(resp. Lemma
5
)
and
\mu_{\Theta}(x,v)=\mu_{\Theta}(x,|v|)
, we obtain

\mu_{\Theta}(x^{j+1},v_{\mathbf{b}}^{j})=\mu_{\Theta}(x^{j+1},v^{j})
with
v_{\mathbf{b}}^{j}=v_{\mathbf{b}}(x^{j},v^{j})
.
Therefore, in the rest of paper, we write
\mathrm{d}\sigma_{j}
as

\mathrm{d}\sigma_{j}=\mu_{\Theta}(x^{j+1},v^{j})\{n(x^{j})\cdot v^{j}\}\mathrm{d}v^{j},

where
v^{j}\in\mathcal{V}_{j}:=\{v^{j}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}:n(x^{j})\cdot v^{j}>0\}
and
(X,V)
in (
1.1
) (resp. (
1.2
)).

Remark 2
.

Though
F
follows the characteristic trajectory,
F
is time independent. Moreover, from Remark
1
and
0<a\leq\Theta(x)\leq b
, we derive for all
i=1,\cdots,k
and
(X,V)
in (
1.1
) (resp. (
1.2
)),

\int_{\mathcal{V}_{i}}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{i}=\int_{\mathcal{V}_{i}}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{j+1},v^{j})\{n(x^{j})\cdot v^{j}\}\mathrm{d}v^{j}\leq\int_{\mathcal{V}_{i}}\frac{1}{2\pi a^{2}}e^{-\frac{|v|^{2}}{2b}}\{n(x^{j})\cdot v^{j}\}\mathrm{d}v^{j}\leq\frac{b^{2}}{a^{2}}.

W.l.o.g. we assume
b^{2}=2a^{2}
(i.e.
\int_{\mathcal{V}_{i}}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{i}\leq 2
) in the rest of paper.

1.2.
Gravitational case

Lemma 3
.

Consider
(X,V)
in (
1.1
), for
v\in\mathcal{V}:=\{v\in\mathbb{R}^{3}:n(x)\cdot v>0\}
,
x\in\partial\Omega
and
v_{\mathbf{b}}=v_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)
, we have

|v_{\mathbf{b}}|=|v|.

(1.10)

Proof.

Following
(X,V)
in (
1.1
) with
v\in\mathcal{V}
, we compute the following derivative:

\begin{split}&\ \ \ \ \frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}s}\Big(\frac{|V(s;t,x,v)|^{2}}{2}+\Phi(X(s;t,x,v))\Big)\\
&=V(s;t,x,v)\cdot\frac{\mathrm{d}V}{\mathrm{d}s}+\nabla\Phi\cdot\frac{\mathrm{d}X}{\mathrm{d}s}\\
&=-V(s;t,x,v)\cdot\nabla\Phi(X(s;t,x,v))+\nabla\Phi\cdot V(s;t,x,v)=0.\end{split}

Recall that

(X(t;t,x,v),V(t;t,x,v))=(x,v)
,
(X(t-t_{\mathbf{b}};t,x,v),V(t-t_{\mathbf{b}};t,x,v))=(x_{\mathbf{b}},v_{\mathbf{b}})
. By taking
s=t-t_{\mathbf{b}}
and
s=t
, we obtain

|v|^{2}/2+\Phi(x)=|v_{\mathbf{b}}|^{2}/2+\Phi(x_{\mathbf{b}}).

Since
\Phi(x)|_{x_{3}=0}\equiv 0
and
x,x_{\mathbf{b}}\in\partial\Omega
, we have

\Phi(x)=\Phi(x_{\mathbf{b}})=0,

which implies
|v_{\mathbf{b}}|=|v|
.
∎

Lemma 4
.

Consider
(X,V)
in (
1.1
), for
v\in\mathcal{V}:=\{v\in\mathbb{R}^{3}:n(x)\cdot v>0\}
and
x\in\partial\Omega
,
there exists a unique time
t_{m}\in[t-t_{\mathbf{b}},t]
such that

V_{3}(t_{m},t,x,v)=0,\ X_{3}(t_{m},t,x,v)=\max\limits_{t-t_{\mathbf{b}}\leq s\leq t}X_{3}(s,t,x,v).

Moreover, we have

\frac{t_{\mathbf{b}}}{3}\leq t-t_{m},\ t_{m}-(t-t_{\mathbf{b}})\leq\frac{2t_{\mathbf{b}}}{3}.

Proof.

Recall
\|\nabla_{x}\phi\|_{\infty}\leq\varrho_{3}
, we have

g-\varrho_{3}\leq\big(\nabla_{x}\Phi\big)_{3}\leq g+\varrho_{3}.

(1.11)

From (
1.1
), (
1.11
) and
0\leq\varrho_{3}\ll 1
, we get

-g-\varrho_{3}\leq\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}s}V_{3}(s;t,x,v)=-\big(\nabla_{X}\Phi(X(s;t,x,v))\big)_{3}\leq-g+\varrho_{3}<0.

(1.12)

We define

t_{m}(t,x,v)=\{t-t_{\mathbf{b}}\leq s\leq t:X_{3}(t_{m},t,x,v)=\max\limits_{t-t_{\mathbf{b}}\leq s\leq t}X_{3}(s,t,x,v),V_{3}(t_{m},t,x,v)=0\}.

(1.13)

The negativity of
\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}s}V_{3}(s;t,x,v)
implies that
t_{m}
is unique for any
(t,x,v)
. Moreover, for
s\in[t-t_{\mathbf{b}},t_{m}]
,
V_{3}\geq 0
implies
X_{3}
is increasing and for
s\in[t_{m},t]
,
V_{3}\leq 0
implies
X_{3}
is decreasing.

From (
1.12
) and the uniqueness of
t_{m}
, we deduce

\begin{split}\frac{1}{2}(g-\varrho_{3})\big(t_{m}-(t-t_{\mathbf{b}})\big)^{2}\leq&X_{3}(t_{m},t,x,v)\leq\frac{1}{2}(g+\varrho_{3})\big(t_{m}-(t-t_{\mathbf{b}})\big)^{2},\\
\frac{1}{2}(g-\varrho_{3})(t-t_{m})^{2}\leq&X_{3}(t_{m},t,x,v)\leq\frac{1}{2}(g+\varrho_{3})(t-t_{m})^{2}.\end{split}

Thus we obtain that

\sqrt{\frac{2X_{3}(t_{m},t,x,v)}{g+\varrho_{3}}}\leq t_{m}-(t-t_{\mathbf{b}}),\ t-t_{m}\leq\sqrt{\frac{2X_{3}(t_{m},t,x,v)}{g-\varrho_{3}}}.

(1.14)

From (
1.14
), we have

\sqrt{\frac{g-\varrho_{3}}{g+\varrho_{3}}}\leq\frac{t_{m}-(t-t_{\mathbf{b}})}{t-t_{m}}\leq\sqrt{\frac{g+\varrho_{3}}{g-\varrho_{3}}}.

(1.15)

Since
\big(t_{m}-(t-t_{\mathbf{b}})\big)+\big(t-t_{m}\big)=t_{\mathbf{b}}
and
\varrho_{3}\ll 1
, we have

\begin{split}&\frac{t_{\mathbf{b}}}{3}\leq\Big(1+\sqrt{\frac{g+\varrho_{3}}{g-\varrho_{3}}}\Big)^{-1}t_{\mathbf{b}}\leq t-t_{m}\leq\Big(1+\sqrt{\frac{g-\varrho_{3}}{g+\varrho_{3}}}\Big)^{-1}t_{\mathbf{b}}\leq\frac{2t_{\mathbf{b}}}{3},\\
&\frac{t_{\mathbf{b}}}{3}\leq\Big(1+\sqrt{\frac{g+\varrho_{3}}{g-\varrho_{3}}}\Big)^{-1}t_{\mathbf{b}}\leq t_{m}-(t-t_{\mathbf{b}})\leq\Big(1+\sqrt{\frac{g-\varrho_{3}}{g+\varrho_{3}}}\Big)^{-1}t_{\mathbf{b}}\leq\frac{2t_{\mathbf{b}}}{3}.\end{split}

(1.16)

∎

Remark 3
.

From (
1.11
) and (
1.16
), we derive that

\frac{1}{6}gt_{\mathbf{b}}\leq\frac{t_{\mathbf{b}}}{3}(g-\varrho_{3})\leq v_{3},\ v_{\mathbf{b},3}\leq\frac{2t_{\mathbf{b}}}{3}(g+\varrho_{3})\leq\frac{4}{3}gt_{\mathbf{b}}.

For the sake of simplicity, we write:
suppose
(X,V)
in (
1.1
), for all
(x,v)\in\gamma_{+}
,

\begin{split}&t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)\lesssim v_{3}\lesssim t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v),\ \ t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)\lesssim v_{\mathbf{b},3}(x,v)\lesssim t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v).\end{split}

(1.17)

Now we consider the change of variables

v\in\{v\in\mathbb{R}^{3}:n(x)\cdot v>0\}\mapsto(x_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v),t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v))\in\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}_{+}.

Since the domain is periodic, this is a local bijective mapping. For fixed
x,t_{\mathbf{b}}
and
x_{\mathbf{b}}
, we introduce the set of velocities
\{v^{m,n}\}
with
m,n\in\mathbb{Z}
such that

v^{m,n}\in\{v^{m,n}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}:n(x)\cdot v^{m,n}>0\}\mapsto(x_{\mathbf{b}}+(m,n,0),t_{\mathbf{b}})=(x_{\mathbf{b}},t_{\mathbf{b}})\in\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}_{+}.

(1.18)

Proposition 3
.

Consider
(X,V)
in (
1.1
),

•

For fixed
x\in\partial\Omega
, and
m,n\in\mathbb{Z}
, we introduce the following map:

v\in\{v\in\mathbb{R}^{3}:n(x)\cdot v>0\}\mapsto(x_{\mathbf{b}},t_{\mathbf{b}}):=(x_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)+(m,n,0),t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v))\in\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}_{+}.

(1.19)

Then the map (
1.19
) is locally bijective and has the change of variable formula as

\mathrm{d}v\lesssim|t_{\mathbf{b}}|^{-3}|n(x_{\mathbf{b}})\cdot v_{\mathbf{b}}|\mathrm{d}t_{\mathbf{b}}\mathrm{d}S_{x_{\mathbf{b}}}.

(1.20)

Therefore, for any non-negative function
\phi(v)
, there exists a constant
C
such that

\int_{v\in v^{m,n}}\phi(v)\mathrm{d}v\leq C\iint_{\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}\phi(x_{\mathbf{b}},t_{\mathbf{b}})|t_{\mathbf{b}}|^{-3}|n(x_{\mathbf{b}})\cdot v_{\mathbf{b}}|\mathrm{d}t_{\mathbf{b}}\mathrm{d}S_{x_{\mathbf{b}}}.

•

Similarly we have a locally bijective map:

v\in\{v\in\mathbb{R}^{3}:n(x)\cdot v<0\}\mapsto(x_{\mathbf{f}},t_{\mathbf{f}}):=(x_{\mathbf{f}}(x,v)+(m,n,0),t_{\mathbf{f}}(x,v))\in\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}_{+},

with

\mathrm{d}v\lesssim|t_{\mathbf{f}}|^{-3}|n(x_{\mathbf{f}})\cdot v_{\mathbf{f}}|\mathrm{d}t_{\mathbf{f}}\mathrm{d}S_{x_{\mathbf{f}}}.

(1.21)

Proof.

We just need to show (
1.20
), since (
1.21
) can be deduced after changing the backward variables into forward variables. For the sake of simplicity, we have abused the notations temporarily:

\begin{split}&x_{\mathbf{b}}:=x^{1}=(x^{1}_{1},x^{1}_{2},x^{1}_{3})=(x^{1}_{\parallel},x^{1}_{3}),\ \ v=(v_{1},v_{2},v_{3})=(v_{\parallel},v_{3}),\\
&v_{\mathbf{b}}=(v_{\mathbf{b},1},v_{\mathbf{b},2},v_{\mathbf{b},3}),\ \ t_{\mathbf{b}}=t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v).\end{split}

Recall that
\Phi(x)=gx_{3}+\phi(x)
, we get
\nabla\nabla\Phi=\nabla\nabla(gx_{3}+\phi(x))=\nabla((0,0,g)+\nabla\phi(x))=\nabla\nabla\phi(x).

Now we compute the determinant of the Jacobian matrix.
Fixing
x,t
and following the characteristics trajectory, we deduce

x^{1}+\int^{t}_{t-t_{\mathbf{b}}}V(s;t,x,v)\mathrm{d}s+(m,n,0)=x,

(1.22)

v_{\mathbf{b}}+\int^{t}_{t-t_{\mathbf{b}}}-\nabla\Phi(X(s;t,x,v))\mathrm{d}s=v.

(1.23)

Inputting (
1.23
) into (
1.22
),

x^{1}+\int^{t}_{t-t_{\mathbf{b}}}(v-\int^{t}_{s}-\nabla\Phi(X(\alpha,t,x,v))\mathrm{d}\alpha)\mathrm{d}s+(m,n,0)=x.

Taking
\partial_{v}
on
\nabla\Phi
, we have

\nabla_{v}\big[\nabla\Phi(X(\alpha,t,x,v)\big]=\nabla_{v}X(\alpha,t,x,v)\cdot\nabla\nabla\Phi(X)=\nabla_{v}X(\alpha,t,x,v)\cdot\nabla\nabla\phi.

Then, we get

\begin{split}&\frac{\partial t_{\mathbf{b}}}{\partial v}=\frac{1}{v_{\mathbf{b},3}}\Big(-(0,0,1)t_{\mathbf{b}}-\int^{t-t_{\mathbf{b}}}_{t}\int^{s}_{t}-\nabla_{v}X\cdot\nabla\nabla\phi_{3}(X(\alpha,t,x,v))\mathrm{d}\alpha\mathrm{d}s\Big),\\
&\frac{\partial x^{1}_{\parallel}}{\partial v}=-\begin{pmatrix}1&0&0\\
0&1&0\end{pmatrix}t_{\mathbf{b}}-\int^{t-t_{\mathbf{b}}}_{t}\int^{s}_{t}-\nabla_{v}X\cdot\nabla\nabla\phi_{\parallel}(X(\alpha,t,x,v))\mathrm{d}\alpha\mathrm{d}s-\frac{\partial t_{\mathbf{b}}}{\partial v}v_{\parallel},\end{split}

where
\phi=(\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\phi_{3})=(\phi_{\parallel},\phi_{3})
.
Therefore, we get

\det\Big(\frac{\partial x^{1}_{\parallel}}{\partial v},\frac{\partial t_{\mathbf{b}}}{\partial v}\Big)=\frac{(t_{\mathbf{b}})^{3}}{v_{\mathbf{b},3}}\times\det\Big[-{Id}_{3\times 3}-\frac{1}{t_{\mathbf{b}}}\int^{t-t_{\mathbf{b}}}_{t}\int^{s}_{t}-\nabla_{v}X\cdot\nabla\nabla\phi\mathrm{d}\alpha\mathrm{d}s\Big].

We set

d_{0}=\Big(\det\big[-{Id}_{3\times 3}-\frac{1}{t_{\mathbf{b}}}\int^{t-t_{\mathbf{b}}}_{t}\int^{s}_{t}-\nabla_{v}X\cdot\nabla\nabla\phi\mathrm{d}\alpha\mathrm{d}s\big]\Big)^{-1},

and recall that

\begin{split}\sup_{x_{3}\geq 0}e^{\varrho_{1}x_{3}}\|\nabla\nabla_{x}\phi\|_{\infty}\leq\varrho_{2}\ll 1\ \text{with}\ \varrho_{1}>1,\ \ \ \text{and}\ \ \|\nabla_{x}\phi\|_{\infty}\leq\varrho_{3}\ll 1.\end{split}

From (
1.1
), we have

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}s}|\nabla_{v}X(s;t,x,v)|\lesssim|\nabla_{v}V(s;t,x,v)|,

\begin{split}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}s}|\nabla_{v}V(s;t,x,v)|&\lesssim|\nabla\nabla_{X}\phi(X(s;t,x,v))||\nabla_{v}X(s;t,x,v)|\lesssim\varrho_{2}e^{-\varrho_{1}X_{3}(s;t,x,v)}|\nabla_{v}X(s;t,x,v)|.\end{split}

Since
\nabla_{v}X(t;t,x,v)=\nabla_{v}x=0
,
\nabla_{v}V(t;t,x,v)=\nabla_{v}v=Id_{3\times 3}
and
t-t_{\mathbf{b}}\leq s\leq t
, we get

\begin{split}|\nabla_{v}X(s;t,x,v)|&\lesssim\int^{t}_{s}|\nabla_{v}V(s_{1};t,x,v)|\mathrm{d}s_{1}\\
&\lesssim|t-s|+\int^{t}_{s}\int^{t}_{s_{1}}\varrho_{2}e^{-\varrho_{1}X_{3}(s_{2};t,x,v)}|\nabla_{v}X(s_{2};t,x,v)|\mathrm{d}s_{2}\mathrm{d}s_{1}.\end{split}

(1.24)

Using the Fubini’s theorem, we derive

\begin{split}\eqref{Xv first}&\lesssim|t-s|+\int^{t}_{s}\int^{s_{2}}_{s}\varrho_{2}e^{-\varrho_{1}X_{3}(s_{2};t,x,v)}|\nabla_{v}X(s_{2};t,x,v)|\mathrm{d}s_{1}\mathrm{d}s_{2}\\
&\lesssim|t-s|+\int^{t}_{s}\varrho_{2}(s_{2}-s)e^{-\varrho_{1}X_{3}(s_{2};t,x,v)}|\nabla_{v}X(s_{2};t,x,v)|\mathrm{d}s_{2}.\end{split}

Using the Gronwall’s inequality, for
t-t_{\mathbf{b}}\leq s\leq t
,

\begin{split}|\nabla_{v}X(s;t,x,v)|&\lesssim|t-s|\exp\big(\int^{t}_{s}\varrho_{2}(s_{2}-s)e^{-\varrho_{1}X_{3}(s_{2};t,x,v)}\mathrm{d}s_{2}\big)\\
&\leq|t-s|\exp\Big(\int^{t}_{t-t_{\mathbf{b}}}\varrho_{2}\big(s_{2}-(t-t_{\mathbf{b}})\big)e^{-\varrho_{1}X_{3}(s_{2};t,x,v)}\mathrm{d}s_{2}\Big).\end{split}

(1.25)

Now we consider
\int^{t}_{t-t_{\mathbf{b}}}\big(s_{2}-(t-t_{\mathbf{b}})\big)e^{-\varrho_{1}X_{3}}\mathrm{d}s_{2}
.
From (
1.11
),
t_{m}
in (
1.13
) and (
1.16
), in the case
s\in[t_{m},t]
where
X_{3}(s,t,x,v)
is decreasing,
we obtain

t-t_{m}\gtrsim\frac{t_{\mathbf{b}}}{2},\ v_{3}\geq(g-\varrho_{3})(t-t_{m})\gtrsim g(t-t_{m})\gtrsim 5t_{\mathbf{b}}.

Thus we have

X_{3}(s,t,x,v)\gtrsim\frac{1}{2}v_{3}(t-s)\gtrsim\frac{5}{2}t_{\mathbf{b}}(t-s).

(1.26)

Using (
1.26
), we derive

\displaystyle\int^{t}_{t_{m}}(s_{2}-(t-t_{\mathbf{b}}))e^{-\varrho_{1}X_{3}}\mathrm{d}s_{2}\leq\int^{t}_{t-t_{\mathbf{b}}/2}\big(s_{2}-(t-t_{\mathbf{b}})\big)e^{-\varrho_{1}\frac{5}{2}t_{\mathbf{b}}(t-s_{2})}\mathrm{d}s_{2}.

(1.27)

Setting
a=t-s_{2}
, we get

\begin{split}\eqref{second estimate on X_v}&=\int^{t_{\mathbf{b}}/2}_{0}(t_{\mathbf{b}}-a)e^{-\varrho_{1}\frac{5}{2}t_{\mathbf{b}}a}\mathrm{d}a\\
&=-\frac{2}{5\varrho_{1}}e^{-\varrho_{1}\frac{5}{2}t_{\mathbf{b}}a}\Bigg|^{t_{\mathbf{b}}/2}_{0}-\bigg(\frac{a}{-\varrho_{1}\frac{5}{2}t_{\mathbf{b}}}e^{-\varrho_{1}\frac{5}{2}t_{\mathbf{b}}a}-\frac{e^{-\varrho_{1}\frac{5}{2}t_{\mathbf{b}}a}}{(-\varrho_{1}\frac{5}{2}t_{\mathbf{b}})^{2}}\bigg)\Bigg|^{t_{\mathbf{b}}/2}_{0}\lesssim\frac{2}{5\varrho_{1}}.\end{split}

In the case
s\in[t-t_{\mathbf{b}},t_{m}]
where
X_{3}(s,t,x,v)
is increasing.
From (
1.16
),

t_{m}-(t-t_{\mathbf{b}})\gtrsim\frac{t_{\mathbf{b}}}{2},\ v_{\mathbf{b},3}\geq(g-\varrho_{3})\big(t_{m}-(t-t_{\mathbf{b}})\big)\gtrsim g(t-t_{m})\gtrsim 5t_{\mathbf{b}}.

From (
1.11
), for
x\in\partial\Omega
,
s\in[t-t_{\mathbf{b}},t_{m}]
, we have

X_{3}(s,t,x,v)\gtrsim\frac{1}{2}v_{\mathbf{b},3}\big(s-(t-t_{\mathbf{b}})\big)\gtrsim\frac{5}{2}t_{\mathbf{b}}\big(s-(t-t_{\mathbf{b}})\big).

(1.28)

Using (
1.28
), we derive

\displaystyle\int^{t_{m}}_{t-t_{\mathbf{b}}}(s_{2}-(t-t_{\mathbf{b}}))e^{-\varrho_{1}X_{3}}\mathrm{d}s_{2}\leq\int^{t-t_{\mathbf{b}}/2}_{t-t_{\mathbf{b}}}\big(s_{2}-(t-t_{\mathbf{b}})\big)e^{-\varrho_{1}\frac{5}{2}t_{\mathbf{b}}\big(s_{2}-(t-t_{\mathbf{b}})\big)}\mathrm{d}s_{2}.

(1.29)

Setting
a=s_{2}-(t-t_{\mathbf{b}})
, we get

\begin{split}\eqref{second estimate on X_v second case}=\int^{t_{\mathbf{b}}/2}_{0}ae^{-\varrho_{1}\frac{5}{2}t_{\mathbf{b}}a}\mathrm{d}a=\bigg(\frac{a}{-\varrho_{1}\frac{5}{2}t_{\mathbf{b}}}e^{-\varrho_{1}\frac{5}{2}t_{\mathbf{b}}a}-\frac{e^{-\varrho_{1}\frac{5}{2}t_{\mathbf{b}}a}}{(-\varrho_{1}\frac{5}{2}t_{\mathbf{b}})^{2}}\bigg)\Bigg|^{t_{\mathbf{b}}/2}_{0}\lesssim\frac{2}{5\varrho_{1}}.\end{split}

Combining (
1.27
) and (
1.29
), we conclude

\eqref{first estimate on X_v}\leq|t-s|\exp(\varrho_{2}/\varrho_{1}).

(1.30)

From (
1.30
) and Fubini’s theorem, we have

\begin{split}&\ \ \ \ \Big|\int^{t-t_{\mathbf{b}}}_{t}\int^{s}_{t}-\nabla_{v}X\cdot\nabla^{2}\phi(X(\alpha;t,x,v))\mathrm{d}\alpha\mathrm{d}s\Big|\\
&\leq\int^{t}_{t-t_{\mathbf{b}}}\int^{t}_{s}|\nabla^{2}\phi(X)||\nabla_{v}X(\alpha;t,x,v)|\mathrm{d}\alpha\mathrm{d}s\leq\int^{t}_{t-t_{\mathbf{b}}}\int^{t}_{s}\varrho_{2}e^{-\varrho X_{3}}|t-\alpha|e^{\varrho_{2}/\varrho_{1}}\mathrm{d}\alpha\mathrm{d}s\\
&\leq\int^{t}_{t-t_{\mathbf{b}}}\varrho_{2}\big(\alpha-(t-t_{\mathbf{b}})\big)e^{-\varrho_{1}X_{3}}|t-\alpha|e^{\varrho_{2}/\varrho_{1}}\mathrm{d}\alpha\\
&\leq\varrho_{2}e^{\varrho_{2}/\varrho_{1}}t_{\mathbf{b}}\int^{t}_{t-t_{\mathbf{b}}}\big(\alpha-(t-t_{\mathbf{b}})\big)e^{-\varrho_{1}X_{3}(\alpha;t,x,v)}\mathrm{d}\alpha\leq e^{\varrho_{2}/\varrho_{1}}t_{\mathbf{b}}\frac{\varrho_{2}}{\varrho_{1}},\end{split}

where the last inequality holds from (
1.30
).
Since
\varrho_{2}\ll 1
, and

|\frac{1}{t_{\mathbf{b}}}\int^{t-t_{\mathbf{b}}}_{t}\int^{s}_{t}-\nabla_{v}\phi d\alpha ds|\leq e^{\varrho_{2}/\varrho_{1}}\frac{\varrho_{2}}{\varrho_{1}},

thus we derive the uniform-in-time bound on
d_{0}
.
From

\det(\frac{\partial x^{1}_{\parallel}}{\partial v},\frac{\partial t_{\mathbf{b}}}{\partial v})=\frac{(t_{\mathbf{b}})^{3}}{v_{\mathbf{b},3}}\times d_{0}
, we prove (
1.20
).
∎

Remark 4
.

We can apply Proposition
3
on

v^{j}\in\mathcal{V}_{j}\mapsto(x^{j+1},t_{\mathbf{b}}^{j}):=(x_{\mathbf{b}}(x^{j},v^{j}),t_{\mathbf{b}}(x^{j},v^{j}))
, and this is also a local bijective mapping. For fixed
t_{\mathbf{b}}^{j}
and
x^{j+1}
, we introduce the set of velocities
\{v^{m,n}_{j}\}
with
m,n\in\mathbb{Z}
such that

v^{m,n}_{j}\in\mathcal{V}_{j}\mapsto(x^{j+1}+(m,n,0),t_{\mathbf{b}}^{j})=(x^{j+1},t_{\mathbf{b}}^{j})\in\partial\Omega\times[0,t_{j}],

(1.31)

with the change of variable formula as

\mathrm{d}v^{m,n}_{j}\lesssim|t_{\mathbf{b}}^{j}|^{-3}{|n(x^{j+1})\cdot v^{m,n}_{j,\mathbf{b}}|}\mathrm{d}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{j}\mathrm{d}S_{x^{j+1}}.

(1.32)

1.3.
With Magnet field

For the technique reason, w.l.o.g. we set

g=10
for the magnet field case in the rest of the paper.

Lemma 5
.

Consider
(X,V)
in (
1.2
), for
v\in\mathcal{V}:=\{v\in\mathbb{R}^{3}:n(x)\cdot v>0\}
,
x\in\partial\Omega
and
v_{\mathbf{b}}=v_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)
, we have

t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)=\frac{|v_{3}|}{5},\ \ |v_{\mathbf{b}}|=|v|.

(1.33)

Proof.

From
(X,V)
in (
1.2
), we have

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}s}V(s;t,x,v)=(B_{3}V_{2}(s;t,x,v),-B_{3}V_{1}(s;t,x,v),-g).

(1.34)

Then we obtain the following derivative,

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}s}\Big(\frac{|V_{3}(s;t,x,v)|^{2}}{2}+\Phi(X(s;t,x,v))\Big)=V_{3}\cdot\frac{\mathrm{d}V_{3}}{\mathrm{d}s}+\nabla\Phi(X)\cdot\frac{\mathrm{d}X}{\mathrm{d}s}=-gV_{3}+gV_{3}=0.

(1.35)

Since

(X(t;t,x,v),V(t;t,x,v))=(x,v)
,
(X(t-t_{\mathbf{b}};t,x,v),V(t-t_{\mathbf{b}};t,x,v))=(x_{\mathbf{b}},v_{\mathbf{b}})
, we have

|v_{3}|^{2}/2+\Phi(x)=|v_{\mathbf{b},3}|^{2}/2+\Phi(x_{\mathbf{b}}).

Since
\Phi(x)|_{x_{3}=0}=0
, we have
\Phi(x)=\Phi(x_{\mathbf{b}})
which implies
|v_{\mathbf{b},3}|=|v_{3}|
.
Moreover, using
\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}s}V_{3}(s;t,x,v)=-g
in (
1.34
), we derive that

t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)=2v_{3}/g=v_{3}/5.

On the other side, from (
1.34
), we also have

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}s}V_{1}(s;t,x,v)=B_{3}V_{2}(s;t,x,v),\ \ \frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}s}V_{2}(s;t,x,v)=-B_{3}V_{1}(s;t,x,v).

(1.36)

Thus we obtain that,

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}s}\Big(|V_{1}(s;t,x,v)|^{2}+|V_{2}(s;t,x,v)|^{2})\Big)=2V_{1}B_{3}V_{2}-2V_{2}B_{3}V_{1}=0.

(1.37)

Therefore we prove
|v_{\mathbf{b}}|=|v|
.
∎

Similarly, we consider the change of variables

v\in\{v\in\mathbb{R}^{3}:n(x)\cdot v>0\}\mapsto(x_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v),t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v))\in\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}_{+}.

Since the domain is periodic, this is a local bijective mapping. For fixed
x,t_{\mathbf{b}}
and
x_{\mathbf{b}}
, we introduce the set of velocities
\{v^{m,n}\}
with
m,n\in\mathbb{Z}
such that

v^{m,n}\in\{v^{m,n}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}:n(x)\cdot v^{m,n}>0\}\mapsto(x_{\mathbf{b}}+(m,n,0),t_{\mathbf{b}})=(x_{\mathbf{b}},t_{\mathbf{b}})\in\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}_{+}.

(1.38)

Proposition 4
.

Consider
(X,V)
in (
1.2
),

•

For fixed
x\in\partial\Omega
, and
m,n\in\mathbb{Z}
, we introduce the following map:

v\in\{v\in\mathbb{R}^{3}:n(x)\cdot v>0\}\mapsto(x_{\mathbf{b}},t_{\mathbf{b}}):=(x_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)+(m,n,0),t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v))\in\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}_{+}.

(1.39)

Then the map (
1.39
) is locally bijective and has the change of variable formula as

\mathrm{d}v=5B^{2}_{3}(2-2\cos(B_{3}t_{\mathbf{b}}))^{-1}\mathrm{d}t_{\mathbf{b}}\mathrm{d}S_{x_{\mathbf{b}}}.

(1.40)

Therefore, for any non-negative function
\phi(v)
, there exists a constant
C
such that

\int_{v\in v^{m,n}}\phi(v)\mathrm{d}v=\iint_{\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}\phi(x_{\mathbf{b}},t_{\mathbf{b}})5B^{2}_{3}(2-2\cos(B_{3}t_{\mathbf{b}}))^{-1}\mathrm{d}t_{\mathbf{b}}\mathrm{d}S_{x_{\mathbf{b}}}.

•

Similarly we have a locally bijective map

v\in\{v\in\mathbb{R}^{3}:n(x)\cdot v<0\}\mapsto(x_{\mathbf{f}},t_{\mathbf{f}}):=(x_{\mathbf{f}}(x,v),t_{\mathbf{f}}(x,v))\in\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}_{+},

with

\mathrm{d}v=5B^{2}_{3}(2-2\cos(B_{3}t_{\mathbf{f}}))^{-1}\mathrm{d}t_{\mathbf{f}}\mathrm{d}S_{x_{\mathbf{f}}}.

(1.41)

Proof.

We just need to show (
1.40
) since (
1.41
) can be deduced after changing the backward variables into forward variables.
Here, we write

x=(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3}),v=(v_{1},v_{2},v_{3})
.

From (
1.36
), for
t-t_{\mathbf{b}}\leq s\leq t
, we assume for
0\leq a
and
0\leq\theta_{0}<2\pi
,

V_{1}(s;t,x,v)=a\sin(B_{3}s+\theta_{0}),\ \ V_{2}(s;t,x,v)=a\cos(B_{3}s+\theta_{0}),

(1.42)

where we set

v_{1}=V_{1}(t;t,x,v)=a\sin(B_{3}t+\theta_{0}),\ \ v_{2}=V_{2}(t;t,x,v)=a\cos(B_{3}t+\theta_{0}).

(1.43)

Given fixed
t
and
x
, we can compute
x_{\mathbf{b}}
,

x_{\mathbf{b}}+\int^{t}_{t-t_{\mathbf{b}}}V(s;t,x,v)\mathrm{d}s=x.

From (
1.42
) and (
1.43
), we get

\begin{split}x_{\mathbf{b},1}&=x_{1}-\int^{t}_{t-t_{\mathbf{b}}}a\sin(B_{3}s+\theta_{0})\mathrm{d}s=x_{1}+\frac{1}{B_{3}}a\cos(B_{3}s+\theta_{0})\Big|^{t}_{t-t_{\mathbf{b}}}\\
&=x_{1}+\big(\frac{1}{B_{3}}a\cos(B_{3}t+\theta_{0})-\frac{1}{B_{3}}a\cos(B_{3}(t-t_{\mathbf{b}})+\theta_{0})\big)\\
&=x_{1}+\frac{1}{B_{3}}v_{2}-\frac{1}{B_{3}}v_{2}\cos(B_{3}t_{\mathbf{b}})-\frac{1}{B_{3}}v_{1}\sin(B_{3}t_{\mathbf{b}}),\end{split}

(1.44)

\begin{split}x_{\mathbf{b},2}&=x_{2}-\int^{t}_{t-t_{\mathbf{b}}}a\cos(B_{3}s+\theta_{0})\mathrm{d}s=x_{2}-\frac{1}{B_{3}}a\sin(B_{3}s+\theta_{0})\Big|^{t}_{t-t_{\mathbf{b}}}\\
&=x_{2}-\big(\frac{1}{B_{3}}a\sin(B_{3}t+\theta_{0})-\frac{1}{B_{3}}a\sin(B_{3}(t-t_{\mathbf{b}})+\theta_{0})\big)\\
&=x_{2}-\frac{1}{B_{3}}v_{1}+\frac{1}{B_{3}}v_{1}\cos(B_{3}t_{\mathbf{b}})-\frac{1}{B_{3}}v_{2}\sin(B_{3}t_{\mathbf{b}}).\end{split}

(1.45)

Thus we can compute out the matrix,

\Big|\det\big[\frac{\partial(x_{\mathbf{b}},t_{\mathbf{b}})}{\partial v}\big]\Big|=\frac{2-2\cos(B_{3}t_{\mathbf{b}})}{5B^{2}_{3}}.

Therefore, we prove (
1.40
).
∎

Remark 5
.

We can apply Proposition
4
on

v^{j}\in\mathcal{V}_{j}\mapsto(x^{j+1},t_{\mathbf{b}}^{j}):=(x_{\mathbf{b}}(x^{j},v^{j}),t_{\mathbf{b}}(x^{j},v^{j}))
, and this is also a local bijective mapping. For fixed
t_{\mathbf{b}}^{j}
and
x^{j+1}
, we introduce the set of velocities
\{v^{m,n}_{j}\}
with
m,n\in\mathbb{Z}
such that

v^{m,n}_{j}\in\mathcal{V}_{j}\mapsto(x^{j+1}+(m,n,0),t_{\mathbf{b}}^{j})=(x^{j+1},t_{\mathbf{b}}^{j})\in\partial\Omega\times[0,t_{j}],

with the change of variable formula as

\mathrm{d}v^{m,n}_{j}=5B^{2}_{3}(2-2\cos(B_{3}t^{j}_{\mathbf{b}}))^{-1}\mathrm{d}t^{j}_{\mathbf{b}}\mathrm{d}S_{x^{j+1}}.

(1.46)

2.
Pointwise control of the Outgoing Flux
\mathfrak{J}(x)

The main purpose of this section is to prove the following pointwise bound of the stationary solution.

Proposition 5
.

Suppose
F(x,v)
solves (
0.7
) with (
0.5
) and (
0.2
) (resp. (
0.7
) with (
0.6
) and (
0.2
)). Recall
b>0
defined in (
0.4
). Then there exists constant
k>0
(see (
2.46
) and (
2.48
) for the precise choice) such that

\|e^{\frac{1}{2b}(|v|^{2}/2+\Phi(x))}F(x,v)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\lesssim 2^{k+1}\times\|F(x,v)\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}.

(2.1)

2.1.
Gravitational case

Definition 2
.

Suppose
F(x,v)
solve (
0.7
) and (
0.2
), define the boundary outgoing flux on
F
, denoted by
\mathfrak{J}(x)
:

\mathfrak{J}(x)=\int_{n(x)\cdot v>0}F(x,v)\{n(x)\cdot v\}\mathrm{d}v,\ \text{for}\ x\in\partial\Omega.

(2.2)

From (
0.2
), we have for
(x,v)\in\gamma_{-}
,

F(x,v)=\mu_{\Theta}(x,v)\mathfrak{J}(x).

(2.3)

An immediate consequence of Lemma
2
and Remark
1
follows:

Lemma 6
.

Suppose
F
solves (
0.7
) and (
0.2
), and
\mathfrak{J}(x)
defined in (
2.2
), then for
t\geq 0
and
k\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{J}(x)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathcal{V}_{0}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{1}<0}F(X(0;t,x,v^{0}),V(0;t,x,v^{0}))\{n(x)\cdot v^{0}\}\mathrm{d}v^{0}

(2.4)

\displaystyle+\int_{\prod_{j=0}^{1}\mathcal{V}_{j}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{2}<0\leq t^{1}}F(X(0;t^{1},x^{1},v^{1}),V(0;t^{1},x^{1},v^{1}))\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Sigma}_{1}

(2.5)

\displaystyle+\int_{\prod_{j=0}^{i}\mathcal{V}_{j}}\sum\limits^{k-1}_{i=2}\Big\{\mathbf{1}_{t^{i+1}<0\leq t^{i}}F(X(0;t^{i},x^{i},v^{i}),V(0;t^{i},x^{i},v^{i}))\Big\}\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Sigma}_{i}

(2.6)

\displaystyle+\int_{\prod_{j=0}^{k}\mathcal{V}_{j}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{k}\geq 0}F(x^{k},v^{k})\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Sigma}_{k},

(2.7)

where

\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Sigma}_{i}:=\frac{\mathrm{d}\sigma_{i}}{\mu_{\Theta}(x^{i+1},v^{i})}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{i-1}\cdots\mathrm{d}\sigma_{1}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{0}
, with
\mathrm{d}\sigma_{j}=\mu_{\Theta}(x^{j+1},v^{j})\{n(x^{j})\cdot v^{j}\}\mathrm{d}v^{j}
in (
0.16
).
Here,
(X,V)
in (
1.1
).

For the proof of Proposition
5
, we start with first two terms in
\mathfrak{J}(x)
stochastic cycles.

2.1.1.
Estimate on first two terms in
\mathfrak{J}(x)

We first do estimate on (
2.4
). From Lemma
3
, Remark
1
and (
2.3
), we have

\begin{split}\eqref{expand_G11}&=\int_{n(x)\cdot v^{0}>0}\mathbf{1}_{t^{1}<0}F(x^{1},v_{\mathbf{b}}^{0})\{n(x)\cdot v^{0}\}\mathrm{d}v^{0}\\
&=\int_{n(x)\cdot v^{0}>0}\mathbf{1}_{t^{1}<0}\ \mu_{\Theta}(x^{1},v^{0})\mathfrak{J}(x^{1})\{n(x)\cdot v^{0}\}\mathrm{d}v^{0}\\
&\leq\|\mathfrak{J}(x)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\int_{n(x)\cdot v^{0}>0}\mathbf{1}_{t^{1}<0}\ \mu_{\Theta}(x^{1},v^{0})\{n(x)\cdot v^{0}\}\mathrm{d}v^{0}.\end{split}

(2.8)

Since
t^{1}<0
,
|n(x)\cdot v^{0}|\gtrsim t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v^{0})=t-t^{1}>t\gg 1

and using the notation
v^{0}=(v^{0}_{1},v^{0}_{2},v^{0}_{3})
, we deduce

\begin{split}\int_{n(x)\cdot v^{0}>0}\mathbf{1}_{t^{1}<0}\ \mu_{\Theta}(x^{1},v^{0})\{n(x)\cdot v^{0}\}\mathrm{d}v^{0}\lesssim\int_{v^{0}_{3}\gtrsim t}\ e^{-\frac{|v^{0}_{3}|^{2}}{2b}}\{v^{0}_{3}\}\mathrm{d}v^{0}_{3}\lesssim e^{-\frac{t^{2}}{2b}}.\end{split}

(2.9)

From (
2.8
) and (
2.9
), we derive

\int_{\mathcal{V}_{0}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{1}<0}F(X(0;t,x,v^{0}),V(0;t,x,v^{0}))\{n(x)\cdot v^{0}\}\mathrm{d}v^{0}\lesssim\|\mathfrak{J}(x)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\times e^{-\frac{t^{2}}{2b}}.

(2.10)

Next we do estimate on (
2.5
). Again from Lemma
3
and (
2.3
), we have

\begin{split}\eqref{expand_G12}&=\int_{\mathcal{V}_{0}}\int_{\mathcal{V}_{1}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{2}<0\leq t^{1}}F(x^{2},v_{\mathbf{b}}^{1})\{n(x^{1})\cdot v^{1}\}\mathrm{d}v^{1}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{0}\\
&=\int_{\mathcal{V}_{0}}\int_{\mathcal{V}_{1}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{2}<0\leq t^{1}}\ \mu_{\Theta}(x^{2},v^{1})\mathfrak{J}(x^{2})\{n(x^{1})\cdot v^{1}\}\mathrm{d}v^{1}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{0}\\
&\lesssim\|\mathfrak{J}(x)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\int_{\mathcal{V}_{0}}\int_{\mathcal{V}_{1}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{2}<0\leq t^{1}}\ \mu_{\Theta}(x^{2},v^{1})\{n(x^{1})\cdot v^{1}\}\mathrm{d}v^{1}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{0}.\end{split}

(2.11)

Since
t^{2}<0
,
t-t^{2}=t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v^{0})+t_{\mathbf{b}}(x^{1},v^{1})
and
|n(x)\cdot v^{0}|\gtrsim t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v^{0})
,
|n(x^{1})\cdot v^{1}|\gtrsim t_{\mathbf{b}}(x^{1},v^{1})
, we get

|n(x)\cdot v^{0}|+|n(x^{1})\cdot v^{1}|\gtrsim t-t^{2}>t.

After setting
v^{0}_{3}=n(x)\cdot v^{0}
and
v^{1}_{3}=n(x^{1})\cdot v^{1}
, we have

\begin{split}&\ \ \ \ \int_{\mathcal{V}_{0}}\int_{\mathcal{V}_{1}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{2}<0\leq t^{1}}\ \mu_{\Theta}(x^{2},v^{1})\{n(x^{1})\cdot v^{1}\}\mathrm{d}v^{1}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{0}\\
&\lesssim\int_{v^{0}_{3}\gtrsim t/2}\ e^{-\frac{|v^{0}_{3}|^{2}}{2b}}\{v^{0}_{3}\}\mathrm{d}v^{0}_{3}\int_{\mathcal{V}_{1}}e^{-\frac{|v^{1}_{3}|^{2}}{2b}}\{v^{1}_{3}\}\mathrm{d}v^{1}_{3}+\int_{\mathcal{V}_{0}}\ e^{-\frac{|v^{0}_{3}|^{2}}{2b}}\{v^{0}_{3}\}\mathrm{d}v^{0}_{3}\int_{v^{1}_{3}\gtrsim t/2}e^{-\frac{|v^{1}_{3}|^{2}}{2b}}\{v^{1}_{3}\}\mathrm{d}v^{1}_{3},\\
&\lesssim\int_{v^{0}_{3}\gtrsim t/2}\ e^{-\frac{|v^{0}_{3}|^{2}}{2b}}\{v^{0}_{3}\}\mathrm{d}v^{0}_{3}+\int_{v^{1}_{3}\gtrsim t/2}e^{-\frac{|v^{1}_{3}|^{2}}{2b}}\{v^{1}_{3}\}\mathrm{d}v^{1}_{3}\lesssim e^{-\frac{t^{2}}{8b}}.\end{split}

(2.12)

From (
2.11
) and (
2.12
), we derive

\int_{\prod_{j=0}^{1}\mathcal{V}_{j}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{2}<0\leq t^{1}}F(X(0;t^{1},x^{1},v^{1}),V(0;t^{1},x^{1},v^{1}))\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Sigma}_{1}\lesssim\|\mathfrak{J}(x)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\times e^{-\frac{t^{2}}{8b}}.

(2.13)

2.1.2.
Estimate on last two terms in
\mathfrak{J}(x)

To estimate (
2.6
), we first apply Proposition
3
on
\mathcal{V}_{j}
for
j=i-2,i-1
and using
|n(x^{j})\cdot v^{j}|\lesssim t_{\mathbf{b}}^{j}
, we derive that

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \int_{\mathcal{V}_{i-2}}\int_{\mathcal{V}_{i-1}}\int_{\mathcal{V}_{i}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{i+1}<0\leq t^{i}}|F(X(0;t^{i},x^{i},v^{i}),V(0;t^{i},x^{i},v^{i}))|\{n(x^{i})\cdot v^{i}\}\mathrm{d}v^{i}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{i-1}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{i-2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{t^{i-2}}\mathrm{d}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}\int_{\partial\Omega}\frac{\mathrm{d}S_{x^{i-1}}}{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}}\sum\limits_{m,n\in\mathbb{Z}}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{i-1},v^{m,n}_{i-2,\mathbf{b}})\int_{0}^{t^{i-2}-t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}}\mathrm{d}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}\int_{\partial\Omega}\frac{\mathrm{d}S_{x^{i}}}{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}}\sum\limits_{m,n\in\mathbb{Z}}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{i},v^{m,n}_{i-1,\mathbf{b}})

(2.14)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \times\bigg(\int_{\mathcal{V}_{i}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{i}-t_{\mathbf{b}}(x^{i},v^{i})<0}\ |F(X(0;t^{i},x^{i},v^{i}),V(0;t^{i},x^{i},v^{i}))|\{n(x^{i})\cdot v^{i}\}\mathrm{d}v^{i}\bigg),

(2.15)

with
t^{i-1}=t^{i-2}-t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}
,
t^{i}=t^{i-1}-t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}
and
v^{m,n}_{i-1,\mathbf{b}}=v_{\mathbf{b}}(x^{i},v^{m,n}_{i-1})
,
v^{m,n}_{i-2,\mathbf{b}}=v_{\mathbf{b}}(x^{i-1},v^{m,n}_{i-2})
.

Lemma 7
.

Consider
(X,V)
in (
1.1
) with
x^{i-1}\in\partial\Omega
, for
t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}\geq 1
,

\sum\limits_{m,n\in\mathbb{Z}}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{i-1},v^{m,n}_{i-2,\mathbf{b}})\lesssim(t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2})^{4}e^{-(t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2})^{2}/2b}.

(2.16)

For
0\leq t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}<1
,

\sum\limits_{m,n\in\mathbb{Z}}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{i-1},v^{m,n}_{i-2,\mathbf{b}})\lesssim\sum\limits_{|m|<2,|n|<2}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{i-1},v^{m,n}_{i-2,\mathbf{b}})+e^{-\frac{1}{2b(t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2})^{2}}},

(2.17)

where
v^{m,n}_{i-2,\mathbf{b}}=v_{\mathbf{b}}(x^{i-1},v^{m,n}_{i-2})
, which was defined in (
0.15
) and (
1.18
).

Proof.

Here, for the sake of simplicity, we have abused the notations temporarily:

x^{i}=(x^{i}_{1},x^{i}_{2},x^{i}_{3})=(x^{i}_{\parallel},x^{i}_{3}),\ v^{m,n}_{i,\mathbf{b}}=(v^{m,n}_{i,\mathbf{b}_{1}},v^{m,n}_{i,\mathbf{b}_{2}},v^{m,n}_{i,\mathbf{b}_{3}})=(v^{m,n}_{i,\mathbf{b}_{\parallel}},v^{m,n}_{i,\mathbf{b}_{3}}).

To estimate
v^{m,n}_{i-2,\mathbf{b}_{\parallel}}
, we recall that

\|\nabla_{x}\phi\|_{\infty}\leq\varrho_{3}\ll 1.
Thus, we have

\begin{split}&|v^{m,n}_{i-2,\mathbf{b}_{1}}|\geq\min\big\{\frac{|x^{i-1}_{1}+m-x^{i-2}_{1}|}{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}}-\varrho_{3}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2},0\big\},\\
&|v^{m,n}_{i-2,\mathbf{b}_{2}}|\geq\min\big\{\frac{|x^{i-1}_{2}+n-x^{i-2}_{2}|}{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}}-\varrho_{3}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2},0\big\}.\end{split}

(2.18)

Now we split the length of
t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}
into two cases:

Case 1:

t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}\geq 1
. From (
2.18
) and
\varrho_{3}\ll 1
, for
|m|\geq(t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2})^{2}
, we bound

\frac{|x^{i-1}_{1}+m-x^{i-2}_{1}|}{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}}-\varrho_{3}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}\gtrsim\frac{|x^{i-1}_{1}+m-x^{i-2}_{1}|}{2t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}}\gtrsim\frac{|m|}{2t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}}.

Similarly, for
|n|\geq(t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2})^{2}
, we bound

\frac{|x^{i-1}_{2}+n-x^{i-2}_{2}|}{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}}-\varrho_{3}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}\gtrsim\frac{|x^{i-1}_{2}+n-x^{i-2}_{2}|}{2t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}}\gtrsim\frac{|n|}{2t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}}.

For
|m|<(t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2})^{2}
, we bound
|v^{m,n}_{i-2,\mathbf{b}_{1}}|\geq 0
, and for
|n|<(t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2})^{2}
, we bound
|v^{m,n}_{i-2,\mathbf{b}_{2}}|\geq 0
. Note that from (
1.17
), we know

t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}\lesssim v^{m,n}_{i-2,\mathbf{b}_{3}}\lesssim t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}
.
To derive (
2.16
), we divide
\{v^{m,n}_{i-2,\mathbf{b}}\}_{m,n\in\mathbb{Z}}
into four parts.

For
|m|<(t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2})^{2}
and
|n|<(t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2})^{2}
, we bound

|v^{m,n}_{i-2,\mathbf{b}}|\geq|v^{m,n}_{i-2,\mathbf{b}_{3}}|\gtrsim t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}
. Therefore, we have

\sum\limits_{|m|<(t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2})^{2},|n|<(t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2})^{2}}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{i-1},v^{m,n}_{i-2,\mathbf{b}})\lesssim(t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2})^{4}e^{-(t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2})^{2}/2b}.

(2.19)

For
|m|<(t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2})^{2}
and
|n|\geq(t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2})^{2}
, we bound

|v^{m,n}_{i-2,\mathbf{b}_{2}}|\gtrsim\frac{|n|}{2t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}}
. Thus, we have

\begin{split}\sum\limits_{|m|<(t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2})^{2},|n|\geq(t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2})^{2}}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{i-1},v^{m,n}_{i-2,\mathbf{b}})&\lesssim\sum\limits_{|m|<(t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2})^{2}}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{i-1},\frac{|n|}{2t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}}+t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2})\\
&\lesssim(t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2})^{2}e^{-(t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2})^{2}/2b}\sum\limits^{\infty}_{n=0}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{i-1},\frac{|n|}{2t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}})\\
&\leq(t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2})^{2}e^{-(t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2})^{2}/2b}(1-e^{-\frac{1}{8b(t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2})^{2}}})^{-1}\lesssim(t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2})^{4}e^{-(t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2})^{2}/2b},\end{split}

(2.20)

where the last inequality holds from the Taylor expansion.

For
|m|\geq(t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2})^{2}
and
|n|<(t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2})^{2}
case, we bound

|v^{m,n}_{i-2,\mathbf{b}_{1}}|\gtrsim\frac{|m|}{2t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}}
. Similar as in (
2.20
), we get

\sum\limits_{|m|\geq(t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2})^{2},|n|<(t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2})^{2}}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{i-1},v^{m,n}_{i-2,\mathbf{b}})\lesssim(t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2})^{4}e^{-(t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2})^{2}/2b}.

(2.21)

For
|m|\geq(t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2})^{2}
and
|n|\geq(t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2})^{2}
, we need to use two lower bounds

|v^{m,n}_{i-2,\mathbf{b}_{2}}|\gtrsim\frac{|n|}{2t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}}
,
|v^{m,n}_{i-2,\mathbf{b}_{1}}|\gtrsim\frac{|m|}{2t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}}
. Then, we derive

\begin{split}\sum\limits_{|m|\geq(t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2})^{2},|n|\geq(t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2})^{2}}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{i-1},v^{m,n}_{i-2,\mathbf{b}})&\lesssim\sum\limits^{\infty}_{m,n=0}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{i-1},\frac{|m|}{2t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}}+\frac{|n|}{2t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}}+t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2})\\
&\lesssim e^{-(t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2})^{2}/2b}\sum\limits^{\infty}_{n=0}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{i-1},\frac{|n|}{2t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}})(1-e^{-\frac{1}{8(t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2})^{2}}})^{-1}\\
&\lesssim e^{-(t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2})^{2}/2b}(1-e^{-\frac{1}{8(t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2})^{2}}})^{-2}\lesssim(t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2})^{4}e^{-(t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2})^{2}/2b}.\end{split}

(2.22)

From (
2.19
), (
2.20
), (
2.21
) and (
2.22
), we conclude that, for
t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}\geq 1
,

\sum\limits_{m,n\in\mathbb{Z}}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{i-1},v^{m,n}_{i-2,\mathbf{b}})\lesssim(t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2})^{4}e^{-(t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2})^{2}/2b}.

Case 2:

0\leq t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}<1
.
In this case
t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}
is small, thus from (
2.18
) and
\varrho_{3}\ll 1
, for
|m|\geq 2
and
|n|\geq 2
, we bound

\begin{split}&\frac{|x^{i-1}_{1}+m-x^{i-2}_{1}|}{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}}-\varrho_{3}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}\gtrsim\frac{|x^{i-1}_{1}+m-x^{i-2}_{1}|}{2t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}}\gtrsim\frac{|m|}{2t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}},\\
&\frac{|x^{i-1}_{2}+n-x^{i-2}_{2}|}{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}}-\varrho_{3}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}\gtrsim\frac{|x^{i-1}_{2}+n-x^{i-2}_{2}|}{2t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}}\gtrsim\frac{|n|}{2t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}}.\end{split}

For
|m|<2
, we bound
|v^{m,n}_{i-2,\mathbf{b}_{1}}|\geq 0
, and for
|n|<2
, we bound
|v^{m,n}_{i-2,\mathbf{b}_{2}}|\geq 0
. Note that from (
1.17
), we know

t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}\lesssim v^{m,n}_{i-2,\mathbf{b}_{3}}\lesssim t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}
.
To obtain (
2.17
), we again divide
\{v^{m,n}_{i-2,\mathbf{b}}\}_{m,n\in\mathbb{Z}}
into four parts.

For
|m|<2
and
|n|<2
, we keep the following five terms summation:

\sum\limits_{|m|<2,|n|<2}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{i-1},v^{m,n}_{i-2,\mathbf{b}}).

(2.23)

For
|m|<2
and
|n|\geq 2
, we bound

|v^{m,n}_{i-2,\mathbf{b}_{2}}|\gtrsim\frac{|n|}{2t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}}
. Thus, we have

\begin{split}\sum\limits_{|m|<2,|n|\geq 2}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{i-1},v^{m,n}_{i-2,\mathbf{b}})&\lesssim\sum\limits_{|m|<2,|n|\geq 2}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{i-1},\frac{|n|}{2t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}}+t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2})\\
&\lesssim 3e^{-(t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2})^{2}/2b}\sum\limits^{\infty}_{n=2}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{i-1},\frac{|n|}{2t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}})\lesssim\sum\limits^{\infty}_{n=2}e^{-\frac{n^{2}}{8b(t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2})^{2}}}\\
&\leq e^{-\frac{1}{2b(t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2})^{2}}}(1-e^{-\frac{1}{8b(t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2})^{2}}})^{-1}\lesssim e^{-\frac{1}{2b(t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2})^{2}}},\end{split}

(2.24)

where the last inequality holds from
0\leq t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}<1
.

For
|m|\geq 2
and
|n|<2
case, we bound

|v^{m,n}_{i-2,\mathbf{b}_{1}}|\gtrsim\frac{|m|}{2t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}}
. Similar as in (
2.24
), we get

\sum\limits_{|m|\geq 2,|n|<2}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{i-1},v^{m,n}_{i-2,\mathbf{b}})\lesssim e^{-\frac{1}{2b(t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2})^{2}}}.

(2.25)

For
|m|\geq 2
and
|n|\geq 2
, we bound

|v^{m,n}_{i-2,\mathbf{b}_{2}}|\gtrsim\frac{|n|}{2t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}}
,
|v^{m,n}_{i-2,\mathbf{b}_{1}}|\gtrsim\frac{|m|}{2t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}}
and we derive

\begin{split}\sum\limits_{|m|\geq 2,|n|\geq 2}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{i-1},v^{m,n}_{i-2,\mathbf{b}})&\lesssim\sum\limits^{\infty}_{m,n=2}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{i-1},\frac{|m|}{2t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}}+\frac{|n|}{2t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}}+t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2})\\
&\lesssim e^{-(t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2})^{2}/2b}\sum\limits^{\infty}_{n=2}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{i-1},\frac{|n|}{2t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}})e^{-\frac{1}{2b(t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2})^{2}}}\lesssim e^{-\frac{1}{2b(t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2})^{2}}}.\end{split}

(2.26)

From (
2.23
), (
2.24
), (
2.25
) and (
2.26
), we conclude that, for
0\leq t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}<1
,

\sum\limits_{m,n\in\mathbb{Z}}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{i-1},v^{m,n}_{i-2,\mathbf{b}})\lesssim\sum\limits_{|m|<2,|n|<2}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{i-1},v^{m,n}_{i-2,\mathbf{b}})+e^{-\frac{1}{2b(t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2})^{2}}},

so we prove (
2.16
) and (
2.17
).
∎

Now we can control (
2.6
) via the following lemma:

Lemma 8
.

For
i=2,\cdots,k-1
and
(X,V)
in (
1.1
), we have

\Big|\int_{\prod_{j=0}^{i}\mathcal{V}_{j}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{i+1}<0\leq t^{i}}F(X(0;t^{i},x^{i},v^{i}),V(0;t^{i},x^{i},v^{i}))\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Sigma}_{i}\Big|\lesssim 2^{i}\times\|F(x,v)\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}.

(2.27)

where

\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Sigma}_{i}:=\frac{\mathrm{d}\sigma_{i}}{\mu_{\Theta}(x^{i+1},v^{i})}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{i-1}\cdots\mathrm{d}\sigma_{1}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{0}
with
\mathrm{d}\sigma_{j}=\mu_{\Theta}(x^{j+1},v^{j})\{n(x^{j})\cdot v^{j}\}\mathrm{d}v^{j}
in (
0.16
).

Proof.

For (
2.27
), it suffices to prove this upper bound for
i=2,...,k-1
,

\int_{\mathcal{V}_{0}}\cdots\int_{\mathcal{V}_{i-1}}\int_{\mathcal{V}_{i}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{i+1}<0\leq t^{i}}|F(X(0;t^{i},x^{i},v^{i}),V(0;t^{i},x^{i},v^{i}))|\{n(x^{i})\cdot v^{i}\}\mathrm{d}v^{i}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{i-1}\cdots\mathrm{d}\sigma_{0}.

(2.28)

Step 1.

Applying Proposition
3
, (
2.14
) and (
2.15
),
we bound the above integration as

\begin{split}\eqref{forcing}\lesssim&\int_{\mathcal{V}_{0}}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{0}\cdots\int_{\mathcal{V}_{i-3}}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{i-3}\int^{t^{i-2}}_{0}\mathrm{d}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}\int_{0}^{t^{i-2}-t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}}\mathrm{d}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}\int_{\partial\Omega}\mathrm{d}S_{x^{i}}\\
&\times\underbrace{\bigg(\int_{\partial\Omega}\frac{\sum\limits_{m,n\in\mathbb{Z}}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{i-1},v^{m,n}_{i-2,\mathbf{b}})}{|t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}|}\times\frac{\sum\limits_{m,n\in\mathbb{Z}}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{i},v^{m,n}_{i-1,\mathbf{b}})}{|t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}|}\mathrm{d}S_{x^{i-1}}\bigg)}_{\eqref{est2:forcing}_{*}}\eqref{est1:forcing}.\end{split}

(2.29)

Step 2.
We claim that

\eqref{est2:forcing}_{*}\lesssim\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}\leq t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}}\langle t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}\rangle^{-5}+\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}\geq t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}}\langle t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}\rangle^{-5}.

(2.30)

We split the cases:

Case 1:

t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}\leq t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}
.
Using (
2.16
) and (
2.17
) in Lemma
7
, we bound

\begin{split}\frac{\sum\limits_{m,n\in\mathbb{Z}}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{i-1},v^{m,n}_{i-2,\mathbf{b}})}{|t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}|}\lesssim\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}\leq 1}\frac{1}{|t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}|}+\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}\geq 1}\frac{1}{|t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}|^{5}},\end{split}

(2.31)

Replacing
i
with
i+1
in (
2.16
) and (
2.17
), we bound

\begin{split}\frac{\sum\limits_{m,n\in\mathbb{Z}}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{i},v^{m,n}_{i-1,\mathbf{b}})}{|t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}|}&\lesssim\underbrace{\sum\limits_{|a|<2,|b|<2}\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}\leq 1}\mathbf{1}_{|x^{i}+(a,b)-x^{i-1}|\leq 2\varrho_{3}(t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1})^{2}}\frac{1}{|t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}|}}_{\eqref{bound2_1and5}_{1}}\\
&+\underbrace{\sum\limits_{|a|<2,|b|<2}\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}\leq 1}\mathbf{1}_{|x^{i}+(a,b)-x^{i-1}|\geq 2\varrho_{3}(t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1})^{2}}\frac{1}{|t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}|}\mu\big(x^{i},\frac{|x^{i}+(a,b)-x^{i-1}|}{2|t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}|}\big)}_{\eqref{bound2_1and5}_{2}}\\
&+\underbrace{\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}\leq 1}\frac{1}{|t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}|}e^{-\frac{1}{2b(t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1})^{2}}}}_{\eqref{bound2_1and5}_{3}}+\underbrace{\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}\geq 1}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{i},|t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}|)}_{\eqref{bound2_1and5}_{4}}.\end{split}

(2.32)

For
\eqref{bound2_1and5}_{1}
, we have

\begin{split}&\ \ \ \ \mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}\leq t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}}\int_{\partial\Omega}\frac{\sum\limits_{m,n\in\mathbb{Z}}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{i-1},v^{m,n}_{i-2,\mathbf{b}})}{|t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}|}\times\eqref{bound2_1and5}_{1}\ \mathrm{d}S_{x^{i-1}}\\
&\lesssim\sum\limits_{|a|<2,|b|<2}\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}\leq t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}}\eqref{bound1_1and5}\bigg(\int_{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}\leq 1,|x^{i}+(a,b)-x^{i-1}|\leq 2\varrho_{3}(t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1})^{2}}\frac{1}{|t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}|}\mathrm{d}S_{x^{i-1}}\bigg)\\
&\lesssim\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}\leq t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}}\Big\{\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}\leq 1}\frac{1}{|t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}|}+\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}\geq 1}\frac{1}{|t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}|^{5}}\Big\}\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}\leq 1}\frac{(\varrho_{3}(t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1})^{2})^{2}}{|t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}|}\\
&\leq\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}\leq t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}}\frac{(t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1})^{3}}{|t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}|}\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}\leq 1}+\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}\geq 1}\frac{1}{|t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}|^{5}}\\
&\leq\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}\leq 1}+\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}\geq 1}\frac{1}{|t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}|^{5}}.\end{split}

(2.33)

For
\eqref{bound2_1and5}_{2}
, we employ a change of variables, for
x^{i}\in\partial\Omega
,
|a|<2
,
|b|<2
and
t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}\geq 0
,
x^{i-1}\in\partial\Omega\mapsto z:=\frac{1}{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}}(x^{i-1}+(a,b)-x^{i})\in\mathfrak{S}^{a,b}_{x^{i},t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}},
where the image
\mathfrak{S}^{a,b}_{x^{i},t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}}
of the map is a two dimensional smooth plane. Using the local chart of
\partial\Omega
we have

\mathrm{d}S_{x^{i-1}}\lesssim|t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}|^{2}\mathrm{d}S_{z}.

From this change of variables and (
2.31
), we conclude that

\begin{split}&\ \ \ \ \mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}\leq t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}}\mathbf{1}_{|x^{i}+(a,b)-x^{i-1}|\geq 2\epsilon(t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1})^{2}}\int_{\partial\Omega}\frac{\sum\limits_{m,n\in\mathbb{Z}}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{i-1},v^{m,n}_{i-2,\mathbf{b}})}{|t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}|}\times\eqref{bound2_1and5}_{2}\ \mathrm{d}S_{x^{i-1}}\\
&\lesssim\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}\leq t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}}\eqref{bound1_1and5}\sum\limits_{|a|<2,|b|<2}\int_{\mathfrak{S}^{a,b}_{x^{i},t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}}}\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}\leq 1}e^{-z^{2}/8b}|t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}|\mathrm{d}S_{z}\\
&\lesssim\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}\leq t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}}\Big\{\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}\leq 1}\frac{1}{|t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}|}+\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}\geq 1}\frac{1}{|t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}|^{5}}\Big\}\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}\leq 1}|t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}|\\
&\lesssim\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}\leq t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}}\Big\{\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}\leq 1}\frac{|t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}|}{|t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}|}+\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}\geq 1}\frac{1}{|t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}|^{5}}\Big\}\lesssim\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}\leq 1}+\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}\geq 1}\frac{1}{|t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}|^{5}}.\end{split}

(2.34)

For
\eqref{bound2_1and5}_{3}
, since
e^{-\frac{1}{2bt^{2}}}\lesssim t^{2}
for
0<t\leq 1
, and we have that

\begin{split}&\ \ \ \ \mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}\leq t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}}\int_{\partial\Omega}\frac{\sum\limits_{m,n\in\mathbb{Z}}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{i-1},v^{m,n}_{i-2,\mathbf{b}})}{|t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}|}\times\eqref{bound2_1and5}_{3}\ \mathrm{d}S_{x^{i-1}}\\
&\lesssim\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}\leq t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}}\eqref{bound1_1and5}\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}\leq 1}\frac{1}{|t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}|}e^{-\frac{1}{2b(t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1})^{2}}}\int_{\partial\Omega}\mathrm{d}S_{x^{i-1}}\\
&\lesssim\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}\leq t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}}\Big\{\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}\leq 1}\frac{1}{|t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}|}+\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}\geq 1}\frac{1}{|t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}|^{5}}\Big\}\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}\leq 1}|t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}|\\
&\leq\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}\leq t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}}\frac{|t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}|}{|t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}|}\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}\leq 1}+\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}\geq 1}\frac{1}{|t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}|^{5}}\\
&\leq\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}\leq 1}+\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}\geq 1}\frac{1}{|t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}|^{5}}.\end{split}

(2.35)

For
\eqref{bound2_1and5}_{4}
, from

\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}\geq 1}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{i},|t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}|)\lesssim\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}\geq 1}
, we can derive that

\begin{split}&\ \ \ \ \mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}\leq t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}}\int_{\partial\Omega}\frac{\sum\limits_{m,n\in\mathbb{Z}}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{i-1},v^{m,n}_{i-2,\mathbf{b}})}{|t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}|}\times\eqref{bound2_1and5}_{4}\ \mathrm{d}S_{x^{i-1}}\\
&\lesssim\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}\leq t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}}\eqref{bound1_1and5}\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}>1}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{i},|t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}|)\int_{\partial\Omega}\mathrm{d}S_{x^{i-1}}\\
&\lesssim\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}\leq t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}}\Big\{\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}\leq 1}\frac{1}{|t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}|}+\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}\geq 1}\frac{1}{|t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}|^{5}}\Big\}\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}\geq 1}\\
&\lesssim\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}\geq 1}\frac{1}{|t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}|^{5}}.\end{split}

(2.36)

Collecting estimate from (
2.33
)-(
2.36
), we deduce that

\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}\leq t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}}\eqref{est2:forcing}_{*}\lesssim\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}\leq 1}+\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}\geq 1}{|t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}|^{-5}}.

(2.37)

Case 2:

t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}\geq t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}
.
We change the role of
i-1
and
i-2
and follow the argument of the previous case. We employ a change of variables, for
x^{i-1}\in\partial\Omega

|a|<2
,
|b|<2
and
t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}\geq 0
,
x^{i-2}\in\partial\Omega\mapsto z:=\frac{1}{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}}(x^{i-2}-x^{i-1})\in\mathfrak{S}_{x^{i-1},t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}},
with
\mathrm{d}S^{a,b}_{x^{i-2}}\lesssim|t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}|^{2}\mathrm{d}S_{z}.

Then we can conclude that

\begin{split}\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}\geq t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}}\eqref{est2:forcing}_{*}\lesssim\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}\leq 1}+\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}\geq 1}{|t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}|^{-5}}.\end{split}

(2.38)

Clearly the above bound and (
2.37
) imply (
2.30
).

Step 3.
Now we apply (
2.30
) on (
2.29
). Then we have

(
2.28
)

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{\mathcal{V}_{0}}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{0}\cdots\int_{\mathcal{V}_{i-3}}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{i-3}\int^{t^{i-2}}_{0}\frac{\mathrm{d}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}}{\langle t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}\rangle^{5}}\int_{0}^{\min\{t^{i-2}-t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1},t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}\}}\mathrm{d}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}\int_{\partial\Omega}\mathrm{d}S_{x^{i}}\eqref{est1:forcing}

(2.39)

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathcal{V}_{0}}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{0}\cdots\int_{\mathcal{V}_{i-3}}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{i-3}\int^{t^{i-2}}_{0}\frac{\mathrm{d}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}}{\langle t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}\rangle^{5}}\int_{0}^{\min\{t^{i-2}-t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2},t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}\}}\mathrm{d}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}\int_{\partial\Omega}\mathrm{d}S_{x^{i}}\eqref{est1:forcing}.

(2.40)

For (
2.39
), we employ the change of variables

(x^{i},t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2},v^{i})\mapsto(y,w)=(X(0;t^{i-2}-t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}-t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1},x^{i},v^{i}),V(0;t^{i-2}-t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}-t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1},x^{i},v^{i}))\in\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}.

From (
1.3
), we derive

|n(x^{i})\cdot v^{i}|\mathrm{d}S_{x^{i}}\mathrm{d}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}\mathrm{d}v^{i}\lesssim\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}w
.
Thus, we bound (
2.39
) as

\begin{split}\eqref{est_a:forcing}&\leq\int_{\mathcal{V}_{0}}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{0}\cdots\int_{\mathcal{V}_{i-3}}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{i-3}\int^{t^{i-2}}_{0}\mathrm{d}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}\langle t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}\rangle^{-5}\iint_{\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}|F(y,w)|\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}w\\
&\lesssim 2^{i}\times\|F(x,v)\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}.\end{split}

A bound of (
2.40
) can be derived similarly, by using the change of variables

(x^{i},t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1},v^{i})\mapsto(y,w)=(X(0;t^{i-2}-t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}-t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1},x^{i},v^{i}),V(0;t_{i-2}-t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}-t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1},x^{i},v^{i}))\in\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3},

with
|n(x^{i})\cdot v^{i}|\mathrm{d}S_{x^{i}}\mathrm{d}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}\mathrm{d}v^{i}\lesssim\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}w
.
∎

Finally, we control (
2.7
) by establishing the following estimate:

Lemma 9
.

Consider
(X,V)
in (
1.1
), there exists
\mathfrak{C}=\mathfrak{C}(\Omega)>0
(see (
2.46
) for the precise choice),
such that

\text{if }\ k\geq\mathfrak{C}t,\text{ then }\sup_{(x,v)\in\bar{\Omega}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Big(\int_{\prod_{j=0}^{k-1}\mathcal{V}_{j}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{k}(t,x,v,v^{1},\cdots,v^{k-1})\geq 0}\ \mathrm{d}\sigma_{0}\cdots\mathrm{d}\sigma_{k-1}\Big)\lesssim e^{-t},

(2.41)

where
\mathrm{d}\sigma_{j}=\mu_{\Theta}(x^{j+1},v^{j})\{n(x^{j})\cdot v^{j}\}\mathrm{d}v^{j}
in (
0.16
).

Proof.

Step 1.

Since
|n(x)\cdot v|\lesssim t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)
for
(x,v)\in\gamma_{+}
, we get

\mathbf{1}_{|n(x)\cdot v|\lesssim\delta}\geq\mathbf{1}_{\delta>t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)}.

Setting
\vartheta
as the angle between
v
and
n(x)
and
r=|v|
, we have

\begin{split}&\ \ \ \ \int_{n(x)\cdot v>0}\mathbf{1}_{\delta>t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)}\mu_{\Theta}(x_{\mathbf{b}},v)|n(x)\cdot v|\mathrm{d}v\\
&\lesssim\int_{|n(x)\cdot v|\lesssim\delta}\mu_{\Theta}(x_{\mathbf{b}},v)|n(x)\cdot v|\mathrm{d}v\\
&\leq\int_{|n(x)\cdot v|\lesssim\delta}\mu_{\Theta}(x_{\mathbf{b}},v)\delta\mathrm{d}v\leq C\int^{\infty}_{0}\delta e^{-\frac{r^{2}}{2b}}r^{2}\mathrm{d}r\int_{\cos\vartheta<\delta/r}\sin\vartheta\mathrm{d}\vartheta\\
&\leq C\int^{\infty}_{0}\delta e^{-\frac{r^{2}}{2b}}\delta r\mathrm{d}r\lesssim C\delta^{2}.\end{split}

(2.42)

Now we define
\mathcal{V}^{\delta}_{i}:=\{v^{i}\in\mathcal{V}_{i}:{|n(x^{i})\cdot v^{i}|}<\delta\}
. Recall (
2.42
), we have

\int_{\mathcal{V}^{\delta}_{j}}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{j}\leq C\delta^{2}.

On the other hand, we have
t_{\mathbf{b}}(x^{i},v^{i})\gtrsim{|n(x^{i})\cdot v^{i}|}
. Therefore, if
v^{i}\in\mathcal{V}_{i}\backslash\mathcal{V}^{\delta}_{i}
, we derive that

t_{\mathbf{b}}(x^{i},v^{i})\geq C_{\Omega}\delta.

If
t_{k}(t,x,v^{0},v^{1},\cdots,v^{k-1})\geq 0
, we conclude such
v^{i}\in\mathcal{V}_{i}\backslash\mathcal{V}^{\delta}_{i}
can exist at most
[\frac{t}{C_{\Omega}\delta}]+1
times. Denote the combination

\begin{pmatrix}M\\
N\end{pmatrix}=\frac{M(M-1)\cdots(M-N+1)}{N(N-1)\cdots 1}=\frac{M!}{N!(M-N)!}
for
M,N\in\mathbb{N}
and
M\geq N
. From Remark
2
and
0<\delta\ll 1
, we have

\begin{split}&\ \ \ \ \int_{\prod_{j=0}^{k-1}\mathcal{V}_{j}}\mathbf{1}_{t_{k}(t,x,v^{0},v^{1},\cdots,v^{k-1})\geq 0}\ \mathrm{d}\sigma_{k-1}\cdots\mathrm{d}\sigma_{0}\\
&\leq 2^{k}\sum\limits^{[\frac{t}{C_{\Omega}\delta}]+1}_{m=0}\begin{pmatrix}k\\
m\end{pmatrix}\big(\int_{\mathcal{V}_{i}^{\delta}}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{i}\big)^{k-m}\leq 2^{k}(C\delta^{2})^{k-[\frac{t}{C_{\Omega}\delta}]}\underbrace{\sum\limits^{[\frac{t}{C_{\Omega}\delta}]+1}_{m=0}\begin{pmatrix}k\\
m\end{pmatrix}}_{\eqref{sum_bound}_{*}}.\end{split}

(2.43)

Step 2.

Recall the Stirling’s formula,

\sqrt{2\pi}k^{k+\frac{1}{2}}e^{-k}\leq k!\leq k^{k+\frac{1}{2}}e^{-k+1}.

(2.44)

Using
(1+\frac{1}{\mathfrak{a}-1})^{\mathfrak{a}-1}\leq e
and (
2.44
), we have for
\mathfrak{a}\geq 2
,

\begin{split}\begin{pmatrix}k\\
\frac{k}{\mathfrak{a}}\end{pmatrix}=\frac{k!}{(k-\frac{k}{\mathfrak{a}})!\frac{k}{\mathfrak{a}}!}&\leq(\frac{\mathfrak{a}}{\mathfrak{a}-1})^{\frac{\mathfrak{a}}{\mathfrak{a}-1}k}\mathfrak{a}^{\frac{k}{\mathfrak{a}}}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{a}^{2}}{k(\mathfrak{a}-1)}}\\
&=\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}\bigg(\mathfrak{a}^{\frac{1}{\mathfrak{a}}}\big(\frac{\mathfrak{a}}{\mathfrak{a}-1}\big)^{\frac{\mathfrak{a}}{\mathfrak{a}-1}}\bigg)^{k}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{a}^{2}}{\mathfrak{a}-1}}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}(e\mathfrak{a})^{\frac{k}{\mathfrak{a}}}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{a}^{2}}{\mathfrak{a}-1}}.\end{split}

Hence, we derive that

\begin{split}\sum\limits^{[\frac{k}{\mathfrak{a}}]}_{i=1}\begin{pmatrix}k\\
i\end{pmatrix}\leq\frac{k}{\mathfrak{a}}\begin{pmatrix}k\\
\frac{k}{\mathfrak{a}}\end{pmatrix}\leq\frac{e}{2\pi}\sqrt{\frac{k}{\mathfrak{a}}}(e\mathfrak{a})^{\frac{k}{\mathfrak{a}}}.\end{split}

(2.45)

Step 3.

Now we estimate
\eqref{sum_bound}_{*}
.
For fixed
0<\delta\ll 1/2
which is independent of
t
, we choose

\mathfrak{a}\in\mathbb{N}\ \text{ such that }(4^{\mathfrak{a}}\delta^{2\mathfrak{a}}e\mathfrak{a})^{\frac{1}{C_{\Omega}\delta}}\leq e^{-2},\ \text{ and set }k:=\mathfrak{a}\Big(\big[\frac{t}{C_{\Omega}\delta}\big]+1\Big).

(2.46)

Using (
2.45
), we have

\eqref{sum_bound}_{*}\lesssim\sqrt{\big[\frac{t}{C_{\Omega}\delta}\big]+1}\Big(e\frac{k}{[\frac{t}{C_{\Omega}\delta}]+1}\Big)^{[\frac{t}{C_{\Omega}\delta}]+1}\lesssim\sqrt{\big[\frac{t}{C_{\Omega}\delta}\big]+1}(e\mathfrak{a})^{[\frac{t}{C_{\Omega}\delta}]+1}.

Hence, we bound (
2.43
) by

2^{k}(\delta^{2\mathfrak{a}}e\mathfrak{a})^{[\frac{t}{C_{\Omega}\delta}]+1}\sqrt{\big[\frac{t}{C_{\Omega}\delta}\big]+1}\lesssim e^{-t}.

This completes the proof and we can conclude

\begin{split}\eqref{expand_G2}&=\int_{\prod_{j=0}^{k}\mathcal{V}_{j}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{k}\geq 0}F(x^{k},v^{k})\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Sigma}_{k}\\
&=\int_{\prod_{j=0}^{k-1}\mathcal{V}_{j}}\mathbf{1}_{t_{k}(t,x,v,v^{1},\cdots,v^{k-1})\geq 0}\ \mathrm{d}\sigma_{0}\cdots\mathrm{d}\sigma_{k-1}\int_{\mathcal{V}_{k}}\mu_{\Theta}(x_{k+1},v_{k})\mathfrak{J}(x_{k+1})\{n(x_{k})\cdot v_{k}\}\mathrm{d}v_{k}\\
&\lesssim\|\mathfrak{J}(x)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\times e^{-t}.\end{split}

(2.47)

∎

Proof of Proposition
5
.

Using (
2.10
), (
2.13
), (
2.47
) and Lemma
8
, we have

\mathfrak{J}(x)\leq\|\mathfrak{J}(x)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\times e^{-\frac{t^{2}}{2b}}+\|\mathfrak{J}(x)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\times e^{-\frac{t^{2}}{8b}}+\sum^{k}_{i=2}2^{i}\times\|F(x,v)\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}+\|\mathfrak{J}(x)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\times e^{-t}.

We can pick sufficiently large but fixed
t
such that

e^{-\frac{t^{2}}{2b}}+e^{-\frac{t^{2}}{8b}}+e^{-t}\leq 1/2,

(2.48)

then we derive

(1-e^{-\frac{t^{2}}{2b}}-e^{-\frac{t^{2}}{8b}}-e^{-t})\|\mathfrak{J}(x)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq\sum^{k}_{i=2}2^{i}\times\|F(x,v)\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}.

Since
t
and
k=\mathfrak{a}\Big(\big[\frac{t}{C_{\Omega}\delta}\big]+1\Big)
are fixed, we conclude that

\|\mathfrak{J}(x)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq(1-e^{-\frac{t^{2}}{2b}}-e^{-\frac{t^{2}}{8b}}-e^{-t})^{-1}\sum^{k}_{i=2}2^{i}\times\|F(x,v)\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}\lesssim 2^{k+1}\times\|F(x,v)\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}.

(2.49)

Moreover,
F(x,v)=F(x_{\mathbf{b}},v_{\mathbf{b}})=\mu_{\Theta}(x_{\mathbf{b}},v_{\mathbf{b}})\mathfrak{J}(x_{\mathbf{b}})
, the
L^{\infty}_{x}
-estimate in (
2.49
) implies

\|\mu^{-1}_{\Theta}(x_{\mathbf{b}},v_{\mathbf{b}})F(x,v)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}=\|\mathfrak{J}(x)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim 2^{k+1}\times\|F(x,v)\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}.

Using
|v|^{2}/2+\Phi(x)=|v_{\mathbf{b}}|^{2}/2
in Lemma
3
and (
0.4
), we get

\mu^{-1}_{\Theta}(x_{\mathbf{b}},v_{\mathbf{b}})=2\pi\Theta^{2}(x_{\mathbf{b}})e^{\frac{|v_{\mathbf{b}}|^{2}}{2\Theta(x_{\mathbf{b}})}}\gtrsim e^{\frac{|v|^{2}/2+\Phi(x)}{2b}}.

(2.50)

Therefore, we prove the proposition.
∎

2.2.
With Magnet field

Similar as Lemma
6
, the stochastic cycles for the boundary outgoing flux
\mathfrak{J}(x)
with magnet field follows:

Lemma 10
.

Suppose
F(x,v)
solves (
0.10
) and (
0.2
), with
\mathfrak{J}(x)
defined in (
2.2
), then for
t\geq 0
and
k\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{J}(x)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathcal{V}_{0}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{1}<0}F(X(0;t,x,v^{0}),V(0;t,x,v^{0}))\{n(x)\cdot v^{0}\}\mathrm{d}v^{0}

(2.51)

\displaystyle+\int_{\prod_{j=0}^{1}\mathcal{V}_{j}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{2}<0\leq t^{1}}F(X(0;t^{1},x^{1},v^{1}),V(0;t^{1},x^{1},v^{1}))\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Sigma}_{1}

(2.52)

\displaystyle+\int_{\prod_{j=0}^{i}\mathcal{V}_{j}}\sum\limits^{k-1}_{i=2}\Big\{\mathbf{1}_{t^{i+1}<0\leq t^{i}}F(X(0;t^{i},x^{i},v^{i}),V(0;t^{i},x^{i},v^{i}))\Big\}\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Sigma}_{i}

(2.53)

\displaystyle+\int_{\prod_{j=0}^{k}\mathcal{V}_{j}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{k}\geq 0}F(x^{k},v^{k})\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Sigma}_{k},

(2.54)

where

\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Sigma}_{i}:=\frac{\mathrm{d}\sigma_{i}}{\mu_{\Theta}(x^{i+1},v^{i})}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{i-1}\cdots\mathrm{d}\sigma_{1}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{0}
with
\mathrm{d}\sigma_{j}=\mu_{\Theta}(x^{j+1},v^{j})\{n(x^{j})\cdot v^{j}\}\mathrm{d}v^{j}
in (
0.16
).
Here,
(X,V)
in (
1.2
).

To prove Proposition
5
for the magnetic case, we start with the estimate on the first two terms in stochastic cycles on
\mathfrak{J}(x)
.
Then we use lemma
13
and
14
to control the rest of terms.

For the first two terms (
2.51
) and (
2.52
).
Following (
2.8
)-(
2.10
) and
t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)=v_{3}/5
, we derive

\eqref{expand_G11 mag}=\int_{\mathcal{V}_{0}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{1}<0}F(X(0;t,x,v^{0}),V(0;t,x,v^{0}))\{n(x)\cdot v^{0}\}\mathrm{d}v^{0}\lesssim\|\mathfrak{J}(x)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\times e^{-\frac{t^{2}}{2b}}.

(2.55)

Similarly, from (
2.11
)-(
2.13
) and using
t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)=v_{3}/5
, we get

\eqref{expand_G12 mag}=\int_{\prod_{j=0}^{1}\mathcal{V}_{j}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{2}<0\leq t^{1}}F(X(0;t^{1},x^{1},v^{1}),V(0;t^{1},x^{1},v^{1}))\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Sigma}_{1}\lesssim\|\mathfrak{J}(x)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\times e^{-\frac{t^{2}}{8b}}.

(2.56)

To estimate (
2.53
), we need to apply Proposition
4
on
\mathcal{V}_{i-1}
and derive

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \int_{\mathcal{V}_{i-1}}\int_{\mathcal{V}_{i}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{i+1}<0\leq t^{i}}|F(X(0;t^{i},x^{i},v^{i}),V(0;t^{i},x^{i},v^{i}))|\{n(x^{i})\cdot v^{i}\}\mathrm{d}v^{i}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{i-1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int^{t^{i-1}}_{0}\int_{\partial\Omega}\frac{5B^{2}_{3}\sum\limits_{m,n\in\mathbb{Z}}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{i},v^{m,n}_{i-1,\mathbf{b}})}{2-2\cos(B_{3}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1})}\{n(x^{i-1})\cdot v^{i-1}\}\mathrm{d}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}\mathrm{d}S_{x^{i}}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \times\int_{\mathcal{V}_{i}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{i+1}<0\leq t^{i}}\ |F(X(0;t^{i},x^{i},v^{i}),V(0;t^{i},x^{i},v^{i}))|\{n(x^{i})\cdot v^{i}\}\mathrm{d}v^{i}.

(2.57)

To control the integrand term
\frac{\sum\limits_{m,n\in\mathbb{Z}}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{i},v^{m,n}_{i-1,\mathbf{b}})}{2-2\cos(B_{3}t^{i}_{\mathbf{b}})}
in (
2.57
), we introduce the following:

Definition 3
.

For fixed
x\in\Omega
, we categorize
v\in\mathcal{V}:=\{v\in\mathbb{R}^{3}:n(x)\cdot v>0\}
into two sets:

\begin{split}&\mathcal{V}^{G_{\mathbf{\varepsilon}}}(x):=\{v\in\mathcal{V}:\frac{\varepsilon}{B_{3}}+\frac{2k\pi}{B_{3}}\leq t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)\leq\frac{2\pi-\varepsilon}{B_{3}}+\frac{2k\pi}{B_{3}}\ \text{with}\ k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}\},\\
&\mathcal{V}^{B_{\mathbf{\varepsilon}}}(x):=\{v\in\mathcal{V}:-\frac{\varepsilon}{B_{3}}+\frac{2k\pi}{B_{3}}\leq t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{B_{3}}+\frac{2k\pi}{B_{3}}\ \text{with}\ k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}\}.\end{split}

In the rest of the paper, we denote

\mathcal{V}^{B_{\varepsilon}}_{i}(x^{i}):=\{v^{i}\in\mathcal{V}^{B_{\mathbf{\varepsilon}}}(x^{i})\subset\mathcal{V}_{i}\}
and
\mathcal{V}^{G_{\varepsilon}}_{i}(x^{i}):=\{v^{i}\in\mathcal{V}^{G_{\mathbf{\varepsilon}}}(x^{i})\subset\mathcal{V}_{i}\}
.

Lemma 11
.

For
0\leq\varepsilon\ll 1
and
(X,V)
in (
1.2
), we have

\int_{\mathcal{V}^{B_{\varepsilon}}}\mathrm{d}\sigma\lesssim\varepsilon.

(2.58)

Proof.

Since
|n(x)\cdot v|=|v_{3}|=5t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)
for
(x,v)\in\gamma_{+}
and
0\leq\varepsilon\ll 1
, we get

\begin{split}&\ \ \int_{v\in\mathcal{V}^{B_{\mathbf{\varepsilon}}}(x)}\mu_{\Theta}(x_{\mathbf{b}},v)|n(x)\cdot v|\mathrm{d}v\\
&\lesssim\int_{v\in\mathcal{V}^{B_{\mathbf{\varepsilon}}}(x)}e^{-\frac{|v_{3}|^{2}}{2b}}|v_{3}|\mathrm{d}v_{3}=\int_{v\in\mathcal{V}^{B_{\mathbf{\varepsilon}}}(x)}e^{-\frac{25t_{\mathbf{b}}^{2}}{2b}}\times 25t_{\mathbf{b}}\mathrm{d}t_{\mathbf{b}}\\
&\lesssim-e^{-t_{\mathbf{b}}^{2}/b}\Bigg|^{\frac{\varepsilon}{B_{3}}}_{0}+\sum^{\infty}_{k=1}\int_{-\frac{\varepsilon}{B_{3}}\leq t_{\mathbf{b}}-\frac{2k\pi}{B_{3}}\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{B_{3}}}e^{-t_{\mathbf{b}}^{2}/b}\mathrm{d}t_{\mathbf{b}}\\
&\lesssim\varepsilon^{2}+\varepsilon\times\sum^{\infty}_{k=1}e^{-(\frac{2k\pi}{B_{3}})^{2}/b}\lesssim\varepsilon,\end{split}

where the last line follows from the Taylor expansion.
Therefore, we conclude (
2.58
).
∎

Lemma 12
.

Consider
(X,V)
in (
1.2
) with
0\leq\varepsilon\ll 1
and
x^{i+1}\in\partial\Omega
, for
v^{m,n}_{i}\in\mathcal{V}^{G_{\mathbf{\varepsilon}}}(x^{i+1})
,
