\sum\limits_{m,n\in\mathbb{Z}}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{i+1},v^{m,n}_{i,\mathbf{b}})\lesssim e^{-25(t^{i}_{\mathbf{b}})^{2}/2b}.

(2.59)

For
v^{m,n}_{i}\in\mathcal{V}^{B_{\mathbf{\varepsilon}}}(x^{i+1})
,

\sum\limits_{m,n\in\mathbb{Z}}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{i+1},v^{m,n}_{i,\mathbf{b}})\lesssim\sum\limits_{|m|<2,|n|<2}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{i+1},v^{m,n}_{i,\mathbf{b}})+e^{-\frac{B^{2}_{3}}{b(1-\cos(B_{3}t^{i}_{\mathbf{b}}))}}e^{-25(t^{i}_{\mathbf{b}})^{2}/2b},

(2.60)

where
v^{m,n}_{i,\mathbf{b}}=v_{\mathbf{b}}(x^{i+1},v^{m,n}_{i})
, which was defined in (
0.15
) and (
1.38
).

Proof.

Here, for the sake of simplicity, we have abused the notations temporarily:

x^{i}=(x^{i}_{1},x^{i}_{2},x^{i}_{3})=(x^{i}_{\parallel},x^{i}_{3}),\ v^{m,n}_{i,\mathbf{b}}=(v^{m,n}_{i,\mathbf{b}_{1}},v^{m,n}_{i,\mathbf{b}_{2}},v^{m,n}_{i,\mathbf{b}_{3}})=(v^{m,n}_{i,\mathbf{b}_{\parallel}},v^{m,n}_{i,\mathbf{b}_{3}}).

Note that from (
1.33
), we know
|v^{m,n}_{i,\mathbf{b}_{3}}|^{2}=25(t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i})^{2}
.
From (
1.44
) and (
1.45
), we have

\begin{split}&\ \ \ \ B^{2}_{3}|x_{\mathbf{b},1}-x_{1}|^{2}+B^{2}_{3}|x_{\mathbf{b},2}-x_{2}|^{2}\\
&=(v_{2}-v_{2}\cos(B_{3}t_{\mathbf{b}})-v_{1}\sin(B_{3}t_{\mathbf{b}}))^{2}+(-v_{1}+v_{1}\cos(B_{3}t_{\mathbf{b}})-v_{2}\sin(B_{3}t_{\mathbf{b}}))^{2}\\
&=(v^{2}_{1}+v^{2}_{2})\times(2-2\cos(B_{3}t_{\mathbf{b}})).\end{split}

Using (
1.37
) and the above equality, we obtain

\big|v^{m,n}_{i,\mathbf{b}_{\parallel}}\big|^{2}=|v^{m,n}_{i,\mathbf{b}_{1}}|^{2}+|v^{m,n}_{i,\mathbf{b}_{2}}|^{2}=|v^{m,n}_{i_{1}}|^{2}+|v^{m,n}_{i_{2}}|^{2}=\frac{B^{2}_{3}\big(|x^{i+1}_{1}-x^{i}_{1}+m|^{2}+|x^{i+1}_{2}-x^{i}_{2}+n|^{2}\big)}{2-2\cos(B_{3}t^{i}_{\mathbf{b}})}.

(2.61)

Now we split the length of
t^{i}_{\mathbf{b}}
into two cases:

Case 1:

v^{m,n}_{i}\in\mathcal{V}^{G_{\mathbf{\varepsilon}}}(x^{i+1})
.
From (
2.61
), for
|m|\geq 2
and
|n|\geq 2
, we bound

\big|v^{m,n}_{i,\mathbf{b}_{\parallel}}\big|^{2}\gtrsim\frac{B^{2}_{3}}{4}(m^{2}+n^{2})

(2.62)

For
|m|<2
, we bound
|v^{m,n}_{i-2,\mathbf{b}_{1}}|\geq 0
, and for
|n|<2
, we bound
|v^{m,n}_{i-2,\mathbf{b}_{2}}|\geq 0
.
To derive (
2.59
), we divide
\{v^{m,n}_{i,\mathbf{b}}\}_{m,n\in\mathbb{Z}}
into four parts.

For
|m|<2
and
|n|<2
, we collect and bound all terms as

\sum\limits_{|m|<2,|n|<2}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{i+1},v^{m,n}_{i,\mathbf{b}})\lesssim e^{-25(t^{i}_{\mathbf{b}})^{2}/2b}.

(2.63)

For
|m|<2
and
|n|\geq 2
, we bound

|v^{m,n}_{i-2,\mathbf{b}_{2}}|\gtrsim\frac{B_{3}}{2}|n|
. Thus, we have

\begin{split}\sum\limits_{|m|<2,|n|\geq 2}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{i+1},v^{m,n}_{i,\mathbf{b}})&\lesssim\sum\limits_{|m|<2}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{i+1},\frac{B_{3}}{2}|n|+5t^{i}_{\mathbf{b}})\\
&\lesssim e^{-25(t^{i}_{\mathbf{b}})^{2}/2b}\sum\limits^{\infty}_{n=0}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{i+1},\frac{B_{3}}{2}|n|)\lesssim e^{-25(t^{i}_{\mathbf{b}})^{2}/2b},\end{split}

(2.64)

where the last inequality holds from
\sum\limits^{\infty}_{n=0}\frac{1}{n^{2}}\leq\infty
. Similarly, for
|m|\geq 2
and
|n|<2
case, we bound

|v^{m,n}_{i-2,\mathbf{b}_{1}}|\gtrsim\frac{B_{3}}{2}|m|
, and get

\sum\limits_{|m|\geq 2,|n|<2}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{i-1},v^{m,n}_{i-2,\mathbf{b}})\lesssim e^{-25(t^{i}_{\mathbf{b}})^{2}/2b}.

(2.65)

For
|m|\geq 2
and
|n|\geq 2
, from (
2.62
) and
\sum\limits_{m,n}\frac{1}{m^{2}+n^{2}}\leq\infty
, we obtain

\sum\limits_{|m|\geq 2,|n|\geq 2}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{i-1},v^{m,n}_{i-2,\mathbf{b}})\lesssim e^{-25(t^{i}_{\mathbf{b}})^{2}/2b}.

(2.66)

Using (
2.63
), (
2.64
), (
2.65
) and (
2.66
), we conclude (
2.59
).

Case 2:

v^{m,n}_{i}\in\mathcal{V}^{B_{\mathbf{\varepsilon}}}(x^{i+1})
.
Since in this case
t^{i}_{\mathbf{b}}
is small, we bound

|v^{m,n}_{i-2,\mathbf{b}_{3}}|\geq 0
.
Again, for
|m|<2
, we bound
|v^{m,n}_{i-2,\mathbf{b}_{1}}|\geq 0
, and for
|n|<2
, we bound
|v^{m,n}_{i,\mathbf{b}_{2}}|\geq 0
.
To obtain (
2.60
), again we divide
\{v^{m,n}_{i,\mathbf{b}}\}_{m,n\in\mathbb{Z}}
into four parts.

For
|m|<2
and
|n|<2
, we collect and keep all terms

\sum\limits_{|m|<2,|n|<2}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{i+1},v^{m,n}_{i,\mathbf{b}}).

(2.67)

For
|m|<2
and
|n|\geq 2
, we bound

|v^{m,n}_{i,\mathbf{b}_{2}}|^{2}\geq\frac{B^{2}_{3}n^{2}}{2-2\cos(B_{3}t^{i}_{\mathbf{b}})}
, and have

\begin{split}\sum\limits_{|m|<2,|n|\geq 2}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{i+1},v^{m,n}_{i,\mathbf{b}})\lesssim e^{-25(t^{i}_{\mathbf{b}})^{2}/2b}\sum\limits^{\infty}_{n=2}e^{-\frac{B^{2}_{3}n^{2}}{4b(1-\cos(B_{3}t^{i}_{\mathbf{b}}))}}\lesssim e^{-\frac{B^{2}_{3}}{b(1-\cos(B_{3}t^{i}_{\mathbf{b}}))}}e^{-25(t^{i}_{\mathbf{b}})^{2}/2b},\end{split}

(2.68)

For
|m|\geq 2
and
|n|<2
, we bound

|v^{m,n}_{i,\mathbf{b}_{1}}|^{2}\geq\frac{B^{2}_{3}m^{2}}{2-2\cos(B_{3}t^{i}_{\mathbf{b}})}
, and get

\begin{split}\sum\limits_{|m|\geq 2,|n|<2}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{i+1},v^{m,n}_{i,\mathbf{b}})&\lesssim e^{-25(t^{i}_{\mathbf{b}})^{2}/2b}\sum\limits^{\infty}_{m=2}e^{-\frac{B^{2}_{3}m^{2}}{4b(1-\cos(B_{3}t^{i}_{\mathbf{b}}))}}\\
&\lesssim e^{-\frac{B^{2}_{3}}{b(1-\cos(B_{3}t^{i}_{\mathbf{b}}))}}e^{-25(t^{i}_{\mathbf{b}})^{2}/2b},\end{split}

(2.69)

For
|m|\geq 2
and
|n|\geq 2
, we bound

\big|v^{m,n}_{i,\mathbf{b}_{\parallel}}\big|^{2}\geq\frac{B^{2}_{3}(m^{2}+n^{2})}{2-2\cos(B_{3}t^{i}_{\mathbf{b}})}
, and derive

\begin{split}\sum\limits_{|m|\geq 2,|n|\geq 2}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{i+1},v^{m,n}_{i,\mathbf{b}})&\lesssim\sum\limits^{\infty}_{m,n=2}e^{-\frac{B^{2}_{3}(m^{2}+n^{2})}{4b(1-\cos(B_{3}t^{i}_{\mathbf{b}}))}}\\
&\lesssim e^{-\frac{2B^{2}_{3}}{b(1-\cos(B_{3}t^{i}_{\mathbf{b}}))}}e^{-25(t^{i}_{\mathbf{b}})^{2}/2b}.\end{split}

(2.70)

From (
2.67
), (
2.68
), (
2.69
) and (
2.70
), we conclude (
2.60
).
∎

Now we are ready to control (
2.53
) via the following lemma:

Lemma 13
.

For
i=2,\cdots,k-1
,
0\leq\varepsilon\ll 1
and
(X,V)
in (
1.2
), we have

\Big|\int_{\prod_{j=0}^{i}\mathcal{V}_{j}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{i+1}<0\leq t^{i}}F(X(0;t^{i},x^{i},v^{i}),V(0;t^{i},x^{i},v^{i}))\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Sigma}_{i}\Big|\lesssim 2^{i}\varepsilon^{-2}\|F(x,v)\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}+\varepsilon^{i-1}\|\mathfrak{J}(x)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}.

(2.71)

where

\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Sigma}_{i}:=\frac{\mathrm{d}\sigma_{i}}{\mu_{\Theta}(x^{i+1},v^{i})}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{i-1}\cdots\mathrm{d}\sigma_{1}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{0}
with
\mathrm{d}\sigma_{j}=\mu_{\Theta}(x^{j+1},v^{j})\{n(x^{j})\cdot v^{j}\}\mathrm{d}v^{j}
in (
0.16
).

Proof.

For (
2.71
), it suffices to prove this upper bound for
i=2,...,k-1
,

\int_{\mathcal{V}_{0}}\cdots\int_{\mathcal{V}_{i-1}}\int_{\mathcal{V}_{i}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{i+1}<0\leq t^{i}}|F(X(0;t^{i},x^{i},v^{i}),V(0;t^{i},x^{i},v^{i}))|\{n(x^{i})\cdot v^{i}\}\mathrm{d}v^{i}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{i-1}\cdots\mathrm{d}\sigma_{0}.

(2.72)

Step 1.
Recall from Definition
3
, we obtain for
j=0,\cdots,i-1
,

v^{j}\in\mathcal{V}^{B_{\varepsilon}}_{j},\ \text{or}\ v^{j}\in\mathcal{V}^{G_{\varepsilon}}_{j}.

Now we split
\{v^{j}\}^{i-1}_{j=0}
into two cases:

Case 1:
For all
0\leq j\leq i-2
,
v^{j}\in\mathcal{V}^{B_{\varepsilon}}_{j}
.
From Lemma
11
, we derive that

\begin{split}\eqref{forcing mag}&=\int_{\mathcal{V}^{B_{\varepsilon}}_{0}}\cdots\int_{\mathcal{V}^{B_{\varepsilon}}_{i-2}}\int_{\mathcal{V}_{i-1}}\int_{\mathcal{V}_{i}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{i+1}<0\leq t^{i}}|F(x^{i+1},v_{\mathbf{b}}^{i})|\{n(x^{i})\cdot v^{i}\}\mathrm{d}v^{i}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{i-1}\cdots\mathrm{d}\sigma_{0}\\
&\lesssim\int_{\mathcal{V}^{B_{\varepsilon}}_{0}}\cdots\int_{\mathcal{V}^{B_{\varepsilon}}_{i-2}}\int_{\mathcal{V}_{i}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{i+1}<0\leq t^{i}}\ \mu_{\Theta}(x^{i+1},v^{i})\mathfrak{J}(x^{i+1})\{n(x^{i})\cdot v^{i}\}\mathrm{d}v^{i}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{i-2}\cdots\mathrm{d}\sigma_{0}\\
&\lesssim\|\mathfrak{J}(x)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\int_{\mathcal{V}^{B_{\varepsilon}}_{0}}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{0}\cdots\int_{\mathcal{V}^{B_{\varepsilon}}_{i-2}}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{i-2}\lesssim\|\mathfrak{J}(x)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\times\varepsilon^{i-1}.\end{split}

Case 2:
There exists
0\leq j\leq i-2
such that
v^{j}\in\mathcal{V}^{G_{\varepsilon}}_{j}
.
Using Proposition
4
, we get

(
2.72
)

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{\mathcal{V}_{0}}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{0}\cdots\int_{\mathcal{V}_{j-1}}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{j-1}\times\int_{v^{j}\in\mathcal{V}^{G_{\varepsilon}}_{j}}\frac{5B^{2}_{3}\sum\limits_{m,n\in\mathbb{Z}}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{j+1},v^{m,n}_{j,\mathbf{b}})}{2-2\cos(B_{3}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{j})}\{n(x^{j})\cdot v^{j}\}\mathrm{d}S_{x^{j+1}}\mathrm{d}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{j}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \times\cdots\times\int_{0}^{t^{i-2}}\int_{\partial\Omega}\frac{5B^{2}_{3}\sum\limits_{m,n\in\mathbb{Z}}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{i-1},v^{m,n}_{i-2,\mathbf{b}})}{2-2\cos(B_{3}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2})}\{n(x^{i-2})\cdot v^{i-2}\}\mathrm{d}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}\mathrm{d}S_{x^{i-1}}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \times\int^{t^{i-1}}_{0}\int_{\partial\Omega}\frac{5B^{2}_{3}\sum\limits_{m,n\in\mathbb{Z}}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{i},v^{m,n}_{i-1,\mathbf{b}})}{2-2\cos(B_{3}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1})}\{n(x^{i-1})\cdot v^{i-1}\}\mathrm{d}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}\mathrm{d}S_{x^{i}}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \times\int_{\mathcal{V}_{i}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{i+1}<0\leq t^{i}}\ |F(X(0;t^{i},x^{i},v^{i}),V(0;t^{i},x^{i},v^{i}))|\{n(x^{i})\cdot v^{i}\}\mathrm{d}v^{i}.

(2.73)

Using the Tonelli’s theorem, we deduce that

\begin{split}\eqref{forcing mag}\lesssim&\int_{\mathcal{V}_{0}}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{0}\cdots\int_{\mathcal{V}_{j-1}}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{j-1}\times\int_{v^{j}\in\mathcal{V}^{G_{\varepsilon}}_{j}}\mathrm{d}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{j}\int_{\partial\Omega}\mathrm{d}S_{x^{i}}\\
&\times\underbrace{\int_{0}^{t^{j+1}}\int_{\partial\Omega}\frac{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{j+1}\sum\limits_{m,n\in\mathbb{Z}}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{j+2},v^{m,n}_{j+1,\mathbf{b}})}{2-2\cos(B_{3}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{j+1})}\times\frac{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{j}\sum\limits_{m,n\in\mathbb{Z}}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{j+1},v^{m,n}_{j,\mathbf{b}})}{2-2\cos(B_{3}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{j})}\mathrm{d}S_{x^{j+1}}\mathrm{d}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{j+1}}_{\eqref{est2:forcing mag}^{*}}\\
&\times\int_{0}^{t^{j+2}}\int_{\partial\Omega}\frac{5B^{2}_{3}\sum\limits_{m,n\in\mathbb{Z}}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{j+3},v^{m,n}_{j+2,\mathbf{b}})}{2-2\cos(B_{3}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{j+2})}\{n(x^{j+2})\cdot v^{j+2}\}\mathrm{d}S_{x^{j+2}}\mathrm{d}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{j+2}\\
&\times\cdots\times\int_{0}^{t^{i-2}}\int_{\partial\Omega}\frac{5B^{2}_{3}\sum\limits_{m,n\in\mathbb{Z}}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{i-1},v^{m,n}_{i-2,\mathbf{b}})}{2-2\cos(B_{3}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2})}\{n(x^{i-2})\cdot v^{i-2}\}\mathrm{d}S_{x^{i-2}}\mathrm{d}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}\\
&\times\underbrace{\int^{t^{i-1}}_{0}\int_{\partial\Omega}\frac{5B^{2}_{3}\sum\limits_{m,n\in\mathbb{Z}}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{i},v^{m,n}_{i-1,\mathbf{b}})}{2-2\cos(B_{3}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1})}\{n(x^{i-1})\cdot v^{i-1}\}\mathrm{d}S_{x^{i-1}}\mathrm{d}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}}_{\eqref{est2:forcing mag}^{**}}\times\eqref{est1:forcing mag}.\end{split}

(2.74)

Step 2.
First we claim that

\begin{split}\eqref{est2:forcing mag}^{**}&=\int^{t^{i-1}}_{0}\int_{\partial\Omega}\frac{5B^{2}_{3}\sum\limits_{m,n\in\mathbb{Z}}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{i},v^{m,n}_{i-1,\mathbf{b}})}{2-2\cos(B_{3}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1})}\{n(x^{i-1})\cdot v^{i-1}\}\mathrm{d}S_{x^{i-1}}\mathrm{d}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}\leq 1.\end{split}

(2.75)

From Lemma
5
, we deduce

\begin{split}\eqref{est2:forcing mag}^{**}&=\int^{t^{i-1}}_{0}\int_{\partial\Omega}\frac{5B^{2}_{3}\sum\limits_{m,n\in\mathbb{Z}}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{i},v^{m,n}_{i-1,\mathbf{b}})}{2-2\cos(B_{3}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1})}\times|n(x^{i})\cdot v^{m,n}_{i-1,\mathbf{b}}|\mathrm{d}S_{x^{i-1}}\mathrm{d}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}.\end{split}

(2.76)

Using Proposition
4
and the fact
t_{\mathbf{f}}(x^{i},v^{m,n}_{i-1,\mathbf{b}})=t_{\mathbf{b}}(x^{i-1},v^{m,n}_{i-1})
, we get

\begin{split}\eqref{bound3_6_1 mag}&\leq\int^{\infty}_{0}\int_{\partial\Omega}\frac{5B^{2}_{3}\sum\limits_{m,n\in\mathbb{Z}}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{i},v^{m,n}_{i-1,\mathbf{b}})}{2-2\cos(B_{3}t_{\mathbf{f}}^{i})}|n(x^{i})\cdot v^{m,n}_{i-1,\mathbf{b}}|\mathrm{d}S_{x^{i-1}}\mathrm{d}t_{\mathbf{f}}^{i}\\
&=\int_{n(x^{i})\cdot v<0}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{i},v)|n(x^{i})\cdot v|\mathrm{d}v=1.\end{split}

(2.77)

Step 3.
Next we claim

\eqref{est2:forcing mag}_{*}=\int_{0}^{t^{j+1}}\int_{\partial\Omega}\frac{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{j+1}\sum\limits_{m,n\in\mathbb{Z}}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{j+2},v^{m,n}_{j+1,\mathbf{b}})}{2-2\cos(B_{3}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{j+1})}\times\frac{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{j}\sum\limits_{m,n\in\mathbb{Z}}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{j+1},v^{m,n}_{j,\mathbf{b}})}{2-2\cos(B_{3}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{j})}\mathrm{d}S_{x^{j+1}}\mathrm{d}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{j+1}\lesssim\varepsilon^{-2}.

(2.78)

From the setting
v^{j}\in\mathcal{V}^{G_{\varepsilon}}_{j}
with
\frac{\varepsilon}{B_{3}}+\frac{2k\pi}{B_{3}}\leq t_{\mathbf{b}}^{j}(x,v)\leq\frac{2\pi-\varepsilon}{B_{3}}+\frac{2k\pi}{B_{3}}
, we derive that

\frac{1}{2-2\cos(B_{3}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{j})}\lesssim\varepsilon^{-2}.

Then using Lemma
12
and
te^{-t^{2}}\lesssim 1
, we bound

\begin{split}\frac{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{j}\sum\limits_{m,n\in\mathbb{Z}}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{j+1},v^{m,n}_{j,\mathbf{b}})}{2-2\cos(B_{3}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{j})}\lesssim t_{\mathbf{b}}^{j}e^{-(t_{\mathbf{b}}^{j})^{2}}\varepsilon^{-2}\lesssim\varepsilon^{-2}.\end{split}

(2.79)

Applying (
2.79
) on
\eqref{est2:forcing mag}_{*}
, we derive that

\eqref{est2:forcing mag}_{*}\lesssim\varepsilon^{-2}\int_{0}^{t^{j+1}}\int_{\partial\Omega}\frac{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{j+1}\sum\limits_{m,n\in\mathbb{Z}}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{j+2},v^{m,n}_{j+1,\mathbf{b}})}{2-2\cos(B_{3}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{j+1})}\mathrm{d}S_{x^{j+1}}\mathrm{d}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{j+1}\lesssim\varepsilon^{-2},

(2.80)

where the last inequality holds from
(
2.75
), and we prove (
2.78
).

Step 4.

Now applying (
2.75
) on
\{v^{k}\}^{i-1}_{k=j+2}
and (
2.78
), we derive that

\begin{split}\eqref{est2:forcing mag}\lesssim&\int_{\mathcal{V}_{0}}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{0}\cdots\int_{\mathcal{V}_{j-1}}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{j-1}\times\varepsilon^{-2}\times\int_{v^{j}\in\mathcal{V}^{G_{\varepsilon}}_{j}}\mathrm{d}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{j}\int_{\partial\Omega}\mathrm{d}S_{x^{i}}\eqref{est1:forcing mag}.\end{split}

(2.81)

For (
2.81
), we employ the change of variables

(x^{i},t_{\mathbf{b}}^{j},v^{i})\mapsto(y,w)=(X(0;t^{j}-t_{\mathbf{b}}^{j}\cdots-t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1},x^{i},v^{i}),V(0;t^{j}-t_{\mathbf{b}}^{j}\cdots-t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1},x^{i},v^{i}))\in\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}.

(2.82)

From (
1.3
), we derive

|n(x^{i})\cdot v^{i}|\mathrm{d}S_{x^{i}}\mathrm{d}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{j}\mathrm{d}v^{i}\lesssim\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}w
.
Thus, we bound (
2.81
) as

\begin{split}\eqref{est_1:forcing mag}\leq\int_{\mathcal{V}_{0}}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{0}\cdots\int_{\mathcal{V}_{j-1}}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{j-1}\times\varepsilon^{-2}\times\iint_{\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}|F(y,w)|\mathrm{d}w\mathrm{d}y\lesssim 2^{j}\varepsilon^{-2}\times\|F(x,v)\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}.\end{split}

Applying the above on each situation when
j=0,\cdots,i-2
in second cases, we deduce

\eqref{forcing mag}\lesssim\sum\limits^{i-2}_{j=0}2^{j}\varepsilon^{-2}\times\|F(x,v)\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}\leq 2^{i}\varepsilon^{-2}\|F(x,v)\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}.

Collecting the estimates from two cases, we prove (
2.71
).
∎

Following Lemma
9
and Remark
2
, we control the last term (
2.54
).

Lemma 14
.

Consider
(X,V)
in (
1.2
), there exists
\mathfrak{C}=\mathfrak{C}(\Omega)>0
(see (
2.46
) for the precise choice), such that

\text{if }\ k\geq\mathfrak{C}t,\text{ then }\sup_{(x,v)\in\bar{\Omega}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Big(\int_{\prod_{j=0}^{k-1}\mathcal{V}_{j}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{k}(t,x,v,v^{1},\cdots,v^{k-1})\geq 0}\ \mathrm{d}\sigma_{0}\cdots\mathrm{d}\sigma_{k-1}\Big)\lesssim e^{-t},

(2.83)

where
\mathrm{d}\sigma_{j}=\mu_{\Theta}(x^{j+1},v^{j})\{n(x^{j})\cdot v^{j}\}\mathrm{d}v^{j}
in (
0.16
).

Proof.

For the magnet field case, from (
2.42
) and
|n(x)\cdot v|=5t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)
for
(x,v)\in\gamma_{+}
, we have

\begin{split}&\ \ \ \ \int_{n(x)\cdot v>0}\mathbf{1}_{\delta>t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)}\mu_{\Theta}(x_{\mathbf{b}},v)|n(x)\cdot v|\mathrm{d}v\leq\int_{|n(x)\cdot v|\leq 5\delta}\mu_{\Theta}(x_{\mathbf{b}},v)|n(x)\cdot v|\mathrm{d}v\lesssim\delta^{2}.\end{split}

The rest of proof follows from Lemma
9
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
5
of magnetic case (
0.6
)
.

Using (
2.55
), (
2.56
), Lemma
13
and
14
, we have

\begin{split}\mathfrak{J}(x)&\leq\|\mathfrak{J}(x)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\times e^{-\frac{t^{2}}{2b}}+\|\mathfrak{J}(x)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\times e^{-\frac{t^{2}}{8b}}\\
&\ \ \ \ +\sum^{k-1}_{i=2}\big(2^{i}\varepsilon^{-2}\|F(x,v)\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}+\varepsilon^{i-1}\|\mathfrak{J}(x)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\big)+\|\mathfrak{J}(x)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\times e^{-t}\\
&\leq\big(e^{-\frac{t^{2}}{2b}}+e^{-\frac{t^{2}}{8b}}+e^{-t}+2\varepsilon\big)\times\|\mathfrak{J}(x)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}+2^{k}\varepsilon^{-2}\times\|F(x,v)\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}.\end{split}

We can pick sufficiently large but fixed
t
and sufficiently small
\varepsilon
such that

e^{-\frac{t^{2}}{2b}}+e^{-\frac{t^{2}}{8b}}+e^{-t}+2\varepsilon\leq 1/2.

(2.84)

Then we derive

(1-e^{-\frac{t^{2}}{2b}}-e^{-\frac{t^{2}}{8b}}-e^{-t}-2\varepsilon)\|\mathfrak{J}(x)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq 2^{k}\varepsilon^{-2}\times\|F(x,v)\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}.

Choosing
k=\mathfrak{a}\Big(\big[\frac{t}{C_{\Omega}\delta}\big]+1\Big)
and
t
in (
2.84
), we conclude that

\|\mathfrak{J}(x)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim 2^{k+1}\times\|F(x,v)\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}.

(2.85)

Moreover,
F(x,v)=F(x_{\mathbf{b}},v_{\mathbf{b}})=\mu_{\Theta}(x_{\mathbf{b}},v_{\mathbf{b}})\mathfrak{J}(x_{\mathbf{b}})
, the
L^{\infty}_{x}
-estimate in (
2.85
) implies

\|\mu^{-1}_{\Theta}(x_{\mathbf{b}},v_{\mathbf{b}})F(x,v)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}=\|\mathfrak{J}(x)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim 2^{k+1}\times\|F(x,v)\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}.

From Lemma
5

and (
0.4
), we get

\mu^{-1}_{\Theta}(x_{\mathbf{b}},v_{\mathbf{b}})=2\pi\Theta^{2}(x_{\mathbf{b}})e^{\frac{|v_{\mathbf{b}}|^{2}}{2\Theta(x_{\mathbf{b}})}}\gtrsim e^{\frac{|v|^{2}/2+\Phi(x)}{2b}}.

(2.86)

Therefore, we prove the proposition.
∎

3.
The existence of the steady state

In this section, we show the existence of steady state under the non-isothermal diffusive boundary condition.
We first use sequential argument on the penalized transport equation, then apply
L^{1}
-
L^{\infty}
bootstrap to derive the solution.
The main purpose of this section is to prove Proposition
6
, Theorem
1
.

Define

\mu(v):=\frac{1}{2\pi}e^{-\frac{|v|^{2}}{2}}

(3.1)

Now we extend this Maxwellian into the whole phase space, such that for
(x,v)\in\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
,

\mu(x,v):=\frac{1}{2\pi}e^{-\frac{|v|^{2}}{2}-\Phi(x)},

(3.2)

where
\mu
satisfies (
0.7
)
(resp. (
0.10
)). Moreover, we set
\|\mu\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}:=\mathfrak{m}_{\mu}>0
.

Proposition 6
.

Recall

\mu_{\Theta}(x,v)=\frac{1}{2\pi\Theta^{2}(x)}e^{-\frac{|v|^{2}}{2\Theta(x)}}
for
(x,v)\in\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
and
0<a\leq\Theta(x)\leq b<\infty
with
b^{2}=2a^{2}
for all
x\in\Omega
. Now we define the following:

r(x,v)=\mu_{\Theta}(x,v)-\mu(x,v),

where
(x,v)\in\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
, with

\int_{\gamma_{-}}r(x,v)|n\cdot v|\mathrm{d}S_{x}\mathrm{d}v=0.

Then there exists a unique solution
f(t,x,v)
to
(
0.7
) (resp. (
0.10
)) and the boundary condition:

f(x,v)=\mu_{\Theta}(x,v)\int_{n(x)\cdot v^{1}>0}f(x,v^{1})\{n(x)\cdot v^{1}\}\mathrm{d}v^{1}+r(x,v),\ \ \text{for}\ (x,v)\in\gamma_{-},

(3.3)

such that

\iint_{\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f(x,v)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}v=0.

(3.4)

For the proof of Proposition
16
, we start with the penalized transport equation with a reduced diffuse reflection boundary condition, with the purpose of setting up a contracting map argument.

Lemma 15
.

For any
\epsilon>0
and for any integer
j>0
, there exists a unique solution
f(t,x,v)
to

\epsilon f^{j}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}f^{j}+(v\times B-\nabla\Phi)\cdot\nabla_{v}f^{j}=0,

(3.5)

f^{j}(x,v)=(1-\frac{1}{j})\mu_{\Theta}(x,v)\int_{n(x)\cdot v^{1}>0}f^{j}(x,v^{1})\{n(x)\cdot v^{1}\}\mathrm{d}v^{1}+r(x,v),\ \ \text{for}\ (x,v)\in\gamma_{-},

(3.6)

where
B
and
\Phi(x)
are defined in (
0.5
) (resp. (
0.6
)).

Moreover, uniformly in
j
, we have

\|f^{j}\|_{1}\lesssim_{\epsilon,\Omega}|r|_{1}

(3.7)

Finally the limit
f^{\epsilon}
as
j\rightarrow\infty
of the sequence
\{f^{j}\}
exists and solves uniquely

\epsilon f^{\epsilon}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}f^{\epsilon}+(v\times B-\nabla\Phi)\cdot\nabla_{v}f^{\epsilon}=0,

(3.8)

f^{\epsilon}(x,v)=\mu_{\Theta}(x,v)\int_{n(x)\cdot v^{1}>0}f^{\epsilon}(x,v^{1})\{n(x)\cdot v^{1}\}\mathrm{d}v^{1}+r(x,v),\ \ \text{for}\ (x,v)\in\gamma_{-}.

(3.9)

where
B
and
\Phi(x)
are defined in (
0.5
) (resp. (
0.6
)).

Proof.

For any
\varepsilon
, we first consider
B
,
\Phi(x)
defined in (
0.5
), and use the following double iteration in both
j
and
l
,

\epsilon f^{l+1}_{j}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}f^{l+1}_{j}-\nabla\Phi\cdot\nabla_{v}f^{l+1}_{j}=0,

(3.10)

f^{l+1}_{j}(x,v)=(1-\frac{1}{j})\mu_{\Theta}(x,v)\int_{n(x)\cdot v^{1}>0}f^{l}_{j}(x,v^{1})\{n(x)\cdot v^{1}\}\mathrm{d}v^{1}+r(x,v),\ \ \text{for}\ (x,v)\in\gamma_{-}.

(3.11)

with initial setting
f^{0}_{j}=0
.

Step 1.

We first fix
j
and take
l\rightarrow\infty
.
Here we claim that
\{f^{l}_{j}\}_{l}
is a Cauchy sequence in
L^{1}
.
Taking the difference of
f^{l+1}_{j}-f^{l}_{j}
, and using Green’s identity on (
3.10
) and (
3.11
), we have

\begin{split}\epsilon\|f^{l+1}_{j}-f^{l}_{j}\|_{1}+|f^{l+1}_{j}-f^{l}_{j}|_{1,+}=|f^{l+1}_{j}-f^{l}_{j}|_{1,-}=|(1-\frac{1}{j})P_{\Theta}(f^{l}_{j}-f^{l-1}_{j})|_{1,-},\end{split}

where
P_{\Theta}f^{l}_{j}:=\mu_{\Theta}(x,v)\int_{n(x)\cdot v^{1}>0}f^{l}_{j}(x,v^{1})\{n(x)\cdot v^{1}\}\mathrm{d}v^{1}
with
\mu_{\Theta}(x,v)=\frac{1}{2\pi\Theta^{2}(x)}e^{-\frac{|v|^{2}}{2\Theta(x)}}
.
By direct computation and
|P_{\Theta}f^{l}_{j}|_{1,-}\leq|f^{l}_{j}|_{1,+}
, we get

\begin{split}|(1-\frac{1}{j})P_{\Theta}(f^{l}_{j}-f^{l-1}_{j})|_{1,-}\leq(1-\frac{1}{j})|f^{l}_{j}-f^{l-1}_{j}|_{1,+}.\end{split}

Thus, we deduce that

\epsilon\|f^{l+1}_{j}-f^{l}_{j}\|_{1}+|f^{l+1}_{j}-f^{l}_{j}|_{1,+}\leq(1-\frac{1}{j})|f^{l}_{j}-f^{l-1}_{j}|_{1,+}.

(3.12)

Since
1-\frac{1}{j}<1
, by iteration over
l
, we deduce

|f^{l+1}_{j}-f^{l}_{j}|_{1,+}\leq(1-\frac{1}{j})^{l}|f^{1}_{j}-f^{0}_{j}|_{1,+}.

Applying this on (
3.12
), we have

\epsilon\|f^{l+1}_{j}-f^{l}_{j}\|_{1}+|f^{l+1}_{j}-f^{l}_{j}|_{1,+}\leq(1-\frac{1}{j})|f^{l}_{j}-f^{l-1}_{j}|_{1,+}\leq(1-\frac{1}{j})^{l}|f^{1}_{j}-f^{0}_{j}|_{1,+}.

Then, for any
l,k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
with
l>k>0
, we derive

\epsilon\|f^{l}_{j}-f^{k}_{j}\|_{1}\leq\epsilon\sum\limits^{l-1}_{p=k}\|f^{p+1}_{j}-f^{p}_{j}\|_{1}\leq\sum\limits^{l-1}_{p=k}(1-\frac{1}{j})^{p}|f^{1}_{j}-f^{0}_{j}|_{1,+}<j(1-\frac{1}{j})^{k}|f^{1}_{j}-f^{0}_{j}|_{1,+}.

Since
f^{0}_{j}=0
and
f^{1}_{j}(x,v)=r(x,v)
for
(x,v)\in\gamma_{-}
, we get
|f^{1}_{j}-f^{0}_{j}|_{1,+}<\infty
. Therefore, we prove the claim.

Now we can take

l\rightarrow\infty
to obtain the
L^{1}
limit such that

f^{l}_{j}\rightarrow f^{j}\in L^{1}
.
Since (
3.10
) is linear and
f^{l}_{j}\rightarrow f^{j}
in
L^{1}
, we can get
f^{j}
is the weak solution for (
3.5
).
Note that on the boundary, we have
|f^{l+1}_{j}-f^{l}_{j}|_{1,+}\leq(1-\frac{1}{j})^{l}|f^{1}_{j}-f^{0}_{j}|_{1,+}
. This shows
f^{l}_{j}
is also a Cauchy sequence in
L^{1}_{\gamma_{+}}
, thus the limit
f^{j}
satisfies (
3.6
).

Suppose
g^{j}
is also the solution for
j>0
, and set
\delta^{j}:=f^{j}-g^{j}
. From Green’s identity, we have,

\epsilon\|\delta^{j}\|_{1}+|\delta^{j}|_{1,+}\leq(1-\frac{1}{j})|P_{\Theta}\delta^{j}|_{1,-}\leq(1-\frac{1}{j})|\delta^{j}|_{1,+}.

Since
\epsilon>0
and
j>0
, we obtain,

\|\delta^{j}\|_{1}\leq 0.

which leads to the uniqueness on the solution to (
3.5
) and (
3.6
).

Step 2.

Now we take
j\rightarrow\infty
for
f^{j}
with
\epsilon>0
. Using Green’s identity and
|P_{\Theta}f^{l}|_{1,-}\leq|f^{l}|_{1,+}
, we obtain that,

\epsilon\|f^{j}\|_{1}+|f^{j}|_{1,+}\leq(1-\frac{1}{j})|f^{j}|_{1,+}+|r|_{1,-}.

(3.13)

Then we derive uniformly in
j
,

\|f^{j}\|_{1}\leq\frac{1}{\epsilon}|r|_{1,-}<\infty,

which shows (
3.7
).
Then following the characteristic line on (
3.5
), we get

\|f^{j}(x,v)\|_{L^{\infty}}=\|e^{-\varepsilon t_{\mathbf{b}}}f^{j}(x_{\mathbf{b}},v_{\mathbf{b}})\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq\|f^{j}(x_{\mathbf{b}},v_{\mathbf{b}})\|_{L^{\infty}}.

Here we define the boundary outgoing flux for
f^{j}(x,v)
, denoted by
\mathfrak{J}^{j}(x)
,

\mathfrak{J}^{j}(x)=\int_{n(x)\cdot v>0}f^{j}(x,v)\{n(x)\cdot v\}\mathrm{d}v,\ \text{for}\ x\in\partial\Omega.

(3.14)

From (
3.6
), we can immediately get

f^{j}(x,v)=(1-\frac{1}{j})\mu_{\Theta}(x,v)\mathfrak{J}^{j}(x)+r(x,v),\ \text{for}\ (x,v)\in\gamma_{-}.

(3.15)

From (
3.5
), (
3.6
) and the stochastic cycle on
f^{j}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathfrak{J}^{j}(x)\leq

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{V}_{0}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{1}<0}\big|f^{j}(X(0;t,x,v^{0}),V(0;t,x,v^{0}))\{n(x)\cdot v^{0}\}\big|\mathrm{d}v^{0}

(3.16)

\displaystyle+\int_{\prod_{j=0}^{1}\mathcal{V}_{j}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{2}<0\leq t^{1}}\Big(\big|f^{j}(X(0;t^{1},x^{1},v^{1}),V(0;t^{1},x^{1},v^{1}))\big|\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Sigma}_{1}+|r(x^{1},v_{\mathbf{b}}^{0})|\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Sigma}_{0}\Big)

(3.17)

\displaystyle+\sum\limits^{k-1}_{i=2}\int_{\prod_{j=0}^{i}\mathcal{V}_{j}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{i+1}<0\leq t^{i}}\Big(\big|f^{j}(X(0;t^{i},x^{i},v^{i}),V(0;t^{i},x^{i},v^{i}))\big|\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Sigma}_{i}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+|r(x^{i},v_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1})|\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Sigma}_{i-1}+|r(x^{i-1},v_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2})|\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Sigma}_{i-2}+\dots+|r(x^{1},v_{\mathbf{b}}^{0})|\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Sigma}_{0}\Big)

(3.18)

\displaystyle+\int_{\prod_{j=0}^{k}\mathcal{V}_{j}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{k}\geq 0}\Big(\big|f^{j}(x^{k},v^{k})\big|\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Sigma}_{k}+|r(x^{k},v_{\mathbf{b}}^{k-1}|)\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Sigma}_{k-1}+\dots+|r(x^{1},v_{\mathbf{b}}^{0})|\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Sigma}_{0}\Big),

(3.19)

where

\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Sigma}_{i}:=\frac{\mathrm{d}\sigma_{i}}{\mu_{\Theta}(x^{i+1},v^{i})}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{i-1}\cdots\mathrm{d}\sigma_{1}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{0}
and

\mathrm{d}\sigma_{j}=\mu_{\Theta}(x^{j+1},v^{j})\{n(x^{j})\cdot v^{j}\}\mathrm{d}v^{j}
in (
0.16
).

From Lemma
3
, we know that
|v_{\mathbf{b}}|=|v|
. Moreover, it is easy to check that
r(x,v)=r(x,|v|)
, thus we deduce that,

\begin{split}\eqref{expand_fj12}&\leq\int_{\prod_{j=0}^{1}\mathcal{V}_{j}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{2}<0\leq t^{1}}\big|f^{j}(X(0;t^{1},x^{1},v^{1}),V(0;t^{1},x^{1},v^{1}))\big|\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Sigma}_{1}+\int_{\mathcal{V}_{0}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{2}<0\leq t^{1}}|r(x^{1},v^{0})|\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Sigma}_{0}.\end{split}

For (
3.18
), we have,

\begin{split}\eqref{expand_fj1}&\leq\int_{\prod_{j=0}^{i}\mathcal{V}_{j}}\sum\limits^{k-1}_{i=2}\Big\{\mathbf{1}_{t^{i+1}<0\leq t^{i}}\big|f^{j}(X(0;t^{i},x^{i},v^{i}),V(0;t^{i},x^{i},v^{i}))\big|\Big\}\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Sigma}_{i}\\
&+\sum\limits^{k-1}_{i=2}\int_{\prod_{j=0}^{i}\mathcal{V}_{j}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{i+1}<0\leq t^{i}}\Big(|r(x^{i},v^{i-1})|\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Sigma}_{i-1}+|r(x^{i-1},v^{i-2})|\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Sigma}_{i-2}+\dots+|r(x^{1},v^{0})|\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Sigma}_{0}\Big).\end{split}

For the last term (
3.19
), we obtain,

\begin{split}\eqref{expand_fj2}\leq\int_{\prod_{j=0}^{k}\mathcal{V}_{j}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{k}\geq 0}\Big(\big|f^{j}(x^{k},v^{k})\big|\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Sigma}_{k}+|r(x^{k},v^{k-1}|)\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Sigma}_{k-1}+\dots+|r(x^{1},v^{0})|\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Sigma}_{0}\Big).\end{split}

By direct computation, we get

\begin{split}\int_{\mathcal{V}_{i-1}}r(x^{i},v^{i-1})\{n(x^{i-1})\cdot v^{i-1}\}\mathrm{d}v^{i-1}&\leq\int_{\mathcal{V}_{i-1}}\big(\mu_{\Theta}(x^{i},v^{i-1})+\mu(x^{i},v^{i-1}))\{n(x^{i-1})\cdot v^{i-1}\}\mathrm{d}v^{i-1}\lesssim 2.\end{split}

Then for any
i=2,\cdots,k
,

\int_{\prod_{j=0}^{i}\mathcal{V}_{j}}r(x^{i},v^{i-1})\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Sigma}_{i-1}\lesssim 2\prod_{j=0}^{i-2}\int_{\mathcal{V}_{i}}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{i}.

(3.20)

Using the above estimate, we derive

\displaystyle\mathfrak{J}^{j}(x)\leq

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{V}_{0}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{1}<0}\big|f^{j}(X(0;t,x,v^{0}),V(0;t,x,v^{0}))\{n(x)\cdot v^{0}\}\big|\mathrm{d}v^{0}

\displaystyle+\int_{\prod_{j=0}^{1}\mathcal{V}_{j}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{2}<0\leq t^{1}}\big|f^{j}(X(0;t^{1},x^{1},v^{1}),V(0;t^{1},x^{1},v^{1}))\big|\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Sigma}_{1}+2

\displaystyle+\int_{\prod_{j=0}^{i}\mathcal{V}_{j}}\sum\limits^{k-1}_{i=2}\Big\{\mathbf{1}_{t^{i+1}<0\leq t^{i}}\big|f^{j}(X(0;t^{i},x^{i},v^{i}),V(0;t^{i},x^{i},v^{i}))\big|\Big\}\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Sigma}_{i}

\displaystyle+\sum\limits^{k-1}_{i=2}\Big(2\prod_{j=0}^{i-2}\int_{\mathcal{V}_{i}}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{i}+2\prod_{j=0}^{i-3}\int_{\mathcal{V}_{i}}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{i}+\dots+2\Big)

\displaystyle+\int_{\prod_{j=0}^{k}\mathcal{V}_{j}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{k}\geq 0}\big|f^{j}(x^{k},v^{k})\big|\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Sigma}_{k}+2\prod_{j=0}^{k-2}\int_{\mathcal{V}_{i}}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{i}+2\prod_{j=0}^{k-3}\int_{\mathcal{V}_{i}}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{i}+\dots+2.

From Remark
2
,
\int_{\mathcal{V}_{i}}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{i}
is uniformly bounded for all
i
and
x\in\Omega
. Now we apply the same idea in Lemma
8
,
9
and Proposition
5
, we can derive

\|\mathfrak{J}^{j}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\|f^{j}\|_{1}+2\leq\frac{1}{\epsilon}|r|_{1}+2.

(3.21)

Using
f^{j}(x,v)=e^{-\epsilon t_{\mathbf{b}}}f^{j}(x_{\mathbf{b}},v_{\mathbf{b}})
,
r(x,v)\in L^{\infty}(\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3})
and (
3.13
), (
3.15
), then for any
(x,v)\in\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
and
j
,

\begin{split}|f^{j}(x,v)|\leq\mu_{\Theta}(x_{\mathbf{b}},v_{\mathbf{b}})\|\mathfrak{J}^{j}\|_{L^{\infty}}+|r(x_{\mathbf{b}},v_{\mathbf{b}})|\lesssim\Big(\frac{1}{\epsilon}|r|_{1}+2\Big)e^{-\frac{1}{2b}(|v|^{2}/2+\Phi(x))},\end{split}

(3.22)

where the last inequality follows from Lemma
3
and (
0.4
).

From the uniform boundness on
\{f^{j}\}
in (
3.22
), then up to a subsequence
\{f^{j_{k}}\}
we obtain the weak
*
limit in
L^{\infty}
such that
f^{j_{k}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\ast}}{{\rightharpoonup}}f^{\epsilon}\in L^{\infty}
.
Since (
3.5
) is linear, we obtain that
f^{j}
is the weak solution for (
3.8
).
Note from (
3.21
), we have uniform
L^{\infty}(\partial\Omega)
bound on
\{\mathfrak{J}^{j}\}
.
Again up to a subsequence, we derive the weak
*
limit in
L^{\infty}(\partial\Omega)
such that
J^{j_{k}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\ast}}{{\rightharpoonup}}J^{\varepsilon}
.
Since
f^{j}(x,v)=(1-\frac{1}{j})\mu_{\Theta}(x_{\mathbf{b}},v_{\mathbf{b}})\mathfrak{J}^{j}(x_{\mathbf{b}},v_{\mathbf{b}})+r(x_{\mathbf{b}},v_{\mathbf{b}})
, we let
f^{\epsilon}(x,v)=\mu_{\Theta}(x_{\mathbf{b}},v_{\mathbf{b}})J^{\varepsilon}(x_{\mathbf{b}},v_{\mathbf{b}})+r(x_{\mathbf{b}},v_{\mathbf{b}})
for
(x,v)\in\gamma_{-}
, and it satisfies (
3.9
).

Step 3.
Moreover, we set
\delta^{j}:=f^{j}-f^{\epsilon}
which solves the following:

\epsilon\delta^{j}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}\delta^{j}-\nabla\Phi\cdot\nabla_{v}\delta^{j}=0,

(3.23)

\delta^{j}(x,v)=(1-\frac{1}{j})P_{\Theta}\delta^{j}-\frac{1}{j}P_{\Theta}f^{\epsilon}.

(3.24)

Again from Green’s identity and (
3.12
), we obtain,

\epsilon\|\delta^{j}\|_{1}+|\delta^{j}|_{1,+}\leq|(1-\frac{1}{j})P_{\Theta}\delta^{j}|_{1,-}+|\frac{1}{j}P_{\Theta}f^{\epsilon}|_{1,-}.

From

|P_{\Theta}f^{\epsilon}|_{1,-}\leq|f^{\epsilon}|_{1,+}
and
f^{\epsilon}\in L^{\infty}
, using (
3.22
), we get that

\epsilon\|\delta^{j}\|_{1}+|\delta^{j}|_{1,+}\leq|\delta^{j}|_{1,+}+\frac{1}{j}(\|f^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|r\|_{L^{\infty}}).

Thus we derive

\|\delta^{j}\|_{1}\leq\frac{1}{\epsilon\times j}(\|f^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}}+2)\rightarrow 0\ \text{as}\ j\rightarrow\infty,

which implies

f^{j}\rightarrow f^{\epsilon}
as
j\rightarrow\infty
in
L^{1}
norm with any
\epsilon>0
.

Suppose
g^{\varepsilon}
is also the solution with the same
\varepsilon>0
, and set
\delta^{\varepsilon}:=f^{\epsilon}-g^{\varepsilon}
. From Green’s identity, we have,

\epsilon\|\delta^{\varepsilon}\|_{1}+|\delta^{\varepsilon}|_{1,+}\leq|P_{\Theta}\delta^{\varepsilon}|_{1,-}\leq|\delta^{\varepsilon}|_{1,+}.

Since
\epsilon>0
, we obtain,

\|\delta^{\varepsilon}\|_{1}\leq 0.

which gives the uniqueness on the solution to (
3.8
) and (
3.9
).

For the magnet field case, we consider
B
,
\Phi(x)
defined in (
0.6
). Similarly, we use the following double iteration in both
j
and
l
,

\epsilon f^{l+1}_{j}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}f^{l+1}_{j}+(v\times B-\nabla\Phi(x))\cdot\nabla_{v}f^{l+1}_{j}=0,

(3.25)

f^{l+1}_{j}(x,v)=(1-\frac{1}{j})\mu_{\Theta}(x,v)\int_{n(x)\cdot v^{1}>0}f^{l}_{j}(x,v^{1})\{n(x)\cdot v^{1}\}\mathrm{d}v^{1}+r(x,v),\ \ \text{for}\ (x,v)\in\gamma_{-}.

(3.26)

with initial setting
f^{0}_{j}=0
.

Since
\nabla_{v}\cdot(v\times B-\nabla\Phi(x))=0
, the magnet field doesn’t influence the integration when we use Green’s identity.
Thus, we still first fix
j
and take
l\rightarrow\infty
, then take
j\rightarrow\infty
to obtain
f^{\epsilon}
.
The rest of proof follows from the gravitational case.
∎

Next Lemma states the uniform in
L^{1}
bound for
f^{\epsilon}
. It will allow us to take the limit as
\epsilon\rightarrow 0
.

Lemma 16
.

For any
\epsilon>0
and
f^{\epsilon}
solving (
3.8
)-(
3.9
)
with
B
,
\Phi(x)
defined in (
0.5
) (resp. (
0.6
)), then uniformly in
\epsilon
and
(x,v)\in\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
,

\mu(x,v)+f^{\epsilon}(x,v)\geq 0.

(3.27)

Moreover, uniformly in
\epsilon
, we have

\iint_{\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f^{\epsilon}(x,v)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}v=0\ \text{and}\ \|f^{\epsilon}\|_{1}\leq 2\|\mu\|_{L^{1}}.

(3.28)

Finally the limit
f
as
\epsilon\rightarrow 0
of the sequence
\{f^{\epsilon}\}
exists, and it solves (
0.7
) and (
3.3
)
(resp. (
0.10
) and (
3.3
)).
Moreover, we obtain for
(x,v)\in\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
,

\mu(x,v)+f(x,v)\geq 0.

(3.29)

Proof.

Step 1.

We first show that for any
l,j\in\mathbb{N}
and
(x,v)\in\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
,

\mu(x,v)+f^{l}_{j}(x,v)\geq 0.

(3.30)

where
f^{l}_{j}(x,v)
is the solution to (
3.10
) and (
3.11
) with
\Phi(x)
defined in (
0.5
).

To prove this, we use the induction. For
l=0
,
f^{0}_{j}=0
and it is easy to check
\mu+f^{0}_{j}\geq 0
.
Next we assume for
0<k\in\mathbb{N}
and
l\leq k
, and we have
\mu+f^{l}_{j}\geq 0
.
Now for
l=k+1
, and we recall the sequential setting on
f^{l}_{j}
.
From (
3.10
) and the characteristic line on
e^{\epsilon t}f^{l}_{j}(x,v)
, for
(x,v)\in\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
,

f^{l}_{j}(x,v)=e^{-\epsilon t_{\mathbf{b}}}f^{l}_{j}(x_{\mathbf{b}},v_{\mathbf{b}}),

(3.31)

So we just need to show
\mu(x,v)+f^{k+1}_{j}(x,v)\geq 0
for
(x,v)\in\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
.
Using the boundary condition (
3.11
) and the assumption that
f^{k}_{j}\geq-\mu(x,v)
, for
(x,v)\in\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
,

\begin{split}f^{k+1}_{j}(x,v)&=(1-\frac{1}{j})\mu_{\Theta}(x,v)\int_{n(x)\cdot v^{1}>0}f^{k}_{j}(x,v^{1})\{n(x)\cdot v^{1}\}\mathrm{d}v^{1}+r(x,v)\\
&\geq\mu_{\Theta}(x,v)\int_{n(x)\cdot v^{1}>0}-\mu(x,v^{1})\{n(x)\cdot v^{1}\}\mathrm{d}v^{1}+r(x,v)\\
&\geq-\mu_{\Theta}(x,v)+r(x,v)=-\mu(x,v),\end{split}

which implies (
3.30
).

Since
f^{l}_{j}\rightarrow f^{j}
as
l\rightarrow\infty
in
L^{1}
and
f^{j}(x,v)
solves (
3.5
)-(
3.6
), we deduce for
(x,v)\in\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
,

\mu(x,v)+f^{j}(x,v)\geq 0

Similarly, since
f^{j}\rightarrow f^{\epsilon}
as
j\rightarrow\infty
in
L^{1}
and
f^{\epsilon}(x,v)
solves (
3.8
)-(
3.9
), for
(x,v)\in\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
,

\mu(x,v)+f^{\epsilon}(x,v)\geq 0.

By following the characteristic line, we conclude (
3.27
) for
(x,v)\in\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
.

Step 2.

Using Green’s identity on (
3.8
) and (
3.9
), we get,

\epsilon\iint_{\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f^{\epsilon}(x,v)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}v+\iint_{\gamma}f^{\epsilon}(x,v)\{n(x)\cdot v\}\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}S_{x}=0.

Using the fact that
\int_{\gamma_{-}}r(x,v)\mathrm{d}S_{x}\mathrm{d}v=0
, we obtain, for any
\epsilon>0
,

\epsilon\iint_{\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f^{\epsilon}(x,v)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}v=0.

Applying (
3.27
) and the zero mass on
f^{\epsilon}
, we derive that

\|\mu+f^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{1}}=\iint_{\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\mu+f^{\epsilon})\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}v=\|\mu\|_{L^{1}}.

Thus we have, uniformly in
\epsilon
,

\|f^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{1}}\leq 2\|\mu\|_{L^{1}}.

Next, we use the same idea in (
3.21
), (
3.22
), then for any
(x,v)\in\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
and
\epsilon
,

\begin{split}|f^{\epsilon}(x,v)|\lesssim\mu_{\Theta}(x_{\mathbf{b}},v_{\mathbf{b}})\|f^{\epsilon}\|_{1}+\|r(x_{\mathbf{b}},v_{\mathbf{b}})\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq\Big(2\|\mu\|_{L^{1}}+2\Big)e^{-\frac{1}{2b}(|v|^{2}/2+\Phi(x))}.\end{split}

(3.32)

Therefore, we derive the weak
*
limit on
\{f^{\epsilon}\}
in
L^{\infty}
norm up to a subsequence, such that

f^{\epsilon}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\ast}}{{\rightharpoonup}}f\in L^{\infty}.

Since (
3.8
) is linear, we derive that
f
is the weak solution for (
0.7
).
Note from (
3.32
), we have uniform
L^{\infty}(\partial\Omega)
bound on
\{\mathfrak{J}^{\varepsilon}\}
where
f^{\epsilon}(x,v)=\mu_{\Theta}(x_{\mathbf{b}},v_{\mathbf{b}})J^{\varepsilon}(x_{\mathbf{b}},v_{\mathbf{b}})+r(x_{\mathbf{b}},v_{\mathbf{b}})
.
Again up to a subsequence, we derive the weak
*
limit on
\{\mathfrak{J}^{\varepsilon}\}
in
L^{\infty}(\partial\Omega)
such that
J^{\varepsilon}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\ast}}{{\rightharpoonup}}J
.
We let
f(x,v)=\mu_{\Theta}(x_{\mathbf{b}},v_{\mathbf{b}})J(x_{\mathbf{b}},v_{\mathbf{b}})+r(x_{\mathbf{b}},v_{\mathbf{b}})
for
(x,v)\in\gamma_{-}
, and it satisfies (
3.3
).

Lastly, for every Borel set
A
in
\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
, the weak
*
convergence in
L^{\infty}
norm implies that

\iint_{\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f^{\epsilon}\times\mathbf{I}_{A}\rightarrow\iint_{\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f\times\mathbf{I}_{A},

where
\mathbf{I}_{A}
is the sign function on
A
. Since
\mu(x,v)+f^{\epsilon}(x,v)\geq 0
, we derive (
3.29
).

For the magnet field case, since the magnet field doesn’t influence the integration when we use Green’s identity, the proof follows from the gravitational case.
∎

Now we are ready to prove Proposition
6
.

Proof of Proposition
6
.

Since we have shown the existence of the solution
f
in Lemma
16
, we only need to prove
\iint_{\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f(x,v)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}v=0
and the uniqueness.

For any
k<\infty
, it is easy to check that
\mathbf{1}_{\{|x|,|v|\leq k\}}\in L^{1}(x.v)
. Applying
f^{\epsilon}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\ast}}{{\rightharpoonup}}f\in L^{\infty}
, we have

\iint_{\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f^{\epsilon}\times\mathbf{1}_{\{|x|,|v|\leq k\}}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}v\rightarrow\iint_{\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f\times\mathbf{1}_{\{|x|,|v|\leq k\}}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}v.

From (
3.8
) and the characteristic line on
e^{\epsilon t}f^{\epsilon}(x,v)
, for
(x,v)\in\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
, we obtain,

f^{\epsilon}(x,v)=e^{-\epsilon t_{\mathbf{b}}}f^{\epsilon}(x_{\mathbf{b}},v_{\mathbf{b}}),

(3.33)

Using (
1.4
)
and (
3.33
), we have

\begin{split}&\ \ \ \ \iint_{\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f^{\epsilon}(x,v)\times\mathbf{1}_{\{|x|>k\ \text{or}\ |v|>k\}}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}v\\
&=\int_{\gamma_{-}}\int_{0}^{t_{+}}f^{\epsilon}(X(t,t-s,x,v),V(t,t-s,x,v))\times\mathbf{1}_{\{|X|>k\ \text{or}\ |V|>k\}}|n(x)\cdot v|\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}S_{x}\\
&=\int_{\gamma_{-}}\int_{0}^{t_{+}}e^{-\epsilon s}f^{\epsilon}(x,v)\times\mathbf{1}_{\{|X|>k\ \text{or}\ |V|>k\}}\ |n(x)\cdot v|\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}S_{x}.\end{split}

(3.34)

From Lemma
3
, we have

|v|^{2}+2\Phi(x)=|v_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)|^{2}.

Using (
1.17
), (
3.22
) and the uniform
L^{\infty}
bound on
f^{\epsilon}
, we deduce

\begin{split}\eqref{first fe xv>k estimate}&\lesssim\int_{\gamma_{-}}\mu_{\Theta}(x,v)\big(\|f^{\epsilon}\|_{\infty}+2\big)\times\mathbf{1}_{\{|v|\gtrsim k\}}\ |n(x)\cdot v|^{2}\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}S_{x}\\
&\lesssim\int_{n(x)\cdot v<0}\mu^{1/b}(x,v)\times\mathbf{1}_{\{|v|\gtrsim k\}}\ |n(x)\cdot v|^{2}\mathrm{d}v.\end{split}

(3.35)

Note that we can derive the same estimate on
f
since
f
and
f^{\epsilon}
share the same characteristics trajectories.
Therefore, for any
\epsilon>0
, we can find sufficient large
k
such that,

\begin{split}&\Big|\iint_{\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f^{\epsilon}\times\mathbf{1}_{\{|x|>k\ \text{or}\ |v|>k\}}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}v\Big|<\frac{\epsilon}{3},\\
&\Big|\iint_{\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f\times\mathbf{1}_{\{|x|>k\ \text{or}\ |v|>k\}}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}v\Big|<\frac{\epsilon}{3}.\end{split}

Next, we can find sufficient small
l
such that, for any
\epsilon<l
,

\Big|\iint_{\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f^{\epsilon}\times\mathbf{1}_{\{|x|,|v|\leq k\}}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}v-\iint_{\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f\times\mathbf{1}_{\{|x|,|v|\leq k\}}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}v\Big|\leq\frac{\epsilon}{3}.

Using the above and the zero mass on
f^{\epsilon}
, we derive

\begin{split}\big|\iint_{\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}v\big|&=\Big|\iint_{\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}v-\iint_{\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f^{\epsilon}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}v\Big|\\
&\leq\Big|\iint_{\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f^{\epsilon}\times\mathbf{1}_{\{|x|,|v|\leq k\}}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}v-\iint_{\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f\times\mathbf{1}_{\{|x|,|v|\leq k\}}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}v\Big|\\
&\ \ \ \ +\Big|\iint_{\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f\times\mathbf{1}_{\{|x|>k\ \text{or}\ |v|>k\}}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}v\Big|+\Big|\iint_{\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f^{\epsilon}\times\mathbf{1}_{\{|x|>k\ \text{or}\ |v|>k\}}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}v\Big|\\
&\leq\frac{\epsilon}{3}+\frac{\epsilon}{3}+\frac{\epsilon}{3}=\epsilon,\end{split}

which concludes (
3.4
).

Suppose
g
is also the solution, and set
\delta:=f-g
. From Green’s identity, we have,

|\delta^{j}|_{1,+}=|\delta^{j}|_{1,-}\leq|\delta^{j}|_{1,+}.

Moreover, we have, for any
x\in\partial\Omega
,

\int_{n(x)\cdot v^{1}>0}|\delta|\{n(x)\cdot v^{1}\}\mathrm{d}v^{1}=\Big|\int_{n(x)\cdot v^{1}>0}\delta\{n(x)\cdot v^{1}\}\mathrm{d}v^{1}\Big|.

which implies
\delta(x,\cdot)
keeps non-positivity/non-negativity for any fixed
x
and

v\in\mathcal{V}:=\{v\in\mathbb{R}^{3}:n(x)\cdot v>0\}
. Then we deduce, for
(x,v)\in\gamma_{+}
,

\delta(x,v)=\mu_{\Theta}(x_{\mathbf{b}},v_{\mathbf{b}})\int_{n(x_{\mathbf{b}})\cdot v^{1}>0}\delta(x_{\mathbf{b}},v^{1})\{n(x_{\mathbf{b}})\cdot v^{1}\}\mathrm{d}v^{1}\ \text{with}\ \mu>0.

This shows that
\delta
doesn’t change non-positivity/non-negativity for any
x
or
v
. Using the fact that both
f
and
g
have zero mass, we derive

\delta=0\ a.e.\ ,

which gives the uniqueness on the solution.

For the magnet field case, we can deduce (
3.34
) by (
1.4
). Moreover, using Lemma
5
, we have
|v|^{2}+2\Phi(x)=|v_{\mathbf{b}}|^{2}
and
t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)=|v_{3}|/5
.
The rest of proof is similar as the gravitational case and so the proof is omitted.
∎

Now we are well equipped to prove Theorem
1
.

Proof of Theorem
1
.

Recall (
3.2
) and
\|\mu\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}=\mathfrak{m}_{\mu}>0
.
For
(x,v)\in\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
, we write

F(x,v)=\frac{\mathfrak{m}}{\mathfrak{m}_{\mu}}\big(\mu(x,v)+f(x,v)\big)

W.l.o.g. we suppose
\mathfrak{m}=\mathfrak{m}_{\mu}
, i.e.

F(x,v)=\mu(x,v)+f(x,v)
.
By direct computation, for
(x,v)\in\gamma_{-}
,

\begin{split}F(x,v)&=\mu(x,v)+\mu_{\Theta}(x,v)\int_{n(x)\cdot v^{1}>0}f(x,v^{1})\{n(x)\cdot v^{1}\}\mathrm{d}v^{1}+r(x,v)\\
&=\mu(x,v)+\mu_{\Theta}(x,v)\int_{n(x)\cdot v^{1}>0}F(x,v^{1})\{n(x)\cdot v^{1}\}\mathrm{d}v^{1}-\mu_{\Theta}(x,v)+r(x,v)\\
&=\mu_{\Theta}(x,v)\int_{n(x)\cdot v^{1}>0}F(x,v^{1})\{n(x)\cdot v^{1}\}\mathrm{d}v^{1},\end{split}

this shows
F(x,v)
solves (
0.7
) and (
0.2
). Moreover, since
\iint_{\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f(x,v)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}v=0
, we derive

\iint_{\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}F(x,v)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}v=\mathfrak{m},

thus we show the existence of the solution.

Next, it is easy to check that for any steady state
F(x,v)
with unit mass,

F(x,v)-\mu(x,v)
need to satisfy (
0.7
) and (
3.4
).
Applying the uniqueness on
f(x,v)
from Proposition
6
, we conclude the uniqueness on
F(x,v)
.
Finally, we derive (
0.9
) from Proposition
5
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
1
for magnetic case
.

For the magnet field case, we also set

F(x,v)=\frac{\mathfrak{m}}{\mathfrak{m}_{\mu}}\big(\mu(x,v)+f(x,v)\big)
, and then for
(x,v)\in\gamma_{-}
,

\begin{split}F(x,v)=\mu_{\Theta}(x,v)\int_{n(x)\cdot v^{1}>0}F(x,v^{1})\{n(x)\cdot v^{1}\}\mathrm{d}v^{1},\end{split}

this shows
F(x,v)
solves (
0.10
) and (
0.2
).
The rest of proof is similar to the proof of Theorem
1
.
∎

Remark 6
.

From (
3.29
), we derive that the steady state is non-negative with positive mass.
In the rest of paper, we use
F_{s}(x,v)
to represent the non-negative solution to
(
0.7
) and (
0.2
)
(resp. (
0.10
) and (
0.2
))
with
\iint_{\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}F_{s}(x,v)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}v=1
.

4.
Weighted
L^{1}
-Estimates

Recall

f(t,x,v)=F(t,x,v)-F_{s}(x,v),

which solves

\displaystyle\partial_{t}f+v\cdot\nabla_{x}f+(v\times B-\nabla\phi-g\mathbf{e}_{3})\cdot\nabla_{v}f=0,

\displaystyle\ \ \text{for}\ \ (t,x,v)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3},

(4.1)

\displaystyle f(t,x,v)|_{t=0}=F_{0}(x,v)-F_{s}(x,v)=f_{0}(x,v),

\displaystyle\ \ \text{for}\ \ (x,v)\in\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3},

(4.2)

\displaystyle f(t,x,v)=\mu_{\Theta}(x,v)\int_{n(x^{1})\cdot v^{1}>0}f(t,x,v^{1})\{n(x^{1})\cdot v^{1}\}\mathrm{d}v^{1},

\displaystyle\ \ \text{for}\ \ (t,x,v)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\gamma_{-}.

(4.3)

The main purpose of this section is to prove Theorem
7
which concerns
L^{1}
-estimates on fluctuations.

Theorem 7
.

Assume the same conditions in Theorem
2
for both cases of (
0.5
) and (
0.6
). Then a unique solution
F(t,x,v)=F_{s}(x,v)+f(t,x,v)
satisfies

\|f(t)\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}\lesssim e^{-\Lambda t}\{\|e^{\theta^{\prime}(|v|^{2}+2\Phi(x))}f_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}+\|e^{\delta(|v|^{2}+2\Phi(x))^{1/2}}f_{0}\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}\},

(4.4)

where
\Lambda=\Lambda(\Omega,\delta,T_{0})
is defined in (
4.69
) and
T_{0}
in (
4.57
).

4.1.
f(t,x,v)
via Stochastic Cycles

Lemma 17
.

Suppose
f
solve (
4.1
)-(
4.3
) for either (
0.5
) or (
0.6
), and
t_{*}\leq t
, then we have

\displaystyle f(t,x,v)=

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}_{t^{1}<t_{*}}f(t_{*},X(t_{*};t,x,v),V(t_{*};t,x,v))

(4.5)

\displaystyle+\mu_{\Theta}(x^{1},v_{\mathbf{b}})\int_{\prod_{j=1}^{i}\mathcal{V}_{j}}\sum\limits^{k-1}_{i=1}\Big\{\mathbf{1}_{t^{i+1}<t_{*}\leq t^{i}}f(t_{*},X(t_{*};t^{i},x^{i},v^{i}),V(t_{*};t^{i},x^{i},v^{i}))\Big\}\mathrm{d}\Sigma_{i}

(4.6)

\displaystyle+\mu_{\Theta}(x^{1},v_{\mathbf{b}})\int_{\prod_{j=1}^{k}\mathcal{V}_{j}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{k}\geq t_{*}}f(t^{k},x^{k},v^{k})\mathrm{d}\Sigma_{k},

(4.7)

where

\mathrm{d}{\Sigma}_{i}:=\frac{\mathrm{d}\sigma_{i}}{\mu_{\Theta}(x^{i+1},v^{i})}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{i-1}\cdots\mathrm{d}\sigma_{1}
, with
\mathrm{d}\sigma_{j}=\mu_{\Theta}(x^{j+1},v^{j})\{n(x^{j})\cdot v^{j}\}\mathrm{d}v^{j}
in (
0.16
). Here,
(X,V)
in (
1.1
) (resp. (
1.2
)).

Proof.

Following the similar steps in Lemma
2
and Remark
1
, we can obtain this Lemma.
∎

Lemma 18
.

Consider
b
in (
0.4
), suppose
\varphi(\tau)\geq 0
,
\varphi^{\prime}\geq 0
, and

\int_{1}^{\infty}e^{-\frac{1}{2b}\tau^{2}}\varphi(\tau)\mathrm{d}\tau<\infty.

(4.8)

Suppose
f
solves (
4.1
)-(
4.3
) for either (
0.5
) or (
0.6
), there exists
C>0
independent of
t_{*}
,
t
, such that for all
0\leq t_{*}\leq t
,

\begin{split}&\ \ \ \ \|\varphi(t_{\mathbf{f}})f(t)\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}+\int^{t}_{t_{*}}\|\varphi^{\prime}(t_{\mathbf{f}})f\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}\mathrm{d}s+\int_{t_{*}}^{t}|\varphi(t_{\mathbf{f}})f|_{L^{1}_{\gamma_{+}}}\mathrm{d}s\\
&\leq\|\varphi(t_{\mathbf{f}})f(t_{*})\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}+C(t-t_{*}+1)\|f(t_{*})\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}+\frac{1}{4}\int^{t}_{t_{*}}|f|_{L^{1}_{\gamma_{+}}}\mathrm{d}s.\end{split}

(4.9)

For the proof of Lemma
18
, We shall start it from Lemma
19
, the energy estimate.

Lemma 19
.

Suppose
f
solves (
4.1
)-(
4.3
) for either (
0.5
) or (
0.6
). For
0\leq t_{*}\leq t
with
\delta\in(0,t-t_{*})
,

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \|f(t)\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}\leq\|f(t_{*})\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}},

(4.10)

\displaystyle\int^{t}_{t_{*}}|f(s)|_{L^{1}_{\gamma_{+}}}\mathrm{d}s\leq\Big\lceil\frac{t-t_{*}}{\delta}\Big\rceil\|f(t_{*})\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}+O(\delta^{2})\int^{t}_{t_{*}}|f(s)|_{L^{1}_{\gamma_{+}}}\mathrm{d}s,

(4.11)

and if
f_{0}
is non-negative, so is
f(t,x,v)
for all
(t,x,v)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
.

Proof.

From (
4.1
), (
4.3
) and taking integration over
(t_{*},t)\times\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
,
we derive that

\|f(t)\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}+\int^{t}_{t*}\iint_{\gamma_{+}}|f|\mathrm{d}s-\int^{t}_{t*}\iint_{\gamma_{-}}|f|\mathrm{d}s=\|f(t_{*})\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}.

Due to the choice of
\mu_{\Theta}(x,v)
in (
0.2
), for
\forall t>0
,

\int_{\gamma_{-}}|f(t,x,v)||n(x)\cdot v|\mathrm{d}S_{x}\mathrm{d}v=\Big|\int_{\gamma_{+}}f(t,x,v)\{n(x)\cdot v\}\mathrm{d}S_{x}\mathrm{d}v\Big|.

Therefore, we have

\begin{split}&\ \ \ \ \int^{t}_{t*}\iint_{\gamma_{+}}|f|\mathrm{d}s-\int^{t}_{t*}\iint_{\gamma_{-}}|f|\mathrm{d}s\\
&=\int^{t}_{t*}\iint_{\gamma_{+}}|f|\mathrm{d}s-\int^{t}_{t*}\Big|\iint_{\gamma_{+}}f\Big|\mathrm{d}s\geq\int^{t}_{t*}\iint_{\gamma_{+}}|f|\mathrm{d}s-\int^{t}_{t*}\iint_{\gamma_{+}}|f|\mathrm{d}s=0,\end{split}

therefore we prove (
4.10
).

Next we work on (
4.11
). For
\delta\in(0,t-t_{*})
and
(x,v)\in\gamma_{+}
,

\begin{split}|f(s,x,v)|&\leq\underbrace{\sum\limits^{\lceil\frac{t-t_{*}-\delta}{\delta}\rceil}_{k=1}\mathbf{1}_{t_{*}+k\delta\leq s\leq t_{*}+(k+1)\delta,\ \delta<t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)}|f(t_{*}+k\delta,X(t_{*}+k\delta,s,x,v),V(t_{*}+k\delta,s,x,v))|}_{\eqref{|f|1}_{1}}\\
&\ \ \ \ +\underbrace{\mathbf{1}_{t_{*}+\delta\leq s,\ \delta\geq t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)}|f(s-t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v),x_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v),v_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v))|}_{\eqref{|f|1}_{2}}\\
&\ \ \ \ +\underbrace{\mathbf{1}_{s-t_{*}<\delta,\ s-t_{*}<t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)}|f(t_{*},X(t_{*},s,x,v),V(t_{*},s,x,v))|}_{\eqref{|f|1}_{3}}\\
&\ \ \ \ +\underbrace{\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)\leq s-t_{*}<\delta}|f(s-t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v),x_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v),v_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v))|}_{\eqref{|f|1}_{4}},\end{split}

(4.12)

where
s\in[t_{*},t]
.

First we do estimate on
\eqref{|f|1}_{1}
.
From (
1.3
), (
4.10
) and
t_{*}+\delta\leq s\leq t
with
\delta<t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)
,

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \int^{t}_{t_{*}}\int_{\gamma_{+}}\eqref{|f|1}_{1}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq\sum\limits^{\lceil\frac{t-t_{*}-\delta}{\delta}\rceil}_{k=1}\int_{\gamma_{+}}\int^{t_{*}+k\delta+t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)}_{t_{*}+k\delta}|f(t_{*}+k\delta,X(t_{*}+k\delta,s,x,v),V(t_{*}+k\delta,s,x,v))|\mathrm{d}s\{n(x)\cdot v\}\mathrm{d}S_{x}\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle\leq\sum\limits^{\lceil\frac{t-t_{*}-\delta}{\delta}\rceil}_{k=1}\|f(t_{*}+k\delta)\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}\leq\Big\lceil\frac{t-t_{*}-\delta}{\delta}\Big\rceil\times\|f(t_{*})\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}.

(4.13)

Now we consider
\eqref{|f|1}_{2}
. For
y=x_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)
and
s\in[t_{*},t]
, we have

\mathbf{1}_{\delta\geq t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)}=\mathbf{1}_{\delta\geq t_{\mathbf{f}}(y,v_{\mathbf{b}})}.

(4.14)

From (
1.5
), (
4.14
) and using the Fubini’s theorem, we derive

\displaystyle\int^{t}_{t_{*}}\int_{\gamma_{+}}\eqref{|f|1}_{2}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=\int_{\gamma_{+}}\int^{t}_{t_{*}+\delta}\mathbf{1}_{\delta\geq t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)}|f(s-t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v),x_{\mathbf{b}},v_{\mathbf{b}})|\mathrm{d}s\{n(x)\cdot v\}\mathrm{d}S_{x}\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\partial\Omega}\int_{n(y)\cdot v<0}\mathbf{1}_{\delta>t_{\mathbf{f}}(y,v)}\int^{t}_{t_{*}}|f(s,y,v)|\mathrm{d}s|n(y)\cdot v|\mathrm{d}S_{y}\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\partial\Omega}\underbrace{\Big(\int_{n(y)\cdot v<0}\mathbf{1}_{\delta>t_{\mathbf{f}}(y,v)}\mu_{\Theta}(y,v)|n(y)\cdot v|\mathrm{d}v\Big)}_{\eqref{est:|f|2}_{*}}\int^{t}_{t_{*}}\int_{n(y)\cdot v^{1}>0}|f(s,y,v^{1})|\{n(y)\cdot v^{1}\}\mathrm{d}v^{1}\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}S_{y}.

(4.15)

From (
2.42
), we derive

\eqref{est:|f|2}\leq O(\delta^{2})\int^{t}_{t_{*}}\int_{\gamma_{+}}|f|\mathrm{d}s.

From (
1.3
), (
4.10
) and
s<t_{*}+t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)
, we have

\int^{t}_{t_{*}}\int_{\gamma_{+}}\eqref{|f|1}_{3}\mathrm{d}s\leq\int^{t_{*}+t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)}_{t_{*}}\int_{\gamma_{+}}\eqref{|f|1}_{3}\mathrm{d}s=\|f(t_{*})\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}\leq\|f(t_{*})\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}.

Again setting
y=x_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)
and
s\in[t_{*},t]
, we have

\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)\leq s-t_{*}<\delta}\leq\mathbf{1}_{\delta\geq t_{\mathbf{f}}(y,v_{\mathbf{b}})}.

(4.16)

From (
1.5
), (
4.16
) and using the Fubini’s theorem, we derive

\displaystyle\int^{t}_{t_{*}}\int_{\gamma_{+}}\eqref{|f|1}_{4}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=\int_{\gamma_{+}}\int^{t_{*}+\delta}_{t_{*}+t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)}\mathbf{1}_{\delta\geq t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)}|f(s-t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v),x_{\mathbf{b}},v_{\mathbf{b}})|\mathrm{d}s\{n(x)\cdot v\}\mathrm{d}S_{x}\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\partial\Omega}\int_{n(y)\cdot v<0}\mathbf{1}_{\delta>t_{\mathbf{f}}(y,v)}\int^{t_{*}+\delta}_{t_{*}}|f(s,y,v)|\mathrm{d}s|n(y)\cdot v|\mathrm{d}S_{y}\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\partial\Omega}\underbrace{\Big(\int_{n(y)\cdot v<0}\mathbf{1}_{\delta>t_{\mathbf{f}}(y,v)}\mu_{\Theta}(y,v)|n(y)\cdot v|\mathrm{d}v\Big)}_{\eqref{est:|f|4}_{*}}\int^{t_{*}+\delta}_{t_{*}}\int_{\gamma_{+}}|f|\mathrm{d}s.

(4.17)

Then we conclude
\eqref{est:|f|4}\leq O(\delta^{2})\int^{t}_{t_{*}}\int_{\gamma_{+}}|f|\mathrm{d}s
, therefore we prove (
4.11
).

To prove the positivity property, we write

f_{-}=\frac{|f|-f}{2}.

From (
4.10
) and the assumption
f_{0}\geq 0
, we have

\|f_{-}(t)\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}=\big\|\frac{|f|(t)-f(t)}{2}\big\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}=\Big\|\frac{\big(|f|-f\big)(t)}{2}\Big\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}\leq\big\|\frac{|f_{0}|-f_{0}}{2}\big\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}=0,

(4.18)

then we conclude
f_{-}(t,x,v)=0
on
\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
.

For the magnet field case, we consider
f
solves (
4.1
)-(
4.3
) for (
0.6
).
By direct computation, we have

\nabla_{v}\cdot(v\times B-\nabla\Phi(x))=\nabla_{v}\cdot(B_{3}v_{2},-B_{3}v_{1},0)=0.

By taking integration over
(t_{*},t)\times\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
,
we derive (
4.10
).
The rest of proof is similar as the gravitational case and so the proof is omitted.
∎

Now we are ready to prove Lemma
18
, which will be used frequently in this paper.

Proof of Lemma
18
.

Considering the characteristics trajectory

\big(s,X(s;t,x,v),V(s;t,x,v)\big)
determined by
(t,x,v)
, then we apply this characteristics on
t_{\mathbf{f}}(x,v)
,

\begin{split}-1&=\frac{d}{ds}t_{\mathbf{f}}(s,X(s;t,x,v),V(s;t,x,v))\\
&=\frac{\partial}{\partial s}t_{\mathbf{f}}(s,X,V)+\frac{\partial}{\partial X}t_{\mathbf{f}}(s,X,V)\cdot\frac{d}{ds}X+\frac{\partial}{\partial V}t_{\mathbf{f}}(s,X,V)\cdot\frac{d}{ds}V,\end{split}

By setting
s=t
, we have

\begin{split}&\frac{\partial}{\partial t}t_{\mathbf{f}}(t,x,v)+\frac{\partial}{\partial x}t_{\mathbf{f}}(t,x,v)\cdot v+\frac{\partial}{\partial v}t_{\mathbf{f}}(t,x,v)\cdot-\nabla\Phi(x)=-1.\end{split}

Then, in the sense of distribution

[\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}-\nabla\Phi\cdot\nabla_{v}]\big(\varphi(t_{\mathbf{f}})|f|\big)=\varphi^{\prime}(t_{\mathbf{f}})[\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}-\nabla\Phi\cdot\nabla_{v}](t_{\mathbf{f}})|f|=-\varphi^{\prime}(t_{\mathbf{f}})|f|.

(4.19)

From (
4.3
), (
4.19
),
\varphi(\tau)\geq 0
,
\varphi^{\prime}\geq 0
and taking integration over
(t_{*},t)\times\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
,
we derive

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \|\varphi(t_{\mathbf{f}})f(t)\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}+\int^{t}_{t_{*}}\|\varphi^{\prime}(t_{\mathbf{f}})f(s)\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}\mathrm{d}s+\int^{t}_{t_{*}}\int_{\gamma_{+}}\varphi(t_{\mathbf{f}})|f|\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq\|\varphi(t_{\mathbf{f}})f(t_{*})\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}+\int^{t}_{t_{*}}\int_{\partial\Omega}\int_{n(x)\cdot v<0}\varphi(t_{\mathbf{f}})|f||n(x)\cdot v|\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}S_{x}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=\|\varphi(t_{\mathbf{f}})f(t_{*})\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}+\int^{t}_{t_{*}}\int_{\partial\Omega}\int_{n(x)\cdot v<0}\varphi(t_{\mathbf{f}})\mu_{\Theta}(x,v)|n(x)\cdot v|\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}S_{x}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\hskip 170.71652pt\times\int_{n(x)\cdot v^{1}>0}|f(s,x,v^{1})|\{n(x^{1})\cdot v^{1}\}\mathrm{d}v^{1}.

(4.20)

Now we prove the following claim:
If (
4.8
) holds, then

\sup_{x\in\partial\Omega}\int_{n(x)\cdot v<0}\varphi(t_{\mathbf{f}})(x,v)\mu_{\Theta}(x,v)|n(x)\cdot v|\mathrm{d}v\lesssim 1.

(4.21)

We split
\int_{n(x)\cdot v<0}\varphi(t_{\mathbf{f}})(x,v)\mu_{\Theta}(x,v)|n(x)\cdot v|\mathrm{d}v
into two parts: integration over the regimes of
t_{\mathbf{f}}\leq 1
and
t_{\mathbf{f}}>1
.

For
t_{\mathbf{f}}\leq 1
, since
|n(x)\cdot v|\lesssim t_{\mathbf{f}}\leq 1
, we bound

\begin{split}\int_{n(x)\cdot v<0}\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{f}}\leq 1}\ \varphi(t_{\mathbf{f}})\mu_{\Theta}(x,v)|n(x)\cdot v|\mathrm{d}v\lesssim\varphi(1)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{-|v|^{2}/2b}\mathrm{d}v\lesssim 1.\end{split}

(4.22)

Next we focus on the integration over the regimes of
t_{\mathbf{f}}>1
.
From (
1.17
) and (
2.16
), we obtain

\int_{n(x)\cdot v<0}\varphi(t_{\mathbf{f}})\mu_{\Theta}(x,v)|n(x)\cdot v|\mathrm{d}v\lesssim\int_{\partial\Omega}\int_{1}^{\infty}\varphi(t_{\mathbf{f}})e^{-|t_{\mathbf{f}}|^{2}/2b}|n(x)\cdot v|\frac{|n(x_{\mathbf{f}})\cdot v_{\mathbf{f}}|}{|t_{\mathbf{f}}|^{3}}\mathrm{d}t_{\mathbf{f}}\mathrm{d}S_{x_{\mathbf{f}}}.

(4.23)

From (
4.8
), we derive that

\eqref{int:rho_2}\lesssim\int_{1}^{\infty}\frac{\varphi(t_{\mathbf{f}})}{|t_{\mathbf{f}}|}e^{-|t_{\mathbf{f}}|^{2}/2b}\mathrm{d}t_{\mathbf{f}}\lesssim\int_{1}^{\infty}\varphi(t_{\mathbf{f}})e^{-|t_{\mathbf{f}}|^{2}/2b}\mathrm{d}t_{\mathbf{f}}\lesssim 1.

Combining the above bound with (
4.22
), we prove (
4.21
).
Then picking sufficiently small
\delta
in (
4.11
) and using (
4.21
), we conclude (
4.9
), through, for
C>1
,

\begin{split}\eqref{rho.f}&\lesssim\int^{t}_{t_{*}}\int_{\gamma_{+}}|f(s,x,v^{1})|\{n(x^{1})\cdot v^{1}\}\mathrm{d}v^{1}\mathrm{d}S_{x}\mathrm{d}s\\
&\leq C(t-t_{*}+1)\|f(t_{*})\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}+\frac{1}{4}\int^{t}_{t_{*}}|f(s)|_{L^{1}(\gamma_{+})}.\end{split}

For the magnet field case, from

\nabla_{v}\cdot(v\times B-\nabla\Phi(x))=0
and
\frac{d}{ds}t_{\mathbf{f}}(s,X(s;t,x,v),V(s;t,x,v))=-1
, we get

[\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}+(v\times B-\nabla\Phi)\cdot\nabla_{v}]\big(\varphi(t_{\mathbf{f}})|f|\big)=-\varphi^{\prime}(t_{\mathbf{f}})|f|.

(4.24)

The rest of proof follows from the gravitational case.
∎

4.2.
Doeblin condition

In this section, we will prove two of the key cornerstones, Proposition
8
and
10
, the lower bound with the unreachable defect.

Proposition 8
.

Suppose
f
solves (
4.1
)-(
4.3
). Assume
f_{0}(x,v)\geq 0
. For any
T_{0}\gg 1
and
N\in\mathbb{N}
there exists
\mathfrak{m}(x,v)\geq 0
, which only depends on
\Omega
and
T_{0}
(see (
4.34
) for the precise form), such that

f(NT_{0},x,v)\geq\mathfrak{m}(x,v)\Big\{\iint_{\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f((N-1)T_{0},x,v)\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x-\iint_{\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{f}}(x,v)\geq\frac{T_{0}}{4}}f((N-1)T_{0},x,v)\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x\Big\}.

(4.25)

Proof.

Step 1.

From (
4.18
), we have
f(t,x,v)\geq 0
from assumption
f_{0}(x,v)\geq 0
.
From (
4.5
)-(
4.7
) and setting
t=NT_{0}
,
t_{*}=(N-1)T_{0}
,
k=2
, we can derive that

\displaystyle f(NT_{0},x,v)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)\leq\frac{T_{0}}{4}}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{1},v_{\mathbf{b}})\int_{\mathcal{V}_{1}}\int_{\mathcal{V}_{2}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{2}\geq(N-1)T_{0}}f(t^{2},x^{2},v^{2})\{n(x^{2})\cdot v^{2}\}\mathrm{d}v^{2}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{1}.

(4.26)

Now we apply Proposition
3
on
v^{1}\in\mathcal{V}_{1}
with (
1.19
) and (
1.20
).
In order to have the bijective mapping with (
1.19
), we restrict the range of
v_{\mathbf{b}}^{1}
as

\mathcal{V}_{1,b}:=\{v_{\mathbf{b}}^{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}:x^{2}+\int^{t}_{t-t_{\mathbf{b}}}\big(v_{\mathbf{b}}+\int^{s}_{t-t_{\mathbf{b}}}-\nabla\Phi(X(\alpha;t^{1},x^{1},v^{1}))\mathrm{d}\alpha\big)\mathrm{d}s=x^{1}\}.

(4.27)

This implies all characteristic trajectories
X(\alpha;t^{1},x^{1},v^{1})
between
x^{1}
and
x^{2}
under
v_{\mathbf{b}}^{1}\in\mathcal{V}_{1,b}
don’t cross the periodic boundary.

Therefore, we derive

\begin{split}\eqref{Doeblin_1}&\gtrsim\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)\leq\frac{T_{0}}{4}}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{1},v_{\mathbf{b}})\int_{0}^{T_{0}-t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)}\int_{\partial\Omega}\underbrace{\frac{|n(x_{1_{\textbf{b}}})\cdot v_{\mathbf{b}}^{1}|}{|t_{\mathbf{b}}^{1}|^{3}}\{n(x^{1})\cdot v^{1}\}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{2},v_{\mathbf{b}}^{1})}_{\eqref{Doeblin_2}_{*}}\\
&\ \ \ \ \ \ \times\int_{n(x^{2})\cdot v^{2}>0}f(t^{2},x^{2},v^{2})\{n(x^{2})\cdot v^{2}\}\mathrm{d}v^{2}\mathrm{d}S_{x^{2}}\mathrm{d}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{1},\end{split}

(4.28)

where
t^{2}=NT_{0}-t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)-t_{\mathbf{b}}^{1}
.

Step 2.
In order to bound the integrands of the first line of (
4.28
), we will further restrict integration regimes. Note that
x^{1}=x_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)
is given,
x^{2}
is free variables and
t^{2}\geq(N-1)T_{0}
.

Now we restrict the integral regimes of the variable
t_{\mathbf{b}}^{1}
as

\mathfrak{T}^{T_{0}}:=\Big\{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{1}\in[0,\infty):T_{0}-t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)-\min\Big(t_{\mathbf{b}}(x^{2},v^{2}),\frac{T_{0}}{4}\Big)\leq t_{\mathbf{b}}^{1}\leq T_{0}-t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)\Big\}.

(4.29)

As a consequence of (
4.29
)
and
t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)\leq\frac{T_{0}}{4}
in (
4.28
), we will derive (
4.30
) and (
4.31
),

\frac{T_{0}}{2}\leq T_{0}-t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)-\frac{T_{0}}{4}\leq t_{\mathbf{b}}^{1}\leq T_{0}.

(4.30)

Secondly, we prove (
4.31
). Note that if
t_{\mathbf{b}}^{1}\in\mathfrak{T}^{T_{0}}
, we have

(N-1)T_{0}\leq t^{2}=NT_{0}-t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)-t_{\mathbf{b}}^{1}\leq(N-1)T_{0}+\min\{t_{\mathbf{b}}(x^{2},v^{2}),\frac{T_{0}}{4}\}.

This implies that, for
y_{*}=X((N-1)T_{0};t^{2},x^{2},v^{2})
and
v_{*}=V((N-1)T_{0};t^{2},x^{2},v^{2})
, we have

t_{\mathbf{f}}(y_{*},v_{*})=t^{2}-(N-1)T_{0}=T_{0}-t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)-t_{\mathbf{b}}^{1}\in\Big[0,\frac{T_{0}}{4}\Big],

(4.31)

where we use
t_{\mathbf{f}}(y_{*},v_{*})\leq t_{\mathbf{b}}(x^{2},v^{2})
since
x^{2}=x_{\mathbf{f}}(y_{*},v_{*})
.

Step 3.
For (
4.28
), we apply the restriction of integral regimes in (
4.27
) and (
4.29
).
Using (
1.17
), (
4.30
) and the assumption
T_{0}\gg 1
, we derive that

\begin{split}\eqref{Doeblin_2}_{*}&\gtrsim\frac{|t_{\mathbf{b}}^{1}|}{|t_{\mathbf{b}}^{1}|^{3}}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{1}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{2},v_{\mathbf{b}}^{1})\gtrsim\frac{1}{|t_{\mathbf{b}}^{1}|}\mu^{1/b}\big(|v_{\mathbf{b}}^{1}-n(x^{2})\cdot v_{\mathbf{b}}^{1}|+|n(x^{2})\cdot v_{\mathbf{b}}^{1}|\big)\\
&\gtrsim\frac{1}{|t_{\mathbf{b}}^{1}|}\mu^{1/b}\big(|n(x^{2})\cdot v_{\mathbf{b}}^{1}|\big)\mu^{1/b}\big(|v_{\mathbf{b}}^{1}-n(x^{2})\cdot v_{\mathbf{b}}^{1}|\big)\\
&\gtrsim\frac{1}{T_{0}}\mu^{1/b}(\frac{gt_{\mathbf{b}}^{1}}{2})\geq e^{-25T_{0}^{2}/b}.\end{split}

Finally we get

(
4.28
)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)\leq\frac{T_{0}}{4}}e^{-25T_{0}^{2}/b}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{1},v_{\mathbf{b}})\int_{\partial\Omega}\mathrm{d}S_{x^{2}}\int_{n(x^{2})\cdot v^{2}>0}\mathrm{d}v^{2}\{n(x^{2})\cdot v^{2}\}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \times\int_{\mathfrak{T}^{T_{0}}}\mathrm{d}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{1}f(NT_{0}-t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)-t_{\mathbf{b}}^{1},x^{2},v^{2})

\displaystyle\gtrsim\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)\leq\frac{T_{0}}{4}}e^{-25T_{0}^{2}/b}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{1},v_{\mathbf{b}})\int_{\partial\Omega}\mathrm{d}S_{x^{2}}\int_{n(x^{2})\cdot v^{2}>0}\mathrm{d}v^{2}\{n(x^{2})\cdot v^{2}\}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \times\int^{T_{0}-t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)}_{T_{0}-t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)-\min\big(t_{\mathbf{b}}(x^{2},v^{2}),\frac{T_{0}}{4}\big)}\mathrm{d}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{1}f(NT_{0}-t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)-t_{\mathbf{b}}^{1},x^{2},v^{2}).

(4.32)

Now we focus on the integrand of (
4.32
). Recall (
4.29
), we have

(NT_{0}-t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)-t_{\mathbf{b}}^{1})-(N-1)T_{0}=T_{0}-t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)-t_{\mathbf{b}}^{1}\in\Big[0,\min\Big(t_{\mathbf{b}}(x^{2},v^{2}),\frac{T_{0}}{4}\Big)\Big].

Now Setting
y_{*}=X((N-1)T_{0};t^{2},x^{2},v^{2})
,
v_{*}=V((N-1)T_{0};t^{2},x^{2},v^{2})
and
\alpha=T_{0}-t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)-t_{\mathbf{b}}^{1}
,
we have

\eqref{lower1}=\int^{\min\big(t_{\mathbf{b}}(x^{2},v^{2}),\frac{T_{0}}{4}\big)}_{0}f\big((N-1)T_{0},y_{*},v_{*}\big)\mathrm{d}\alpha.

(4.33)

From (
4.31
), we have
t_{\mathbf{f}}(y_{*},v_{*})\in\big[0,\frac{T_{0}}{4}\big]
. Now applying (
1.3
), we conclude that

\eqref{Doeblin_2}\geq\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)\leq\frac{T_{0}}{4}}e^{-25T_{0}^{2}/b}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{1},v_{\mathbf{b}})\iint_{\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{f}}(y,v)\in[0,\frac{T_{0}}{4}]}f((N-1)T_{0},y,v)\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}y.

We conclude (
4.25
) by setting

\mathfrak{m}(x,v):=\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)\leq\frac{T_{0}}{4}}e^{-25T_{0}^{2}/b}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{1},v_{\mathbf{b}}).

(4.34)

∎

An immediate consequence of Proposition
8
, follows.

Proposition 9
.

Suppose
f
solves (
4.1
)-(
4.3
), and satisfies (
0.11
). Then for all
T_{0}\gg 1
and
N\in\mathbb{N}
,

\|f(NT_{0})\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}\leq(1-\|\mathfrak{m}\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}})\|f((N-1)T_{0})\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}+2\|\mathfrak{m}\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}\|\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{f}}\geq\frac{T_{0}}{4}}f((N-1)T_{0})\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}.

(4.35)

Moreover, we have

\|\mathfrak{m}\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}:=\mathfrak{m}_{T_{0}}\lesssim e^{-25T_{0}^{2}/b}T_{0}|\partial\Omega|.

(4.36)

Proof.

We decompose

\displaystyle f((N-1)T_{0},x,v)=f_{N-1,+}(x,v)-f_{N-1,-}(x,v),

where

\begin{split}&f_{N-1,+}(x,v)=\mathbf{1}_{f((N-1)T_{0},x,v)\geq 0}f\big((N-1)T_{0},x,v\big),\\
&f_{N-1,-}(x,v)=\mathbf{1}_{f((N-1)T_{0},x,v)<0}|f\big((N-1)T_{0},x,v\big)|.\end{split}

Let
f_{\pm}(s,x,v)
solve (
4.1
) for
s\in[(N-1)T_{0},NT_{0}]
with the initial data
f_{N-1,+}
and
f_{N-1,-}
at
s=(N-1)T_{0}
, respectively.

Now we apply Proposition
8
on
f_{\pm}(t,x,v)
and conclude (
4.25
) for
f=f_{+}
and
f=f_{-}
respectively. We also note that

\iint_{\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f((N-1)T_{0},x,v)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}v=\iint_{\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f_{N-1,+}(x,v)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}v-\iint_{\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f_{N-1,-}(x,v)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}v=0.

This implies,

\iint_{\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f_{N-1,\pm}(x,v)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}v=\frac{1}{2}\iint_{\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}|f((N-1)T_{0},x,v)|\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}v.

(4.37)

From (
4.25
),

f_{N-1,\pm}(x,v)\geq\mathfrak{m}(x,v)\iint f_{N-1,\pm}(x,v)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}v-\mathfrak{m}(x,v)\iint_{\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{f}}(x,v)\geq\frac{T_{0}}{4}}f_{N-1,\pm}(x,v)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}v

Using (
4.37
), we have

f_{N-1,\pm}(x,v)\geq\underbrace{\mathfrak{m}(x,v)\Big(\frac{1}{2}\|f((N-1)T_{0})\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}-\|\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{f}}\geq\frac{T_{0}}{4}}f((N-1)T_{0})\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}\Big)}_{\mathfrak{l}(x,v)}.

(4.38)

Then we deduce

\begin{split}|f(NT_{0},x,v)|&=|f_{N-1,+}(x,v)-f_{N-1,-}(x,v)+\mathfrak{l}(x,v)-\mathfrak{l}(x,v)|\\
&\leq|f_{N-1,+}(x,v)-\mathfrak{l}(x,v)|+|f_{N-1,-}(x,v)-\mathfrak{l}(x,v)|.\end{split}

From (
4.38
),

|f(NT_{0},x,v)|\leq f_{N-1,+}(x,v)+f_{N-1,-}(x,v)-2\mathfrak{l}(x,v).

(4.39)

Note that
f_{+}(NT_{0},x,v)+f_{-}(NT_{0},x,v)
solves (
4.1
) with the initial datum

f_{N-1,+}+f_{N-1,-}=\big|f\big((N-1)T_{0},x,v\big)\big|.

Using (
4.37
), (
4.39
) and taking the integration on (
4.38
) over
\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
, we derive

\begin{split}\|f(NT_{0})\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}&\leq\iint_{\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f_{N-1,+}(x,v)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}v+\iint_{\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f_{N-1,-}(x,v)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}v-\iint_{\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}2\mathfrak{l}(x,v)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}v\\
&=(1-\|\mathfrak{m}\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}})\|f((N-1)T_{0})\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}+2\|\mathfrak{m}\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}\|\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{f}}\geq\frac{T_{0}}{4}}f((N-1)T_{0})\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}},\end{split}

therefore we prove (
4.35
).

To derive (
4.36
), it suffices to bound
\|\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)\leq\frac{T_{0}}{4}}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{1},v_{\mathbf{b}})\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}
.
From Lemma
3
, (
0.4
), (
1.3
) and the fact that
t_{\mathbf{b}}(t-s,X(t-s,t,x,v),V(t-s,t,x,v))=t_{\mathbf{b}}(t,x,v)-s
, we have

\begin{split}&\ \ \ \ \|\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)\leq\frac{T_{0}}{4}}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{1},v_{\mathbf{b}})\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}=\int_{\gamma_{+}}\int_{\max\{0,t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)-\frac{T_{0}}{4}\}}^{t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{1},v)\{n(x)\cdot v\}\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}S_{x}\\
\\
&\lesssim\int_{\gamma_{+}}\Big(\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)\leq\frac{T_{0}}{4}}\int_{0}^{t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)}\mathrm{d}s+\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)\geq\frac{T_{0}}{4}}\int^{t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)-\frac{T_{0}}{4}}\mathrm{d}s\Big)e^{-|v|^{2}/2b}\{n(x)\cdot v\}\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}S_{x}\\
&\leq\frac{T_{0}}{4}\int_{\partial\Omega}\mathrm{d}S_{x}\int_{n(x)\cdot v>0}e^{-|v|^{2}/2b}\{n(x)\cdot v\}\mathrm{d}v\lesssim T_{0}|\partial\Omega|.\end{split}

Combining the above bound with (
4.34
), we conclude (
4.36
).
∎

Proposition 10
.

Suppose
f
solves (
4.1
)-(
4.3
) for (
0.6
) and assume
f_{0}(x,v)\geq 0
. For any
T_{0}\gg 1
and
N\in\mathbb{N}
there exists
\mathfrak{m}(x,v)\geq 0
, which only depends on
\Omega
and
T_{0}
(see (
4.52
) for the precise form), such that

f(NT_{0},x,v)\geq\mathfrak{m}(x,v)\Big\{\iint_{\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f((N-1)T_{0},x,v)\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x-\iint_{\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{f}}(x,v)\geq\frac{T_{0}}{4}}f((N-1)T_{0},x,v)\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x\Big\}.

(4.40)

And if
f
also satisfies (
0.11
), then for any
T_{0}\gg 1
and
N\in\mathbb{N}

\|f(NT_{0})\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}\leq(1-\|\mathfrak{m}\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}})\|f((N-1)T_{0})\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}+2\|\mathfrak{m}\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}\|\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{f}}\geq\frac{T_{0}}{4}}f((N-1)T_{0})\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}.

(4.41)

Moreover, we have

\|\mathfrak{m}\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}:=\mathfrak{m}_{T_{0}}\lesssim e^{-25T_{0}^{2}/b}T_{0}|\partial\Omega|,

(4.42)

where
b
was defined in (
0.4
).

Proof.

Step 1.

From Lemma
19
, we have
f(t,x,v)\geq 0
from assumption
f_{0}(x,v)\geq 0
.
From (
4.5
)-(
4.7
) and setting
t=NT_{0}
,
t_{*}=(N-1)T_{0}
,
k=2
, we can derive that

f(NT_{0},x,v)\geq\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)\leq\frac{T_{0}}{4}}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{1},v_{\mathbf{b}})\int_{\mathcal{V}_{1}}\int_{\mathcal{V}_{2}}\int_{\mathcal{V}_{3}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{3}\geq(N-1)T_{0}}f(t^{3},x^{3},v^{3})\{n(x^{3})\cdot v^{3}\}\mathrm{d}v^{3}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{2}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{1}.

(4.43)

Now we apply Proposition
4
on
v^{1}\in\mathcal{V}_{1}
and
v^{2}\in\mathcal{V}_{2}
with (
1.39
) and (
1.40
).
In order to have the bijective mapping with (
1.39
), we restrict the range of
v_{\mathbf{b}}^{1}
and
v_{\mathbf{b}}^{2}
as

\begin{split}&\mathcal{V}_{1,b}:=\{v_{\mathbf{b}}^{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}:x^{2}+\int^{t}_{t-t_{\mathbf{b}}}\big(v_{\mathbf{b}}^{1}+\int^{s}_{t-t_{\mathbf{b}}}-\nabla\Phi(X(\alpha;t^{1},x^{1},v^{1}))\mathrm{d}\alpha\big)\mathrm{d}s=x^{1}\},\\
&\mathcal{V}_{2,b}:=\{v_{\mathbf{b}}^{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}:x^{3}+\int^{t-t_{\mathbf{b}}}_{t-t_{\mathbf{b}}-t_{\mathbf{b}}^{1}}\big(v_{\mathbf{b}}^{2}+\int^{s}_{t-t_{\mathbf{b}}-t_{\mathbf{b}}^{1}}-\nabla\Phi(X(\alpha;t^{2},x^{2},v^{2}))\mathrm{d}\alpha\big)\mathrm{d}s=x^{2}\}.\end{split}

(4.44)

This implies two characteristic trajectories
X(\alpha;t^{1},x^{1},v^{1})
between
x^{1}
and
x^{2}
under
v_{\mathbf{b}}^{1}\in\mathcal{V}_{1,b}
, and
X(\alpha;t^{2},x^{2},v^{2})
between
x^{2}
and
x^{3}
under
v_{\mathbf{b}}^{2}\in\mathcal{V}_{2,b}
don’t cross the periodic boundary.

Therefore, we derive

\begin{split}\eqref{Doeblin_1 mag}&\geq\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)\leq\frac{T_{0}}{4}}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{1},v_{\mathbf{b}})\int_{0}^{T_{0}-t_{\mathbf{b}}}\int_{\partial\Omega}\underbrace{\frac{5B^{2}_{3}}{2-2\cos(B_{3}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{1})}\{n(x^{1})\cdot v^{1}\}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{2},v_{\mathbf{b}}^{1})}_{\eqref{Doeblin_2 mag}_{**}}\mathrm{d}S_{x^{2}}\mathrm{d}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{1}\\
&\ \ \ \ \ \ \times\int_{0}^{T_{0}-t_{\mathbf{b}}-t_{\mathbf{b}}^{1}}\int_{\partial\Omega}\underbrace{\frac{5B^{2}_{3}}{2-2\cos(B_{3}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{2})}\{n(x^{2})\cdot v^{2}\}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{3},v_{\mathbf{b}}^{2})}_{\eqref{Doeblin_2 mag}_{*}}\mathrm{d}S_{x^{3}}\mathrm{d}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{2}\\
&\ \ \ \ \ \ \times\int_{n(x^{3})\cdot v^{3}>0}f(t^{3},x^{3},v^{3})\{n(x^{3})\cdot v^{3}\}\mathrm{d}v^{3},\end{split}

(4.45)

where
t^{3}=NT_{0}-t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)-t_{\mathbf{b}}^{1}-t_{\mathbf{b}}^{2}
.

Step 2.
In order to bound the integrands of the first line of (
4.45
), we will further restrict integration regimes. Note that
x^{1}=x_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)
is given,
x^{2}
is free variables and
t^{2}\geq(N-1)T_{0}
.

Now we restrict the integral regimes of the variable
t_{\mathbf{b}}^{1}
and
t_{\mathbf{b}}^{2}
as

\begin{split}&\mathfrak{T}^{T_{0}}:=\Big\{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{1},t_{\mathbf{b}}^{2}\geq 0:T_{0}-t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)-\min\Big(t_{\mathbf{b}}(x^{3},v^{3}),\frac{T_{0}}{4}\Big)\leq t_{\mathbf{b}}^{1}+t_{\mathbf{b}}^{2}\leq T_{0}-t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)\Big\},\\
&\mathfrak{T}_{2}^{T_{0}}:=\Big\{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{2}\in[0,\infty):\frac{\pi}{2B_{3}}\leq t_{\mathbf{b}}^{2}\leq\frac{3\pi}{2B_{3}}\Big\},\end{split}

(4.46)

As a consequence of (
4.46
)
and
t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)\leq\frac{T_{0}}{4}
in (
4.45
), we will derive

\frac{T_{0}}{2}\leq T_{0}-t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)-\frac{T_{0}}{4}\leq t_{\mathbf{b}}^{1}+t_{\mathbf{b}}^{2}\leq T_{0},

(4.47)

and since
B_{3},T_{0}\geq 1
, the restriction on
t_{\mathbf{b}}^{2}
is reasonable.

Secondly, note that if
t_{\mathbf{b}}^{1},t_{\mathbf{b}}^{2}\in\mathfrak{T}^{T_{0}}
, we have

(N-1)T_{0}\leq t^{3}=NT_{0}-t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)-t_{\mathbf{b}}^{1}-t_{\mathbf{b}}^{2}\leq(N-1)T_{0}+\min\{t_{\mathbf{b}}(x^{3},v^{3}),\frac{T_{0}}{4}\}.

This implies that, for
y_{*}=X((N-1)T_{0};t^{3},x^{3},v^{3})
and
v_{*}=V((N-1)T_{0};t^{3},x^{3},v^{3})
, we have

t_{\mathbf{f}}(y_{*},v_{*})=t^{3}-(N-1)T_{0}=T_{0}-t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)-t_{\mathbf{b}}^{1}-t_{\mathbf{b}}^{2}\in\Big[0,\frac{T_{0}}{4}\Big],

(4.48)

where we use
t_{\mathbf{f}}(y_{*},v_{*})\leq t_{\mathbf{b}}(x^{3},v^{3})
since
x^{3}=x_{\mathbf{f}}(y_{*},v_{*})
.

Step 3.
For (
4.45
), we apply the restriction of integral regimes in (
4.44
) and (
4.46
), and obtain

\pi/2\leq B_{3}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{2}\leq 3\pi/2
and
\|x^{3}-x^{2}\|\leq\sqrt{2}
.
Then using (
0.4
), (
1.33
), (
2.61
) with
m=n=0
, we derive that

\begin{split}\eqref{Doeblin_2 mag}_{*}&=\frac{5B^{2}_{3}}{2-2\cos(B_{3}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{2})}\{n(x^{2})\cdot v^{2}\}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{3},v_{\mathbf{b}}^{2})\\
&\geq\frac{5B^{2}_{3}}{4}\times\frac{5\pi}{2B_{3}}\times e^{-\frac{1}{2b}\big(B^{2}_{3}+(\frac{15\pi}{2B_{3}})^{2}\big)}\gtrsim B_{3}e^{-\frac{1}{2b}B^{2}_{3}}.\end{split}

Similarly, we get

\begin{split}\eqref{Doeblin_2 mag}_{**}&=\frac{5B^{2}_{3}}{2-2\cos(B_{3}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{1})}\{n(x^{1})\cdot v^{1}\}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{2},v_{\mathbf{b}}^{1})\\
&\geq\frac{25B^{2}_{3}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{1}}{2-2\cos(B_{3}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{1})}\times e^{-\frac{1}{2b}\big(\frac{2B^{2}_{3}}{2-2\cos(B_{3}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{1})}+(5t_{\mathbf{b}}^{1})^{2}\big)}.\end{split}

Finally, applying the change of variable
(t_{\mathbf{b}}^{1},t_{\mathbf{b}}^{2})\mapsto(t_{\mathbf{b}}^{1},t_{\mathbf{b}}^{1}+t_{\mathbf{b}}^{2})
, we get

(
4.45
)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)\leq\frac{T_{0}}{4}}B_{3}e^{-\frac{1}{2b}B^{2}_{3}}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{1},v_{\mathbf{b}})\int_{\partial\Omega}\mathrm{d}S_{x^{3}}\int_{n(x^{3})\cdot v^{3}>0}\{n(x^{3})\cdot v^{3}\}\mathrm{d}v^{3}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \times\int^{T_{0}-t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)}_{T_{0}-t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)-\min\big(t_{\mathbf{b}}(x^{3},v^{3}),\frac{T_{0}}{4}\big)}\mathrm{d}(t_{\mathbf{b}}^{1}+t_{\mathbf{b}}^{2})f(NT_{0}-t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)-t_{\mathbf{b}}^{1}-t_{\mathbf{b}}^{2},x^{3},v^{3})

(4.49)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \times\int_{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{1}+t_{\mathbf{b}}^{2}-\frac{3\pi}{2B_{3}}}^{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{1}+t_{\mathbf{b}}^{2}-\frac{\pi}{2B_{3}}}\int_{\partial\Omega}\frac{25B^{2}_{3}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{1}}{2-2\cos(B_{3}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{1})}\times e^{-\frac{1}{2b}\big(\frac{2B^{2}_{3}}{2-2\cos(B_{3}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{1})}+(5t_{\mathbf{b}}^{1})^{2}\big)}\mathrm{d}S_{x^{2}}\mathrm{d}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{1}.

(4.50)

Now we focus on the integrand of (
4.49
). Recall (
4.46
), we have

NT_{0}-t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)-t_{\mathbf{b}}^{1}-t_{\mathbf{b}}^{2}-(N-1)T_{0}=T_{0}-t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)-t_{\mathbf{b}}^{1}-t_{\mathbf{b}}^{2}\in\Big[0,\min\Big(t_{\mathbf{b}}(x^{3},v^{3}),\frac{T_{0}}{4}\Big)\Big].

Setting
y_{*}=X((N-1)T_{0};t^{3},x^{3},v^{3})
,
v_{*}=V((N-1)T_{0};t^{3},x^{3},v^{3})
and
\alpha=T_{0}-t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)-t_{\mathbf{b}}^{1}-t_{\mathbf{b}}^{2}
,
we have

\eqref{lower1 mag}=\int^{\min\big(t_{\mathbf{b}}(x^{3},v^{3}),\frac{T_{0}}{4}\big)}_{0}f\big((N-1)T_{0},y_{*},v_{*}\big)\mathrm{d}\alpha.

(4.51)

From (
4.48
), we have
t_{\mathbf{f}}(y_{*},v_{*})\in\big[0,\frac{T_{0}}{4}\big]
.
Next, from (
4.47
) we can deduce that

\frac{T_{0}}{2}-\frac{3\pi}{2B_{3}}\leq t_{\mathbf{b}}^{1}\leq T_{0}-\frac{\pi}{2B_{3}}.

Picking
T_{0}>10
and
B_{3}\geq 1
, we get
T_{0}/5\leq t_{\mathbf{b}}^{1}\leq T_{0}
. Note that the time gap for
t_{\mathbf{b}}^{1}
is
\pi/B_{3}
and
2-2\cos(B_{3}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{1})
is periodic, we derive

\eqref{lower12 mag}\gtrsim e^{-\frac{1}{2b}(5t_{\mathbf{b}}^{1})^{2}}\geq e^{-25T_{0}^{2}/b}.

Now applying (
1.3
), we conclude that

\eqref{Doeblin_2 mag}\gtrsim\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)\leq\frac{T_{0}}{4}}e^{-25T_{0}^{2}/b}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{1},v_{\mathbf{b}})\iint_{\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{f}}(y,v)\in[0,\frac{T_{0}}{4}]}f((N-1)T_{0},y,v)\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}y.

We conclude (
4.40
) by setting

\mathfrak{m}(x,v):=\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)\leq\frac{T_{0}}{4}}e^{-25T_{0}^{2}/b}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{1},v_{\mathbf{b}}).

(4.52)

Since
\mathfrak{m}(x,v)
is equal to (
4.34
) used in the gravitational case,
the proof for (
4.41
) and (
4.42
) follows from Proposition
9
and so the proof is omitted.
∎

4.3.
Proof of weighted
L^{1}
-Estimates

Now we establish the uniform estimate of the following energy:

{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|f\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}:=\|f\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}+\frac{4\mathfrak{m}_{T_{0}}}{\varphi(\frac{T_{0}}{4})}\Big({1+\frac{1}{\delta T_{0}}}\Big)\times\|\varphi(t_{\mathbf{f}})f\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}},

(4.53)

with
\|\mathfrak{m}\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}:=\mathfrak{m}_{T_{0}}
(see (
4.36
)) and
\varphi
defined in (
4.54
).

Here we first introduce the weight function
\varphi
.

Definition 4
.

For
0<\delta<\infty
, we set

\begin{split}&\varphi(\tau):=e^{\delta\tau}.\end{split}

(4.54)

First, we check
\varphi
satisfies (
4.8
):

\begin{split}&\int_{1}^{\infty}e^{-\frac{1}{2b}\tau^{2}}e^{\delta\tau}\mathrm{d}\tau\lesssim\int_{1}^{\infty}e^{-\frac{1}{2b}(\tau-b\delta)^{2}}\mathrm{d}\tau<\infty.\end{split}

Second, we notice that,

\varphi(0)=1.

(4.55)

Finally, we check, for
\tau\geq 0
,

\begin{split}&\varphi^{\prime}(\tau)=\delta\varphi(\tau).\end{split}

(4.56)

Lemma 20
.

Choose
T_{0}>1
such that

\delta T_{0}>1,\ \ 4C(1+T_{0})(1+\delta T_{0})(\delta T_{0})^{-1}\Big(\varphi(\frac{T_{0}}{4})\Big)^{-1}\leq\frac{1}{2}.

(4.57)

Suppose
f
solves (
4.1
)-(
4.3
) for either (
0.5
) or (
0.6
), for all
N\in\mathbb{N}
,

\begin{split}&\ \ \ \ \|f(NT_{0})\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}+\frac{4\mathfrak{m}_{T_{0}}}{\varphi(\frac{T_{0}}{4})}\Big\{\|\varphi(t_{\mathbf{f}})f(NT_{0})\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}+\frac{1}{\delta T_{0}}\|\varphi(t_{\mathbf{f}})f(NT_{0})\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}\Big\}\\
&\leq\big\{1-\mathfrak{m}_{T_{0}}+\mathfrak{m}_{T_{0}}\frac{4C(1+T_{0})(1+\delta T_{0})}{\delta T_{0}\varphi(\frac{T_{0}}{4})}\big\}\times\|f((N-1)T_{0})\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}\\
&\hskip 85.35826pt+\frac{4\mathfrak{m}_{T_{0}}}{\varphi(\frac{T_{0}}{4})}\Big\{\frac{1}{2}\|\varphi(t_{\mathbf{f}})f((N-1)T_{0})\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}+\frac{1}{\delta T_{0}}\|\varphi(t_{\mathbf{f}})f((N-1)T_{0})\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}\Big\}.\end{split}

(4.58)

Proof.

Applying Lemma
18
on
f(t,x,v)
which solves (
4.1
)-(
4.3
), with
\varphi
in (
4.54
).
From (
4.55
) and (
4.56
), we derive that, for
(N-1)T_{0}\leq t_{*}\leq t=NT_{0}
,

\|\varphi(t_{\mathbf{f}})f(NT_{0})\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}+\frac{3}{4}\int^{NT_{0}}_{t_{*}}|f(s)|_{L^{1}_{\gamma_{+}}}\mathrm{d}s\leq\|\varphi(t_{\mathbf{f}})f(t_{*})\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}+C(1+T_{0})\|f(t_{*})\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}.

(4.59)

For
(N-1)T_{0}=t_{*}\leq t=NT_{0}
,

\begin{split}&\ \ \ \ \|\varphi(t_{\mathbf{f}})f(NT_{0})\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}+\int^{NT_{0}}_{(N-1)T_{0}}\big\{\|\varphi^{\prime}(t_{\mathbf{f}})f(s)\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}+\frac{3}{4}|f(s)|_{L^{1}_{\gamma_{+}}}\big\}\mathrm{d}s\\
&\leq\|\varphi(t_{\mathbf{f}})f((N-1)T_{0})\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}+C(1+T_{0})\|f((N-1)T_{0})\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}.\end{split}

(4.60)

From (
4.10
), (
4.56
) and (
4.59
), we derive that,

\begin{split}\int^{NT_{0}}_{(N-1)T_{0}}\|\varphi^{\prime}(t_{\mathbf{f}})f(t_{*})\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}\mathrm{d}t_{*}&=\delta\int^{NT_{0}}_{(N-1)T_{0}}\|\varphi(t_{\mathbf{f}})f(t_{*})\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}\mathrm{d}t_{*}\\
