&\geq\delta\int^{NT_{0}}_{(N-1)T_{0}}\big\{\|\varphi(t_{\mathbf{f}})f(NT_{0})\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}-C(1+T_{0})\|f(t_{*})\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}\big\}\mathrm{d}t_{*}\\
&\geq\delta T_{0}\|\varphi(t_{\mathbf{f}})f(NT_{0})\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}-\delta C(1+T_{0})T_{0}\|f((N-1)T_{0})\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}.\end{split}

Applying the above bound on (
4.60
), we have

\begin{split}&\ \ \ \ \|\varphi(t_{\mathbf{f}})f(NT_{0})\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}+\delta T_{0}\|\varphi(t_{\mathbf{f}})f(NT_{0})\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}+\frac{3}{4}\int^{NT_{0}}_{(N-1)T_{0}}|f(s)|_{L^{1}_{\gamma_{+}}}\mathrm{d}s\\
&\leq\|\varphi(t_{\mathbf{f}})f((N-1)T_{0})\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}+C(1+T_{0})(1+\delta T_{0})\|f((N-1)T_{0})\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}.\end{split}

(4.61)

From (
4.55
) and (
4.56
), we obtain

\mathbf{1}_{t_{\mathbf{f}}\geq\frac{T_{0}}{4}}\leq\big(\varphi(\frac{T_{0}}{4})\big)^{-1}\varphi(t_{\mathbf{f}}).

Combining the above bound with (
4.35
), we have

\begin{split}&\|f(NT_{0})\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}\leq(1-\mathfrak{m}_{T_{0}})\|f((N-1)T_{0})\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}+2\mathfrak{m}_{T_{0}}\big(\varphi(\frac{T_{0}}{4})\big)^{-1}\|\varphi(t_{\mathbf{f}})f((N-1)T_{0})\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}.\end{split}

(4.62)

For
T_{0}>0
in (
4.57
) and considering
\eqref{L1_coerc'}+\frac{4\mathfrak{m}_{T_{0}}}{\delta T_{0}\varphi(\frac{T_{0}}{4})}\eqref{energy:phi1}
, we deduce (
4.58
).

For the magnet field case, since we have proved Proposition
10
and Lemma
20
, the rest of proof follows from the gravitational case.
∎

Now we are well equipped to prove Theorem
7
and
7
.

Proof of Theorem
7
.

We first note that
t_{\mathbf{f}}(x,v)\leq t_{\mathbf{f}}(x_{\mathbf{b}},v_{\mathbf{b}})\lesssim|n(x_{\mathbf{b}})\cdot v_{\mathbf{b}}|\leq|v_{\mathbf{b}}|
and
|v|^{2}+2\Phi(x)=|v_{\mathbf{b}}|^{2}
shown in Lemma
3
. Thus, we deduce

t_{\mathbf{f}}\lesssim(|v|^{2}+2\Phi(x))^{1/2}.

Since
\varphi(\tau)=e^{\delta\tau}
with
0<\delta<\infty
, we derive that

|\varphi(t_{\mathbf{f}})|\lesssim\big|\varphi\big((|v|^{2}+2\Phi(x))^{1/2}\big)\big|=e^{\delta(|v|^{2}+2\Phi(x))^{1/2}}.

(4.63)

Now fix
T_{0}
in (
4.57
) and recall norms of
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\cdot\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}
in (
4.53
),

{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|f\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}=\|f\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}+\frac{4\mathfrak{m}_{T_{0}}}{\varphi(\frac{T_{0}}{4})}\Big({1+\frac{1}{\delta T_{0}}}\Big)\times\|\varphi(t_{\mathbf{f}})f\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}},

From (
4.58
), and setting

\mathfrak{R}:=\max\big\{1-\mathfrak{m}_{T_{0}}(1-\frac{4C(1+T_{0})(1+\delta T_{0})}{\delta T_{0}\varphi(\frac{T_{0}}{4})}),\ \frac{1+\delta T_{0}/2}{1+\delta T_{0}}\big\}
,
we derive for all
N\in\mathbb{N}
,

\begin{split}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|f(NT_{0})\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}\leq\mathfrak{R}\times{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|f((N-1)T_{0})\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}.\end{split}

(4.64)

Moreover, from
T_{0}
in (
4.57
), we have

\begin{split}&\frac{1+\delta T_{0}/2}{1+\delta T_{0}}=1-\frac{\delta T_{0}}{2(1+\delta T_{0})}<1,\\
&1-\mathfrak{m}_{T_{0}}(1-\frac{4C(1+T_{0})(1+\delta T_{0})}{\delta T_{0}\varphi(\frac{T_{0}}{4})})\leq 1-\frac{1}{2}\mathfrak{m}_{T_{0}}<1.\end{split}

(4.65)

For
t\geq 0
, we can choose
N_{*}\in\mathbb{N}
such that
t\in[N_{*}T_{0},(N_{*}+1)T_{0}]
.
Then applying (
4.9
), (
4.10
) first, and using (
4.64
) successively, we conclude that

\begin{split}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|f(t)\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}&\lesssim{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|f(N_{*}T_{0})\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}\leq\mathfrak{R}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|f((N_{*}-1)T_{0})\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}\leq\cdots\leq\mathfrak{R}^{N_{*}}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|f(0)\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}.\end{split}

(4.66)

Note that
N_{*}T_{0}\leq t\leq(N_{*}+1)T_{0}
and
\mathfrak{R}<1
in (
4.65
), we deduce

\mathfrak{R}^{N_{*}}\leq\mathfrak{R}^{\frac{t}{T_{0}}-1}\lesssim\big(\mathfrak{R}^{\frac{1}{T_{0}}}\big)^{t}.

(4.67)

Since
T_{0}
is fixed, we have

{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|f(t)\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}\leq\eqref{est:|||1}\lesssim\big(\mathfrak{R}^{\frac{1}{T_{0}}}\big)^{t}\times{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|f(0)\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}.

(4.68)

Now we choose the following constant
\Lambda
, such that

\Lambda=-\frac{1}{T_{0}}\ln(\mathfrak{R}).

(4.69)

Therefore, we derive that

e^{-\Lambda}=\mathfrak{R}^{\frac{1}{T_{0}}}.

From (
1.3
) and the assumption

\|e^{\theta^{\prime}(|v|^{2}+2\Phi(x))}f_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}<\infty
, we have

{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|f(0)\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}\lesssim\|e^{\theta^{\prime}(|v|^{2}+2\Phi(x))}f_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}+\|\varphi(t_{\mathbf{f}})f_{0}\|_{1}.

Using (
4.68
), (
4.69
), (
4.63
), and the assumption
\|e^{\delta(|v|^{2}+2\Phi(x))^{1/2}}f_{0}\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}<\infty
, we finally prove Theorem
7
via

\|f(t)\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}\leq{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|f(t)\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}\lesssim e^{-\Lambda t}\{\|e^{\theta^{\prime}(|v|^{2}+2\Phi(x))}f_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}+\|e^{\delta(|v|^{2}+2\Phi(x))^{1/2}}f_{0}\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}\}.

∎

Proof of Theorem
7
.

Recall from Lemma
19
and a null flux condition of the boundary, we conclude (
0.11
) by taking integration over
(t_{*},t)\times\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
.

Next, to show (
4.4
) , we fix
T_{0}
in (
4.57
) and recall norm of
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\cdot\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}
in (
4.53
).
From (
4.58
), for all
N\in\mathbb{N}
, we obtain

{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|f(NT_{0})\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}\leq{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|f((N-1)T_{0})\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}\leq\cdots\leq{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|f(0)\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}.

The rest of proof is similar to the proof of Theorem

.
∎

5.
Continuity on
\mathfrak{J}(x)

In this section, we first show the flux function
\mathfrak{J}(x)
is continuous. Then we use the continuity to obtain the uniform upper bound of the residual measures in the stochastic cycles. We outline the strategy here: We begin by showing boundary outgoing flux
\mathfrak{J}(x)
for any steady state is continuous on the boundary
\partial\Omega
.
Assume any sequence
x^{i}\rightarrow x\in\partial\Omega
, we expand
\mathfrak{J}(x^{i})
and
\mathfrak{J}(x)
into the integration forms:

\mathfrak{J}(x)=\int_{n(x)\cdot v>0}F(x,v)\{n(x)\cdot v\}\mathrm{d}v,\ \ \mathfrak{J}(x^{i})=\int_{n(x^{i})\cdot v>0}F(x^{i},v)\{n(x^{i})\cdot v\}\mathrm{d}v.

We introduce the following set:

\Gamma^{\delta}=\{(x,v)\in\gamma:|n(x)\cdot v|\leq\delta\ \text{or}\ |v|\geq\frac{1}{\delta}\},

(5.1)

and write
\mathfrak{J}(x)
as

\mathfrak{J}(x)=\int_{v\in\gamma_{+}\cap\Gamma^{\delta}}F(x,v)\{n(x)\cdot v\}\mathrm{d}v+\int_{v\in\gamma_{+}\backslash\Gamma^{\delta}}F(x,v)\{n(x)\cdot v\}\mathrm{d}v.

We can control the first part by

\int_{\Gamma^{\delta}}\mu_{\Theta}(x_{\mathbf{b}},v)\{n(x_{\mathbf{b}})\cdot v\}\mathrm{d}v\lesssim\delta^{2}
with
0<\delta\ll 1
(see Lemma
22
).
For the second part, we first apply the the change of variable
v\mapsto(t_{\mathbf{b}},S_{x_{\mathbf{b}}})
on the integration forms.
Then we compare and obtain the pointwise convergence on the integrands
where the key is that
v
and
v_{\mathbf{b}}
are continuous.
Applying the dominated convergence theorem, we derive

\mathfrak{J}(x)\rightarrow\mathfrak{J}(x^{i})
and prove the continuity in Lemma
23
.

Then we consider the boundary outgoing flux
\mathfrak{J}(x)
on non-negative steady state
F_{s}(x,v)
with unit mass.
Since the boundary is compact and
\mathfrak{J}(x)
is continuous,
\mathfrak{J}(x)
achieves an infimum
L\geq 0
on
\partial\Omega
.
Applying the change of variable
v\mapsto(t_{\mathbf{b}},S_{x_{\mathbf{b}}})
on the integration form of
\mathfrak{J}(x)
, we can show that if
L=0
,
\mathfrak{J}(x)
has to be zero almost everywhere. This will contradict with the assumption that
F_{s}(x,v)
has unit mass. Thus, we deduce that
L>0
.

Now we consider the residual measure as part of the stochastic cycle on
\mathfrak{J}(x)
and obtain

\mathfrak{J}(x)\geq\int_{\prod_{j=0}^{i-1}\mathcal{V}_{j}}\mathbf{1}_{0<t_{i}\leq t}\ \mathrm{d}\sigma_{i-1}\cdots\mathrm{d}\sigma_{1}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{0}\times\mathfrak{J}(x^{i}).

(5.2)

Using the
L^{\infty}
-Estimates for
\mathfrak{J}(x)
on the left side, we write
\mathfrak{J}(x)\leq U
.
On the right hand, we apply the lower bound on
\mathfrak{J}(x^{i})
as
\mathfrak{J}(x^{i})\geq L
.
Therefore, we establish the uniform estimate on residual measure

\begin{split}\int_{\prod_{j=0}^{i-1}\mathcal{V}_{j}}\mathbf{1}_{0<t_{i}\leq t}\ \mathrm{d}\sigma_{i-1}\cdots\mathrm{d}\sigma_{1}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{0}\leq L^{-1}U.\end{split}

(5.3)

For the magnet field case, we also start with showing boundary outgoing flux
\mathfrak{J}(x)
for any steady state is continuous on the boundary
\partial\Omega
.
For any sequence
x^{i}\rightarrow x\in\partial\Omega
, we expand
\mathfrak{J}(x^{i})
and
\mathfrak{J}(x)
into the integration forms and apply the change of variable
v\mapsto(t_{\mathbf{b}},S_{x_{\mathbf{b}}})
on them.

The main difficulty still comes from the Jacobian term
\frac{5B^{2}_{3}}{2-2\cos(B_{3}t^{i}_{\mathbf{b}})}
in the integrands. Thus, we need to introduce the following set:

\Gamma^{\delta}=\{(x,v)\in\gamma:v\in\mathcal{V}^{B_{\delta}}(x)\ \text{or}\ |v|\geq\frac{1}{\delta}\}.

(5.4)

Now we split
(x,v)
into two parts
\Gamma^{\delta}
and
\gamma_{+}\backslash\Gamma^{\delta}
.
Though the Jacobian term can be large when
(x,v)\in\Gamma^{\delta}
, we use

\int_{\Gamma^{\delta}}\mu_{\Theta}(x_{\mathbf{b}},v)\{n(x_{\mathbf{b}})\cdot v\}\mathrm{d}v\lesssim\delta
(see Lemma
25
) to control
\int_{v\in\gamma_{+}\cap\Gamma^{\delta}}F(x,v)\{n(x)\cdot v\}\mathrm{d}v
.
For
(x,v)\in\gamma_{+}\backslash\Gamma^{\delta}
, we compute and derive the pointwise convergence on the integrands in forms of
\mathfrak{J}(x)
and
\mathfrak{J}(x^{i})
.
Then we apply the dominated convergence theorem to deduce the continuity in Lemma
26
.

5.1.
Gravitational case

For the sake of simplicity, we have abused the notations temporarily in section
5.1
and
5.2
:
all superscript represent some sequences instead of the notations in stochastic cycles.

Suppose
F(x,v)
solves (
0.7
) and (
0.2
) and
\mathfrak{J}(x)
defined in (
2.2
), for
x\in\partial\Omega
,

\begin{split}\mathfrak{J}(x)&=\int_{n(x)\cdot v>0}F(x,v)\{n(x)\cdot v\}\mathrm{d}v\\
&=\sum_{m,n\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{v\in v^{m,n}}F(x,v)\{n(x)\cdot v\}\mathrm{d}v\\
&=\sum_{m,n\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{\partial\Omega}\int^{\infty}_{0}\underbrace{\mu_{\Theta}(x_{\mathbf{b}},v)\mathfrak{J}(x_{\mathbf{b}})\{n(x)\cdot v\}\Big\{\frac{|n(x_{\mathbf{b}})\cdot v_{\mathbf{b}}|}{(t_{\mathbf{b}})^{3}}d_{0}\Big\}}_{\mathfrak{J}^{m,n}(x)}\mathrm{d}t_{\mathbf{b}}\mathrm{d}x_{\mathbf{b}},\end{split}

(5.5)

where
(d_{0})^{-1}=\det\big[-{Id}_{3\times 3}-\frac{1}{t_{\mathbf{b}}^{1}}\int^{t^{1}-t_{\mathbf{b}}^{1}}_{t^{1}}\int^{s}_{t^{1}}-\nabla_{v^{1}}X\cdot\nabla\nabla\phi\mathrm{d}\alpha\mathrm{d}s\big]
, and
v^{m,n}
defined in (
1.18
).

Now we build the sequence
\{x^{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
with
\|x^{i}-x\|\rightarrow 0
as
i\rightarrow\infty
. Similar, we have

\mathfrak{J}(x^{i})=\sum_{m,n\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{\partial\Omega}\int^{\infty}_{0}\underbrace{\mu_{\Theta}(x^{i}_{\mathbf{b}},v^{i})\mathfrak{J}(x^{i}_{\mathbf{b}})\{n(x^{i})\cdot v^{i}\}\Big\{\frac{|n(x^{i}_{\mathbf{b}})\cdot v^{i}_{\mathbf{b}}|}{(t^{i}_{\mathbf{b}})^{3}}d_{i,0}\Big\}}_{\mathfrak{J}^{m,n}(x^{i})}\mathrm{d}t^{i}_{\mathbf{b}}\mathrm{d}x^{i}_{\mathbf{b}},

(5.6)

where
t^{i}_{\mathbf{b}}=t_{\mathbf{b}}(x^{i},v^{i})
,
v^{i}_{\mathbf{b}}=v_{\mathbf{b}}(x^{i},v^{i})
and
x^{i}_{\mathbf{b}}=x_{\mathbf{b}}(x^{i},v^{i})
.

Lemma 21
.

Consider
(x,v),(x^{i},v^{i})\in\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
, and their backward stochastic cycles with
(X,V)
in (
1.1
):

(t_{\mathbf{b}},x_{\mathbf{b}}+(m,n,0),v_{\mathbf{b}}),\ \ (t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i},x_{\mathbf{b}}^{i}+(m,n,0),v_{\mathbf{b}}^{i}).

Suppose
m,n\in\mathbb{Z}
are fixed,
t^{i}_{\mathbf{b}}=t_{\mathbf{b}}
,
x^{i}_{\mathbf{b}}=x_{\mathbf{b}}
, and
|x^{i}-x|\rightarrow 0
as
i\rightarrow\infty
, then

(1)

v^{i}\rightarrow v
as
i\rightarrow\infty
,

(2)

v^{i}_{\mathbf{b}}(x^{i},v^{i})\rightarrow v_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)
as
i\rightarrow\infty
,

(3)

\mathfrak{J}^{m,n}(x^{i})\rightarrow\mathfrak{J}^{m,n}(x)
as
i\rightarrow\infty
.

Proof.

From fixed
x_{\mathbf{b}},t_{\mathbf{b}}
and
t
with
t_{\mathbf{b}}=t^{i}_{\mathbf{b}}
and
x_{\mathbf{b}}=x^{i}_{\mathbf{b}}
, we have

x_{\mathbf{b}}+\int^{t}_{t-t_{\mathbf{b}}}(v-\int^{t}_{s}-\nabla\Phi(X(\alpha,t,x,v))\mathrm{d}\alpha)\mathrm{d}s=x.

(5.7)

x_{\mathbf{b}}+\int^{t}_{t-t_{\mathbf{b}}}(v^{i}-\int^{t}_{s}-\nabla\Phi(X(\alpha,t,x^{i},v^{i}))\mathrm{d}\alpha)\mathrm{d}s=x^{i}.

(5.8)

Subtracting (
5.7
) from (
5.8
), we deduce

t_{\mathbf{b}}(v-v^{i})+\underbrace{\int^{t}_{t-t_{\mathbf{b}}}\int^{t}_{s}\nabla\Phi(X(\alpha,t,x,v))\mathrm{d}\alpha\mathrm{d}s-\int^{t}_{t-t_{\mathbf{b}}}\int^{t}_{s}\nabla\Phi(X(\alpha,t,x^{i},v^{i}))\mathrm{d}\alpha\mathrm{d}s}_{\eqref{xvxivi}^{*}}=x-x^{i}.

(5.9)

To control
\eqref{xvxivi}^{*}
, we need to estimate
\big|\nabla_{x}\int^{t}_{t-t_{\mathbf{b}}}\int^{s}_{t}\nabla\Phi(X(\alpha,t,x,v))\mathrm{d}\alpha\mathrm{d}s\big|
.
From (
1.1
), we have

\begin{split}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}s}|\nabla_{x}X(s;t,x,v)|&\lesssim|\nabla_{x}V(s;t,x,v)|,\\
\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}s}|\nabla_{x}V(s;t,x,v)|&\lesssim|\nabla\nabla_{X}\phi(X(s;t,x,v))||\nabla_{x}X(s;t,x,v)|\\
&\lesssim\varrho_{2}e^{-\varrho_{1}X_{3}(s;t,x,v)}|\nabla_{x}X(s;t,x,v)|.\end{split}

Since
\nabla_{x}X(t;t,x,v)=\nabla_{x}x=Id_{3\times 3}
,
\nabla_{x}V(t;t,x,v)=\nabla_{x}v=0
and
t-t_{\mathbf{b}}\leq s\leq t
, we get

\begin{split}|\nabla_{x}X(s;t,x,v)|&\lesssim 1+\int^{t}_{s}|\nabla_{x}V(s_{1};t,x,v)|\mathrm{d}s_{1}\\
&\lesssim 1+\int^{t}_{s}\int^{t}_{s_{1}}\varrho_{2}e^{-\varrho_{1}X_{3}(s_{2};t,x,v)}|\nabla_{x}X(s_{2};t,x,v)|\mathrm{d}s_{2}\mathrm{d}s_{1}.\end{split}

(5.10)

Using the Fubini’s theorem, we derive

\begin{split}\eqref{Xx first}&\lesssim 1+\int^{t}_{s}\int^{s_{2}}_{s}\varrho_{2}e^{-\varrho_{1}X_{3}(s_{2};t,x,v)}|\nabla_{x}X(s_{2};t,x,v)|\mathrm{d}s_{1}\mathrm{d}s_{2}\\
&\lesssim 1+\int^{t}_{s}\varrho_{2}(s_{2}-s)e^{-\varrho_{1}X_{3}(s_{2};t,x,v)}|\nabla_{x}X(s_{2};t,x,v)|\mathrm{d}s_{2}.\end{split}

Using the Gronwall’s inequality, for
t-t_{\mathbf{b}}\leq s\leq t
,

\begin{split}&\ \ \ \ |\nabla_{x}X(s;t,x,v)|\\
&\lesssim\exp\big(\int^{t}_{s}\varrho_{2}(s_{2}-s)e^{-\varrho_{1}X_{3}(s_{2};t,x,v)}\mathrm{d}s_{2}\big)\\
&\leq\exp\Big(\int^{t}_{t-t_{\mathbf{b}}}\varrho_{2}\big(s_{2}-(t-t_{\mathbf{b}})\big)e^{-\varrho_{1}X_{3}(s_{2};t,x,v)}\mathrm{d}s_{2}\Big).\end{split}

(5.11)

Using (
1.30
), we derive

|\nabla_{x}X(s;t,x,v)|\leq e^{\varrho_{2}/\varrho_{1}}.

(5.12)

From direct computation, we have,

\nabla_{x}\big[\nabla\Phi(X(\alpha,t,x,v)\big]=\nabla_{x}X(\alpha,t,x,v)\cdot\nabla\nabla\Phi(X)=\nabla_{x}X(\alpha,t,x,v)\cdot\nabla\nabla\phi,

and from (
5.12
) and Fubini’s theorem, we have

\begin{split}&\ \ \ \ \Big|\int^{t-t_{\mathbf{b}}}_{t}\int^{s}_{t}-\nabla_{x}X\cdot\nabla^{2}\phi(X(\alpha;t,x,v))\mathrm{d}\alpha\mathrm{d}s\Big|\\
&\leq\int^{t}_{t-t_{\mathbf{b}}}\int^{t}_{s}|\nabla^{2}\phi(X)||\nabla_{x}X(\alpha;t,x,v)|\mathrm{d}\alpha\mathrm{d}s\\
&\leq\int^{t}_{t-t_{\mathbf{b}}}\int^{t}_{s}\varrho_{2}e^{-\varrho_{1}X_{3}}e^{\varrho_{2}/\varrho_{1}}\mathrm{d}\alpha\mathrm{d}s\\
&\leq\int^{t}_{t-t_{\mathbf{b}}}\varrho_{2}\big(\alpha-(t-t_{\mathbf{b}})\big)e^{-\varrho_{1}X_{3}}e^{\varrho_{2}/\varrho_{1}}\mathrm{d}\alpha\leq e^{\varrho_{2}/\varrho_{1}}\frac{\varrho_{2}}{\varrho_{1}},\end{split}

(5.13)

where the last inequality holds from (
1.30
).
Applying Mean value theorem, we get

\begin{split}|\eqref{xvxivi}^{*}|&\leq\|\nabla_{v}\int^{t}_{t-t_{\mathbf{b}}}\int^{t}_{s}\nabla\Phi(X(\alpha,t,x,v))\mathrm{d}\alpha\mathrm{d}s\|_{\infty}\times|v-v^{i}|\\
&+\|\nabla_{x}\int^{t}_{t-t_{\mathbf{b}}}\int^{t}_{s}\nabla\Phi(X(\alpha,t,x^{i},v^{i}))\mathrm{d}\alpha\mathrm{d}s\|_{\infty}\times|x-x^{i}|.\end{split}

Using (
5.13
), we deduce

|\eqref{xvxivi}^{*}|\leq e^{\varrho_{2}/\varrho_{1}}\frac{\varrho_{2}}{\varrho_{1}}t_{\mathbf{b}}\times|v-v^{i}|+e^{\varrho_{2}/\varrho_{1}}\frac{\varrho_{2}}{\varrho_{1}}\times|x-x^{i}|.

Combining the above with (
5.9
), we derive

|t_{\mathbf{b}}(v-v^{i})|\leq e^{\varrho_{2}/\varrho_{1}}\frac{\varrho_{2}}{\varrho_{1}}t_{\mathbf{b}}\times|v-v^{i}|+e^{\varrho_{2}/\varrho_{1}}\frac{\varrho_{2}}{\varrho_{1}}\times|x-x^{i}|+|x-x^{i}|.

Since
e^{\varrho_{2}/\varrho_{1}}\frac{\varrho_{2}}{\varrho_{1}}\ll 1
, we conclude that

|v-v^{i}|\lesssim\frac{1}{t_{\mathbf{b}}}|x-x^{i}|,

(5.14)

which implies, for fixed
t_{\mathbf{b}}\neq 0
,

|v-v^{i}|\rightarrow 0
as
i\rightarrow\infty
.

Now, we begin to prove the second part. From fixed
t_{\mathbf{b}}
, we have

v_{\mathbf{b}}+\int^{t}_{t-t_{\mathbf{b}}}-\nabla\Phi(X(s,t,x,v))\mathrm{d}s=v.

(5.15)

v^{i}_{\mathbf{b}}+\int^{t}_{t-t_{\mathbf{b}}}-\nabla\Phi(X(s,t,x^{i},v^{i}))\mathrm{d}s=v^{i}.

(5.16)

Subtracting (
5.15
) from (
5.16
), we deduce

(v_{\mathbf{b}}-v^{i}_{\mathbf{b}})-\underbrace{\int^{t}_{t-t_{\mathbf{b}}}\nabla\Phi(X(s,t,x,v))\mathrm{d}s+\int^{t}_{t-t_{\mathbf{b}}}\nabla\Phi(X(s,t,x^{i},v^{i}))\mathrm{d}s}_{\eqref{vvivbvib}^{*}}=v-v^{i}.

(5.17)

To control
\eqref{vvivbvib}^{*}
, we need to estimate
\big|\nabla_{x,v}\int^{t}_{t-t_{\mathbf{b}}}\nabla\Phi(X(s,t,x,v))\mathrm{d}s\big|
.
From direct computation, we have

\big|\nabla_{x,v}\int^{t}_{t-t_{\mathbf{b}}}\nabla\Phi(X(s,t,x,v))\mathrm{d}s\big|=\big|\int^{t}_{t-t_{\mathbf{b}}}\nabla\nabla\phi\cdot\nabla_{x,v}X(s,t,x,v)\mathrm{d}s\big|.

Using (
1.26
), (
1.30
) and (
5.12
), we get

\begin{split}&\big|\int^{t}_{t-t_{\mathbf{b}}}\nabla\nabla\phi\cdot\nabla_{x}X(s,t,x,v)\mathrm{d}s\big|\lesssim\big|\int^{t}_{t-t_{\mathbf{b}}}\varrho_{2}e^{-\varrho_{1}X_{3}}e^{\varrho_{2}/\varrho_{1}}\mathrm{d}s\big|\lesssim\varrho_{2}e^{\varrho_{2}/\varrho_{1}},\\
&\big|\int^{t}_{t-t_{\mathbf{b}}}\nabla\nabla\phi\cdot\nabla_{v}X(s,t,x,v)\mathrm{d}s\big|\lesssim\big|\int^{t}_{t-t_{\mathbf{b}}}\varrho_{2}e^{-\varrho_{1}X_{3}}|t-s|e^{\varrho_{2}/\varrho_{1}}\mathrm{d}s\big|\lesssim e^{\varrho_{2}/\varrho_{1}}\frac{\varrho_{2}}{\varrho_{1}}.\end{split}

(5.18)

Applying Mean value theorem, we get

\begin{split}|\eqref{vvivbvib}^{*}|&\leq\|\nabla_{v}\int^{t}_{t-t_{\mathbf{b}}}\nabla\Phi(X(s,t,x,v))\mathrm{d}s\|_{\infty}\times|v-v^{i}|\\
&+\|\nabla_{x}\int^{t}_{t-t_{\mathbf{b}}}\nabla\Phi(X(s,t,x,v))\mathrm{d}s\|_{\infty}\times|x-x^{i}|.\end{split}

Using (
5.18
), we deduce

|\eqref{xvxivi}^{*}|\leq e^{\varrho_{2}/\varrho_{1}}\frac{\varrho_{2}}{\varrho_{1}}\times|v-v^{i}|+\varrho_{2}e^{\varrho_{2}/\varrho_{1}}\times|x-x^{i}|.

Combining the above with (
5.17
), we derive

|v_{\mathbf{b}}-v^{i}_{\mathbf{b}}|\leq e^{\varrho_{2}/\varrho_{1}}\frac{\varrho_{2}}{\varrho_{1}}\times|v-v^{i}|+\varrho_{2}e^{\varrho_{2}/\varrho_{1}}\times|x-x^{i}|+|v-v^{i}|.

Using
\varrho_{2}\ll 1
,
\varrho_{1}>1
and
(x^{i},v^{i})\rightarrow(x,v)
as
i\rightarrow\infty
, we conclude, for fixed
t_{\mathbf{b}}\neq 0
,

|v_{\mathbf{b}}-v^{i}_{\mathbf{b}}|\rightarrow 0\ \text{as}\ i\rightarrow\infty.

Finally, from the implicit function theorem,
X(s,t,x,v)
is a smooth function.
Since
m,n
are fixed,
t^{i}_{\mathbf{b}}=t_{\mathbf{b}}
and
x^{i}_{\mathbf{b}}=x_{\mathbf{b}}
,
we deduce that
d_{i,0}\rightarrow d_{0}
as
i\rightarrow\infty
from the first two parts.
Consider (
5.5
) and (
5.6
), we derive

\mathfrak{J}^{m,n}(x^{i})\rightarrow\mathfrak{J}^{m,n}(x)\ \text{as}\ |x^{i}-x|\rightarrow 0.

(5.19)

∎

Lemma 22
.

Define

\Gamma^{\delta}=\{(x,v)\in\gamma:|n(x)\cdot v|\leq\delta\ \text{or}\ |v|\geq\frac{1}{\delta}\}.

Consider
(X,V)
in (
1.1
),
then for
0<\delta\ll 1
and
x\in\partial\Omega
,

\int_{\Gamma^{\delta}}\mu_{\Theta}(x_{\mathbf{b}},v)\{n(x_{\mathbf{b}})\cdot v\}\mathrm{d}v\lesssim\delta^{2}.

(5.20)

Proof.

From (
2.42
), we can obtain

\int_{|n(x)\cdot v|\lesssim\delta}\mu_{\Theta}(x_{\mathbf{b}},v)|n(x_{\mathbf{b}})\cdot v|\mathrm{d}v\lesssim\delta^{2}.

(5.21)

On the other hand, from
|n(x_{\mathbf{b}})\cdot v|\leq|v|
and (
0.4
), we have

\begin{split}\int_{|v|\geq\frac{1}{\delta}}\mu_{\Theta}(x_{\mathbf{b}},v)|n(x_{\mathbf{b}})\cdot v|\mathrm{d}v&<\int_{|v|\geq\frac{1}{\delta}}\mu_{\Theta}(x_{\mathbf{b}},v)|v|\mathrm{d}v\\
&\lesssim\int^{\infty}_{\frac{1}{\delta}}e^{-\frac{r^{2}}{2b}}r\mathrm{d}r\leq e^{-\frac{1}{2b\delta^{2}}}\lesssim\delta^{2}.\end{split}

(5.22)

Combining (
5.21
) and (
5.22
), we conclude (
5.20
).
∎

Now we are ready to prove the continuity on
\mathfrak{J}(x)
.

Lemma 23
.

Suppose
F(x,v)
solves (
0.7
) and (
0.2
), then
\mathfrak{J}(x)=\int_{n(x)\cdot v>0}F(x,v)\{n(x)\cdot v\}\mathrm{d}v
is continuous on
\partial\Omega
.

Proof.

For
(x,v)\in\gamma_{+}\backslash\Gamma^{\delta}
, we have
|n(x)\cdot v|\geq\delta
and
|v|\leq\frac{1}{\delta}
.
Using (
1.17
), we have

\displaystyle t_{\mathbf{b}}\gtrsim|v_{3}|=|n(x)\cdot v|\geq\delta,

(5.23)

\displaystyle t_{\mathbf{b}}\lesssim|v_{3}|=|n(x)\cdot v|\leq|v|\leq\frac{1}{\delta}.

(5.24)

Using the above and (
1.22
), for
x,x_{\mathbf{b}}\in\partial\Omega
and
m,n\in\mathbb{Z}
, we have

\begin{split}|x-x_{\mathbf{b}}+(m,n)|&=|\int^{t}_{t-t_{\mathbf{b}}}V(s;t,x,v)\mathrm{d}s|\\
&\leq t_{\mathbf{b}}\times\|V(s;t,x,v)\|_{\infty}\lesssim\delta^{-2}.\end{split}

This implies

|m|\lesssim\delta^{-2},|n|\lesssim\delta^{-2}.

(5.25)

For any
0<\epsilon
, we can find sufficient small and fixed
\delta=\delta(\epsilon)
such that
\delta^{2}\leq\frac{\epsilon}{3}
.
Recall (
5.5
), we split
\mathfrak{J}(x)
into two following parts:

\begin{split}\mathfrak{J}(x)&=\int_{n(x)\cdot v>0}F(x,v)\{n(x)\cdot v\}\mathrm{d}v\\
&=\underbrace{\int_{v\in\gamma_{+}\cap\Gamma^{\delta}}F(x,v)\{n(x)\cdot v\}\mathrm{d}v}_{\eqref{j_split}_{1}}+\underbrace{\int_{v\in\gamma_{+}\backslash\Gamma^{\delta}}F(x,v)\{n(x)\cdot v\}\mathrm{d}v}_{\eqref{j_split}_{2}}.\end{split}

(5.26)

Using the uniform boundness on
\mathfrak{J}(x)
, Lemma
3
and Lemma
22
, we get

\eqref{j_split}_{1}\lesssim\|\mathfrak{J}\|_{L^{\infty}}\int_{v\in\Gamma^{\delta}}\mu_{\Theta}(x_{\mathbf{b}},v)\{n(x_{\mathbf{b}})\cdot v\}\mathrm{d}v\lesssim\delta^{2}\leq\frac{\epsilon}{3}.

(5.27)

Using (
5.23
), (
5.24
) and (
5.25
), we find that for
v\in\gamma_{+}\backslash\Gamma^{\delta}
,

\delta\lesssim t_{\mathbf{b}}\lesssim\frac{1}{\delta}\ \text{and}\ 0\leq|m|,|n|\lesssim\delta^{-2},

which implies

\begin{split}\eqref{j_split}_{2}&\leq\sum_{|m|,|n|\lesssim\delta^{-2}}\int_{\partial\Omega}\int^{\frac{1}{\delta}}_{\delta}\mu_{\Theta}(x_{\mathbf{b}},v)\mathfrak{J}(x_{\mathbf{b}})\{n(x)\cdot v\}\Big\{\frac{|n(x_{\mathbf{b}})\cdot v_{\mathbf{b}}|}{(t_{\mathbf{b}})^{3}}d_{0}\Big\}\mathrm{d}t_{\mathbf{b}}\mathrm{d}x_{\mathbf{b}},\end{split}

(5.28)

Thus, we have

\mathfrak{J}(x)=\sum_{|m|,|n|\lesssim\delta^{-2}}\int_{\partial\Omega}\int^{\frac{1}{\delta}}_{\delta}\mu_{\Theta}(x_{\mathbf{b}},v)\mathfrak{J}(x_{\mathbf{b}})\{n(x)\cdot v\}\Big\{\frac{|n(x_{\mathbf{b}})\cdot v_{\mathbf{b}}|}{(t_{\mathbf{b}})^{3}}d_{0}\Big\}\mathrm{d}t_{\mathbf{b}}\mathrm{d}x_{\mathbf{b}}+\Delta_{\mathfrak{J}}(x),

where
|\Delta_{\mathfrak{J}}(x)|=\eqref{j_split}_{1}\leq\frac{\epsilon}{3}
.

We can do the similar estimate on
\mathfrak{J}(x^{i})
, and due to the uniform boundness on
\mathfrak{J}(x)
, and the pointwise convergence shown in (
5.19
) as
\delta\leq t_{\mathbf{b}}
. We apply the dominated convergence theorem in finitely many terms in (
5.28
), and derive that

\sum_{|m|,|n|\lesssim\delta^{-2}}\int_{\partial\Omega}\int^{\frac{1}{\delta}}_{\delta}\mathfrak{J}^{m,n}(x^{i})\mathrm{d}t^{i}_{\mathbf{b}}\mathrm{d}x^{i}_{\mathbf{b}}\rightarrow\sum_{|m|,|n|\lesssim\delta^{-2}}\int_{\partial\Omega}\int^{\frac{1}{\delta}}_{\delta}\mathfrak{J}^{m,n}(x)\mathrm{d}t_{\mathbf{b}}\mathrm{d}x_{\mathbf{b}}\ \text{as}\ x^{i}\rightarrow x.

Then there exists sufficient large
N
such that for any
i\geq N
, we have

\Big|\sum_{|m|,|n|\lesssim\delta^{-2}}\int_{\partial\Omega}\int^{\frac{1}{\delta}}_{\delta}\mathfrak{J}^{m,n}(x^{i})\mathrm{d}t^{i}_{\mathbf{b}}\mathrm{d}x^{i}_{\mathbf{b}}-\sum_{|m|,|n|\lesssim\delta^{-2}}\int_{\partial\Omega}\int^{\frac{1}{\delta}}_{\delta}\mathfrak{J}^{m,n}(x)\mathrm{d}t_{\mathbf{b}}\mathrm{d}x_{\mathbf{b}}\Big|\leq\frac{\epsilon}{3}.

(5.29)

Now we compute the difference between
\mathfrak{J}(x)
and
\mathfrak{J}(x^{i})
with
\delta
and
i
defined above,

\begin{split}&\ \ \ \ |\mathfrak{J}(x)-\mathfrak{J}(x^{i})|\\
&=\Big|\sum_{|m|,|n|\lesssim\delta^{-2}}\int_{\partial\Omega}\int^{\frac{1}{\delta}}_{\delta}\mathfrak{J}^{m,n}(x)\mathrm{d}t_{\mathbf{b}}\mathrm{d}x_{\mathbf{b}}+\Delta_{\mathfrak{J}}(x)-\sum_{|m|,|n|\lesssim\delta^{-2}}\int_{\partial\Omega}\int^{\frac{1}{\delta}}_{\delta}\mathfrak{J}^{m,n}(x^{i})\mathrm{d}t^{i}_{\mathbf{b}}\mathrm{d}x^{i}_{\mathbf{b}}-\Delta_{\mathfrak{J}}(x^{i})\Big|\\
&\leq\Big|\sum_{|m|,|n|\lesssim\delta^{-2}}\int_{\partial\Omega}\int^{\frac{1}{\delta}}_{\delta}\mathfrak{J}^{m,n}(x^{i})\mathrm{d}t^{i}_{\mathbf{b}}\mathrm{d}x^{i}_{\mathbf{b}}-\sum_{|m|,|n|\lesssim\delta^{-2}}\int_{\partial\Omega}\int^{\frac{1}{\delta}}_{\delta}\mathfrak{J}^{m,n}(x)\mathrm{d}t_{\mathbf{b}}\mathrm{d}x_{\mathbf{b}}\Big|+|\Delta_{\mathfrak{J}}(x)|+|\Delta_{\mathfrak{J}}(x^{i})|\\
&\leq\frac{\epsilon}{3}+\frac{\epsilon}{3}+\frac{\epsilon}{3}=\epsilon.\end{split}

(5.30)

Therefore, we conclude that
\mathfrak{J}(x)
is continuous on
\partial\Omega
.
∎

Remark 7
.

Suppose
F(x,v)
solves (
0.7
) and (
0.2
).
Since
F(x,v)=\mu_{\Theta}(x_{\mathbf{b}},v_{\mathbf{b}})\mathfrak{J}(x)
and
x_{\mathbf{b}},v_{\mathbf{b}}
are continuous for
n(x)\cdot v\neq 0
. Therefore,
F(x,v)
is continuous if
\mu_{\Theta}(x,v)
is a continuous function and
n(x)\cdot v\neq 0
.
A similar argument can be made for the case with magnet field after we conclude Lemma
26
.

5.2.
With Magnet field

Now consider
F(x,v)
solves (
0.10
) and (
0.2
), with
\mathfrak{J}(x)
defined in (
2.2
), for
x\in\partial\Omega
,

\begin{split}\mathfrak{J}(x)&=\int_{n(x)\cdot v>0}F(x,v)\{n(x)\cdot v\}\mathrm{d}v\\
&=\sum_{m,n\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{v\in v^{m,n}}F(x,v)\{n(x)\cdot v\}\mathrm{d}v\\
&=\sum_{m,n\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{\partial\Omega}\int^{\infty}_{0}\underbrace{\mu_{\Theta}(x_{\mathbf{b}},v)\mathfrak{J}(x_{\mathbf{b}})\{n(x)\cdot v\}\big\{\frac{5B^{2}_{3}}{2-2\cos(B_{3}t_{\mathbf{b}})}\big\}}_{\mathfrak{J}^{m,n}(x)}\mathrm{d}t_{\mathbf{b}}\mathrm{d}x_{\mathbf{b}},\end{split}

(5.31)

where
v^{m,n}
defined in (
1.38
).

Now we build the sequence
\{x^{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
with
|x^{i}-x|\rightarrow 0
as
i\rightarrow\infty
. Similar, we have

\mathfrak{J}(x^{i})=\sum_{m,n\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{\partial\Omega}\int^{\infty}_{0}\underbrace{\mu_{\Theta}(x^{i}_{\mathbf{b}},v^{i})\mathfrak{J}(x^{i}_{\mathbf{b}})\{n(x^{i})\cdot v^{i}\}\big\{\frac{5B^{2}_{3}}{2-2\cos(B_{3}t^{i}_{\mathbf{b}})}\big\}}_{\mathfrak{J}^{m,n}(x^{i})}\mathrm{d}t^{i}_{\mathbf{b}}\mathrm{d}x^{i}_{\mathbf{b}},

(5.32)

where
t^{i}_{\mathbf{b}}=t_{\mathbf{b}}(x^{i},v^{i})
,
v^{i}_{\mathbf{b}}=v_{\mathbf{b}}(x^{i},v^{i})
and
x^{i}_{\mathbf{b}}=x_{\mathbf{b}}(x^{i},v^{i})
.

Lemma 24
.

Consider
(x,v),(x^{i},v^{i})\in\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
, and their backward stochastic cycles with
(X,V)
in (
1.2
):

(t_{\mathbf{b}},x_{\mathbf{b}}+(m,n,0),v_{\mathbf{b}}),\ \ (t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i},x_{\mathbf{b}}^{i}+(m,n,0),v_{\mathbf{b}}^{i}).

Suppose
m,n\in\mathbb{Z}
are fixed,
t^{i}_{\mathbf{b}}=t_{\mathbf{b}}
,
x^{i}_{\mathbf{b}}=x_{\mathbf{b}}
and
|x^{i}-x|\rightarrow 0
as
i\rightarrow\infty
, then

|v^{i}|\rightarrow|v|\ \text{as}\ i\rightarrow\infty.

(5.33)

Moreover, we have

\mathfrak{J}^{m,n}(x^{i})\rightarrow\mathfrak{J}^{m,n}(x)\ \text{as}\ i\rightarrow\infty.

(5.34)

Proof.

From (
1.33
) and the assumption
t^{i}_{\mathbf{b}}=t_{\mathbf{b}}
, we obtain that

5t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)=|v_{3}|=|v^{i}_{3}|.

Using (
2.61
) and the assumption
x^{i}_{\mathbf{b}}=x_{\mathbf{b}}
, we get

\begin{split}&\big|v_{\parallel}\big|^{2}=\frac{B^{2}_{3}\times|x-x_{\mathbf{b}}+(m,n,0)|^{2}}{2-2\cos(B_{3}t_{\mathbf{b}})},\ \ \big|v^{i}_{\parallel}\big|^{2}=\frac{B^{2}_{3}\times|x^{i}-x_{\mathbf{b}}+(m,n,0)|^{2}}{2-2\cos(B_{3}t_{\mathbf{b}})},\end{split}

where
v_{\parallel}=(v_{1},v_{2})
and
v^{i}_{\parallel}=(v^{i}_{1},v^{i}_{2})
.
From
|x^{i}-x|\rightarrow 0
, we derive
\big|v_{\parallel}\big|\rightarrow\big|v^{i}_{\parallel}\big|
, and obtain (
5.33
).

Next, from the implicit function theorem, the characteristic trajectory
X(s,t,x,v)
is smooth.
Using (
1.33
), (
5.33
), and for fixed
m,n
with
0<t^{i}_{\mathbf{b}}=t_{\mathbf{b}}
and
x^{i}_{\mathbf{b}}=x_{\mathbf{b}}
, we conclude (
5.34
).
∎

Following Lemma
11
and Lemma
22
, we derive the following Lemma:

Lemma 25
.

Define

\Gamma^{\delta}=\{(x,v)\in\gamma:v\in\mathcal{V}^{B_{\delta}}(x)\ \text{or}\ |v|\geq\frac{1}{\delta}\}.

Consider
(X,V)
in (
1.2
),
then for
0<\delta\ll 1
and
x\in\partial\Omega
,

\int_{\Gamma^{\delta}}\mu_{\Theta}(x_{\mathbf{b}},v)\{n(x_{\mathbf{b}})\cdot v\}\mathrm{d}v\lesssim\delta.

Now we are able to prove the continuity on
\mathfrak{J}(x)
under magnet field.

Lemma 26
.

Suppose
F(x,v)
solves (
0.10
) and (
0.2
), with
\mathfrak{J}(x)
defined in (
2.2
), then
\mathfrak{J}(x)
is continuous on
\partial\Omega
.

Proof.

For
(x,v)\in\gamma_{+}\backslash\Gamma^{\delta}
with
0<\delta\ll 1
, we have
v\in\mathcal{V}^{G_{\delta}}(x)
. This shows that

\frac{\delta}{B_{3}}+\frac{2k\pi}{B_{3}}\leq t_{\mathbf{b}}(x,v)\leq\frac{2\pi-\delta}{B_{3}}+\frac{2k\pi}{B_{3}}\ \text{for}\ k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}.

(5.35)

On the other hand, for
|v|\leq\frac{1}{\delta}
, we have

t_{\mathbf{b}}\lesssim|n(x)\cdot v|\leq|v|\leq\frac{1}{\delta}.

(5.36)

Using the above estimate, for
x,x_{\mathbf{b}}\in\partial\Omega
and
m,n\in\mathbb{Z}
, we have

\begin{split}|x-x_{\mathbf{b}}+(m,n)|&=|\int^{t}_{t-t_{\mathbf{b}}}V(s;t,x,v)\mathrm{d}s|\\
&\leq t_{\mathbf{b}}\times\|V(s;t,x,v)\|_{\infty}\lesssim\delta^{-2}.\end{split}

This implies

|m|\lesssim\delta^{-2},|n|\lesssim\delta^{-2}.

(5.37)

For any
\epsilon>0
, we can find sufficient small and fixed
\delta=\delta(\epsilon)
such that
\delta\leq\frac{\epsilon}{3}
.
Recall (
5.31
), we split
\mathfrak{J}(x)
into two parts:

\begin{split}\mathfrak{J}(x)&=\int_{n(x)\cdot v>0}F(x,v)\{n(x)\cdot v\}\mathrm{d}v\\
&=\underbrace{\int_{v\in\gamma_{+}\cap\Gamma^{\delta}}F(x,v)\{n(x)\cdot v\}\mathrm{d}v}_{\eqref{j_split mag}_{1}}+\underbrace{\int_{v\in\gamma_{+}\backslash\Gamma^{\delta}}F(x,v)\{n(x)\cdot v\}\mathrm{d}v}_{\eqref{j_split mag}_{2}}.\end{split}

(5.38)

Using the uniform boundness on
\mathfrak{J}(x)
, Lemma
5
and Lemma
25
, we get

\eqref{j_split mag}_{1}\lesssim\|\mathfrak{J}\|_{L^{\infty}}\int_{v\in\Gamma^{\delta}}\mu_{\Theta}(x_{\mathbf{b}},v)\{n(x_{\mathbf{b}})\cdot v\}\mathrm{d}v\lesssim\delta\leq\frac{\epsilon}{3}.

(5.39)

Using (
5.35
), (
5.36
) and (
5.37
), we find that for
v\in\gamma_{+}\backslash\Gamma^{\delta}
,

v\in B_{\delta},\ t_{\mathbf{b}}\lesssim\frac{1}{\delta}\ \text{and}\ 0\leq|m|,|n|\lesssim\delta^{-2},

which implies

\begin{split}\eqref{j_split mag}_{2}&\leq\sum_{|m|,|n|\lesssim\delta^{-2}}\int_{\partial\Omega}\int^{\frac{1}{\delta}}_{0}\mathbf{1}_{v\in B_{\delta}}\times\mu_{\Theta}(x_{\mathbf{b}},v)\mathfrak{J}(x_{\mathbf{b}})\{n(x)\cdot v\}\big\{\frac{5B^{2}_{3}}{2-2\cos(B_{3}t_{\mathbf{b}})}\big\}\mathrm{d}t_{\mathbf{b}}\mathrm{d}x_{\mathbf{b}},\end{split}

(5.40)

Thus, we have

\mathfrak{J}(x)=\sum_{|m|,|n|\lesssim\delta^{-2}}\int_{\partial\Omega}\int^{\frac{1}{\delta}}_{0}\mathbf{1}_{v\in B_{\delta}}\times\mathfrak{J}^{m,n}(x)\mathrm{d}t_{\mathbf{b}}\mathrm{d}x_{\mathbf{b}}+\Delta_{\mathfrak{J}}(x),

where
\Delta_{\mathfrak{J}}(x)=\eqref{j_split mag}_{1}\leq\frac{\epsilon}{3}
.

Recall (
5.32
), given a sequence
\{x^{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
with
|x^{i}-x|\rightarrow 0
as
i\rightarrow\infty
, we can do the similar estimate on
\mathfrak{J}(x^{i})
.
Due to the uniform boundness and the pointwise convergence shown in (
5.34
). We apply the dominated convergence theorem in finitely many terms in (
5.40
), and derive that

\sum_{|m|,|n|\lesssim\delta^{-2}}\int^{\frac{1}{\delta}}_{0}\mathbf{1}_{v\in B_{\delta}}\times\mathfrak{J}^{m,n}(x^{i})\mathrm{d}t^{i}_{\mathbf{b}}\mathrm{d}x^{i}_{\mathbf{b}}\rightarrow\sum_{|m|,|n|\lesssim\delta^{-2}}\int^{\frac{1}{\delta}}_{0}\mathbf{1}_{v\in B_{\delta}}\times\mathfrak{J}^{m,n}(x)\mathrm{d}t_{\mathbf{b}}\mathrm{d}x_{\mathbf{b}}\ \ \text{as}\ x^{i}\rightarrow x.

Then there exists sufficient large
N
such that for any
i\geq N
, we have

\Big|\sum_{|m|,|n|\lesssim\delta^{-2}}\int^{\frac{1}{\delta}}_{0}\mathbf{1}_{v\in B_{\delta}}\times\mathfrak{J}^{m,n}(x^{i})\mathrm{d}t^{i}_{\mathbf{b}}\mathrm{d}x^{i}_{\mathbf{b}}-\sum_{|m|,|n|\lesssim\delta^{-2}}\int^{\frac{1}{\delta}}_{0}\mathbf{1}_{v\in B_{\delta}}\times\mathfrak{J}^{m,n}(x)\mathrm{d}t_{\mathbf{b}}\mathrm{d}x_{\mathbf{b}}\Big|\leq\frac{\epsilon}{3}.

(5.41)

Now we compute the difference between
\mathfrak{J}(x)
and
\mathfrak{J}(x^{i})
with
\delta
and
i
defined above,

\begin{split}&\ \ \ \ |\mathfrak{J}(x)-\mathfrak{J}(x^{i})|\\
&=\Big|\sum_{|m|,|n|\lesssim\delta^{-2}}\int^{\frac{1}{\delta}}_{0}\mathbf{1}_{v\in B_{\delta}}\times\mathfrak{J}^{m,n}(x)\mathrm{d}t_{\mathbf{b}}\mathrm{d}x_{\mathbf{b}}+\Delta_{\mathfrak{J}}(x)-\sum_{|m|,|n|\lesssim\delta^{-2}}\int^{\frac{1}{\delta}}_{0}\mathbf{1}_{v\in B_{\delta}}\times\mathfrak{J}^{m,n}(x^{i})\mathrm{d}t^{i}_{\mathbf{b}}\mathrm{d}x^{i}_{\mathbf{b}}-\Delta_{\mathfrak{J}}(x^{i})\Big|\\
&\leq\eqref{j_split^2 second estimate mag}+|\Delta_{\mathfrak{J}}(x)|+|\Delta_{\mathfrak{J}}(x^{i})|\leq\frac{\epsilon}{3}+\frac{\epsilon}{3}+\frac{\epsilon}{3}=\epsilon.\end{split}

(5.42)

Therefore, we conclude that
\mathfrak{J}(x)
is continuous on
\partial\Omega
.
∎

5.3.
Uniform
L^{1}
bound of Residual Measure

Recall that in (
2.39
) and (
2.81
), we bound the residual measure terms as

\int_{\mathcal{V}_{0}}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{0}\cdots\int_{\mathcal{V}_{i-1}}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{i-1}\leq 2^{i},\ \text{with}\ i=2,\cdots,k-1,

where
\mathrm{d}\sigma_{j}=\mu_{\Theta}(x^{j+1},v^{j})\{n(x^{j})\cdot v^{j}\}\mathrm{d}v^{j}
in (
0.16
).
To derive the decay of exponential moments, we show the following uniform
L^{1}
bound of the residual measure:

Proposition 11
.

Consider

(X,V)
in (
1.1
) (resp. (
1.2
)),
then for
i=1,\cdots,k-1
,
there exists
C>0
such that

\int_{\prod_{j=0}^{i-1}\mathcal{V}_{j}}\mathbf{1}_{0<t_{i}\leq t}\ \mathrm{d}\sigma_{i-1}\cdots\mathrm{d}\sigma_{1}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{0}\leq C,

(5.43)

where
\mathrm{d}\sigma_{j}=\mu_{\Theta}(x^{j+1},v^{j})\{n(x^{j})\cdot v^{j}\}\mathrm{d}v^{j}
in (
0.16
), and

C=C(\Omega,\Theta)
is independent of both
i
and
t
.

Proof.

Recall from Lemma
23
, we have the continuity on the following:

\mathfrak{J}(x)=\int_{n(x)\cdot v>0}F_{s}(x,v)\{n(x)\cdot v\}\mathrm{d}v,\ \text{for}\ x\in\partial\Omega,

where we consider
F_{s}(x,v)\geq 0
solving (
0.7
) and (
0.2
) with
\iint_{\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}F_{s}(x,v)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}v=1
.

From the
L^{\infty}_{x}
-estimate on
\mathfrak{J}(x)
in Proposition
5
, there exists
U>0
such that

\|\mathfrak{J}(x)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq U.

Since
\partial\Omega
is compact and
\mathfrak{J}(x)
is continuous,
\mathfrak{J}(x)
must have the infimum, and we define

L:=\inf\limits_{x\in\partial\Omega}\mathfrak{J}(x).

It is easy to see that
L\geq 0
. Moreover, from (
2.2
), (
2.3
) and Lemma
3
, we have

\mathfrak{J}(x)=\int_{n(x)\cdot v>0}\mu_{\Theta}(x_{\mathbf{b}},v)\mathfrak{J}(x_{\mathbf{b}})\{n(x)\cdot v\}\mathrm{d}v,\ \text{for}\ x\in\partial\Omega.

Now we claim that
L>0
.
Suppose not, if
\mathfrak{J}(x)=0
for some
x\in\partial\Omega
, we consider the map:

v^{0,0}\in\mathcal{V}\mapsto(x_{\mathbf{b}},t_{\mathbf{b}}),

with
1\leq t_{\mathbf{b}}\leq 10
.
Then the integrand has to be zero almost everywhere since it is non-negative. This implies

\mathfrak{J}(x)
has to be zero almost everywhere, which contradicts with
\iint_{\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}F_{s}(x,v)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}v>0
.
Therefore, we derive that
L>0
and show the claim.

From the stochastic cycles in Lemma
17
, for
x\in\partial\Omega
, we have

\begin{split}U\geq\mathfrak{J}(x)&\geq\int_{\prod_{j=0}^{i-1}\mathcal{V}_{j}}\mathbf{1}_{0<t_{i}\leq t}\ \mathrm{d}\sigma_{i-1}\cdots\mathrm{d}\sigma_{1}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{0}\int_{\mathcal{V}_{i}}F_{s}(x^{i},v^{i})\{n(x^{i})\cdot v^{i}\}\mathrm{d}v^{i}\\
&\geq\int_{\prod_{j=0}^{i-1}\mathcal{V}_{j}}\mathbf{1}_{0<t_{i}\leq t}\ \mathrm{d}\sigma_{i-1}\cdots\mathrm{d}\sigma_{1}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{0}\times L.\end{split}

Therefore, for
i=2,\cdots,k-1
, we derive

\int_{\prod_{j=0}^{i-1}\mathcal{V}_{j}}\mathbf{1}_{0<t_{i}\leq t}\ \mathrm{d}\sigma_{i-1}\cdots\mathrm{d}\sigma_{1}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{0}\leq L^{-1}U.

For the magnet field case, since we have proved
\mathfrak{J}(x)
is continuous in Lemma
26
. Following the steps in gravitational case, we can conclude (
5.43
).
∎

From Proposition
11
and
following the similar idea in Lemma
8
, we obtain:

Lemma 27
.

Suppose
f(t,x,v)
solves (
4.1
)-(
4.3
) and
(X,V)
in (
1.1
), for
0\leq t^{i}\leq t
,
i=2,\cdots,k-1
,

\int_{\prod_{j=0}^{i}\mathcal{V}_{j}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{i+1}<0\leq t^{i}}\int^{t^{i}}_{0}\varrho^{\prime}(s)f(s,X(s;t^{i},x^{i},v^{i}),V(s;t^{i},x^{i},v^{i}))\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Sigma}_{i}\lesssim\int^{t}_{0}\|\varrho^{\prime}(s)f(s)\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}\mathrm{d}s.

(5.44)

where

\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Sigma}_{i}:=\frac{\mathrm{d}\sigma_{i}}{\mu_{\Theta}(x^{i+1},v^{i})}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{i-1}\cdots\mathrm{d}\sigma_{1}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{0}
with
\mathrm{d}\sigma_{j}=\mu_{\Theta}(x^{j+1},v^{j})\{n(x^{j})\cdot v^{j}\}\mathrm{d}v^{j}
in (
0.16
).

Proof.

For (
5.44
), it suffices to prove this upper bound for
i=2,...,k-1
,

\int_{\mathcal{V}_{0}}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{0}\cdots\int_{\mathcal{V}_{i-1}}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{i-1}\underbrace{\int_{\mathcal{V}_{i}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{i+1}<0\leq t^{i}}\int^{t^{i}}_{0}\varrho^{\prime}(s)|f(s,X(s;t^{i},x^{i},v^{i}),V(s;t^{i},x^{i},v^{i}))|\mathrm{d}s\{n(x^{i})\cdot v^{i}\}\mathrm{d}v^{i}}_{\eqref{forcing_2}^{*}}.

(5.45)

Following Lemma
8
, (
2.39
) and (
2.40
), we have

\displaystyle\eqref{forcing_2}\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{V}_{0}}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{0}\cdots\int_{\mathcal{V}_{i-3}}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{i-3}\int^{t^{i-2}}_{0}\frac{\mathrm{d}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}}{\langle t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}\rangle^{5}}\int_{0}^{\min\{t^{i-2}-t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1},t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}\}}\mathrm{d}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}\int_{\partial\Omega}\mathrm{d}S_{x^{i}}\eqref{forcing_2}^{*}

(5.46)

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathcal{V}_{0}}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{0}\cdots\int_{\mathcal{V}_{i-3}}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{i-3}\int^{t^{i-2}}_{0}\frac{\mathrm{d}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}}{\langle t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}\rangle^{5}}\int_{0}^{\min\{t^{i-2}-t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2},t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}\}}\mathrm{d}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}\int_{\partial\Omega}\mathrm{d}S_{x^{i}}\eqref{forcing_2}^{*}.

(5.47)

For (
5.46
), we employ the change of variables

(x^{i},t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2},v^{i})\mapsto(y,w)=(X(s;t^{i-2}-t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}-t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1},x^{i},v^{i}),V(s;t^{i-2}-t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}-t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1},x^{i},v^{i}))\in\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3},

with
|n(x^{i})\cdot v^{i}|\mathrm{d}S_{x^{i}}\mathrm{d}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}\mathrm{d}v^{i}\lesssim\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}w
. Applying this change of variables, Proposition
11
and
0\leq t^{i}\leq t
, we derive

\begin{split}\eqref{est_a:forcing_2}&\leq\int_{\mathcal{V}_{0}}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{0}\cdots\int_{\mathcal{V}_{i-3}}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{i-3}\int^{t^{i-2}}_{0}\mathrm{d}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}\langle t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}\rangle^{-5}\int^{t^{i}}_{0}\varrho^{\prime}(s)\iint_{\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}|f(s,y,w)|\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}w\mathrm{d}s\\
&\lesssim\int^{t}_{0}\|\varrho^{\prime}(s)f(s)\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}\mathrm{d}s.\end{split}

A bound of (
5.47
) can be derived, using the change of variables

(x^{i},t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1},v^{i})\mapsto(y,w)=(X(s;t^{i-2}-t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}-t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1},x^{i},v^{i}),V(s;t_{i-2}-t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}-t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1},x^{i},v^{i}))\in\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3},

with
|n(x^{i})\cdot v^{i}|\mathrm{d}S_{x^{i}}\mathrm{d}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}\mathrm{d}v^{i}\lesssim\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}w
.
∎

For the magnetic case, we need Proposition
11
and Lemma
13
to derive the following:

Lemma 28
.

Suppose
f(t,x,v)
solves (
4.1
)-(
4.3
) for (
0.6
) and
(X,V)
in (
1.2
),
for
0\leq t^{i}\leq t
,
i=2,\cdots,k-1

and
0<\varepsilon\ll 1
,

\begin{split}&\ \ \ \ \int_{\prod_{j=0}^{i}\mathcal{V}_{j}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{i+1}<0\leq t^{i}}\int^{t^{i}}_{0}\varrho^{\prime}(s)f(s,X(s;t^{i},x^{i},v^{i}),V(s;t^{i},x^{i},v^{i}))\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Sigma}_{i}\\
&\lesssim\varepsilon^{i-1}\int_{\mathcal{V}_{i}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{i+1}<0\leq t^{i}}\ \varrho(t^{i})f(t^{i},x^{i},v^{i})\{n(x^{i})\cdot v^{i}\}\mathrm{d}v^{i}+(i-1)\varepsilon^{-2}\int^{t}_{0}\|\varrho^{\prime}(s)f(s)\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}\mathrm{d}s.\end{split}

(5.48)

where

\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Sigma}_{i}:=\frac{\mathrm{d}\sigma_{i}}{\mu_{\Theta}(x^{i+1},v^{i})}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{i-1}\cdots\mathrm{d}\sigma_{1}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{0}
with
\mathrm{d}\sigma_{j}=\mu_{\Theta}(x^{j+1},v^{j})\{n(x^{j})\cdot v^{j}\}\mathrm{d}v^{j}
in (
0.16
).

Proof.

It suffices to prove this upper bound for
i=2,...,k-1
,

\int_{\prod_{j=0}^{i-1}\mathcal{V}_{j}}\int_{\mathcal{V}_{i}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{i+1}<0\leq t^{i}}\int^{t^{i}}_{0}|\varrho^{\prime}(s)f(s,X(s;t^{i},x^{i},v^{i}),V(s;t^{i},x^{i},v^{i}))|\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Sigma}_{i}.

(5.49)

Again recall Definition
3
, we obtain for
j=0,\cdots,i-1
and
0<\varepsilon\ll 1
,

v^{j}\in\mathcal{V}^{B_{\varepsilon}}_{j},\ \text{or}\ v^{j}\in\mathcal{V}^{G_{\varepsilon}}_{j}.

Now we split
\{v^{j}\}^{i-1}_{j=0}
into two cases:

Case 1:
For all
0\leq j\leq i-2
,
v^{j}\in\mathcal{V}^{B_{\varepsilon}}_{j}
.
From Lemma
11
and
13
, we derive that

\begin{split}\eqref{forcing_2 mag}&\lesssim\varepsilon^{i-1}\int_{\mathcal{V}_{i}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{i+1}<0\leq t^{i}}\int^{t^{i}}_{0}|\varrho^{\prime}(s)f(s,X(s;t^{i},x^{i},v^{i}),V(s;t^{i},x^{i},v^{i}))|\mathrm{d}s\{n(x^{i})\cdot v^{i}\}\mathrm{d}v^{i}\\
&=\varepsilon^{i-1}\int_{\mathcal{V}_{i}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{i+1}<0\leq t^{i}}\int^{t^{i}}_{0}\varrho^{\prime}(s)\mathrm{d}s|f(t^{i},x^{i},v^{i})|\{n(x^{i})\cdot v^{i}\}\mathrm{d}v^{i}\\
&\lesssim\varepsilon^{i-1}\int_{\mathcal{V}_{i}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{i+1}<0\leq t^{i}}\ \varrho(t^{i})f(t^{i},x^{i},v^{i})\{n(x^{i})\cdot v^{i}\}\mathrm{d}v^{i}.\end{split}

Case 2:
There exists
0\leq j\leq i-2
such that
v^{j}\in\mathcal{V}^{G_{\varepsilon}}_{j}
.
Using Proposition
4
, we get

(
5.49
)

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{\mathcal{V}_{0}}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{0}\cdots\int_{\mathcal{V}_{j-1}}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{j-1}\times\int_{v^{j}\in\mathcal{V}^{G_{\varepsilon}}_{j}}\frac{5B^{2}_{3}\sum\limits_{m,n\in\mathbb{Z}}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{j+1},v^{m,n}_{j,\mathbf{b}})}{2-2\cos(B_{3}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{j})}\{n(x^{j})\cdot v^{j}\}\mathrm{d}S_{x^{j+1}}\mathrm{d}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{j}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \times\cdots\times\int_{0}^{t^{i-2}}\int_{\partial\Omega}\frac{5B^{2}_{3}\sum\limits_{m,n\in\mathbb{Z}}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{i-1},v^{m,n}_{i-2,\mathbf{b}})}{2-2\cos(B_{3}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2})}\{n(x^{i-2})\cdot v^{i-2}\}\mathrm{d}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-2}\mathrm{d}S_{x^{i-1}}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \times\int^{t^{i-1}}_{0}\int_{\partial\Omega}\frac{5B^{2}_{3}\sum\limits_{m,n\in\mathbb{Z}}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{i},v^{m,n}_{i-1,\mathbf{b}})}{2-2\cos(B_{3}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1})}\{n(x^{i-1})\cdot v^{i-1}\}\mathrm{d}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1}\mathrm{d}S_{x^{i}}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \times\int_{\mathcal{V}_{i}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{i+1}<0\leq t^{i}}\ \int^{t^{i}}_{0}|\varrho^{\prime}(s)f(s,X(s;t^{i},x^{i},v^{i}),V(s;t^{i},x^{i},v^{i}))|\mathrm{d}s\{n(x^{i})\cdot v^{i}\}\mathrm{d}v^{i}.

(5.50)

Following the similar steps in Lemma
13
, we derive that

\begin{split}\eqref{forcing_2 mag}\lesssim&\int_{\mathcal{V}_{0}}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{0}\cdots\int_{\mathcal{V}_{j-1}}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{j-1}\times\varepsilon^{-2}\times\int_{v^{j}\in\mathcal{V}^{G_{\varepsilon}}_{j}}\mathrm{d}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{j}\int_{\partial\Omega}\mathrm{d}S_{x^{i}}\eqref{est1:forcing_2 mag}.\end{split}

(5.51)

For (
5.51
), we employ the change of variables

(x^{i},t_{\mathbf{b}}^{j},v^{i})\mapsto(y,w)=(X(s;t^{j}-t_{\mathbf{b}}^{j}\cdots-t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1},x^{i},v^{i}),V(s;t^{j}-t_{\mathbf{b}}^{j}\cdots-t_{\mathbf{b}}^{i-1},x^{i},v^{i}))\in\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3},

with

|n(x^{i})\cdot v^{i}|\mathrm{d}S_{x^{i}}\mathrm{d}t_{\mathbf{b}}^{j}\mathrm{d}v^{i}\lesssim\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}w
. Applying the above and Proposition
11
, we derive

\begin{split}\eqref{est_1:forcing_2 mag}&\lesssim\varepsilon^{-2}\times\int^{t^{i}}_{0}\varrho^{\prime}(s)\mathrm{d}s\iint_{\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}|f(s,y,w)|\mathrm{d}w\mathrm{d}y\\
&\lesssim\varepsilon^{-2}\times\int^{t}_{0}\|\varrho^{\prime}(s)f(s)\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}\mathrm{d}s.\end{split}

Applying the above on all parts when
j=0,\cdots,i-2
in second cases, we deduce

\eqref{forcing_2 mag}\lesssim\sum\limits^{i-2}_{j=0}\varepsilon^{-2}\times\int^{t}_{0}\|\varrho^{\prime}(s)f(s)\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}\mathrm{d}s\leq(i-1)\varepsilon^{-2}\times\int^{t}_{0}\|\varrho^{\prime}(s)f(s)\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}\mathrm{d}s.

Collecting the estimates from two cases, we prove this Lemma.
∎

6.
Estimates on Exponential Moments

Using Proposition
11
, now we are able to show the asymptotic behaviour of the exponential moments. The main purpose of this section to prove Theorem
2
.

We first set
w(x,v):=e^{\theta(|v|^{2}+2\Phi(x))}
,
w^{\prime}(x,v):=e^{\theta^{\prime}(|v|^{2}+2\Phi(x))}
for
0<\theta<\theta^{\prime}<\frac{1}{2b}
with
b
defined in (
0.4
).
Then we include a time dependent weight function
\varrho(t)
and consider the stochastic cycle representation of
\varrho(t)w^{\prime}(x,v)f(t,x,v)
:

\displaystyle\varrho(t)w^{\prime}(x,v)f(t,x,v)=\mathbf{1}_{t^{1}<0}\ \varrho(t)w^{\prime}(X(0;t,x,v),V(0;t,x,v))f(0,X,V)

(6.1)

\displaystyle+w^{\prime}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{1},v_{\mathbf{b}})\int_{\prod_{j=1}^{i}\mathcal{V}_{j}}\sum\limits^{k-1}_{i=1}\Big\{\mathbf{1}_{t^{i+1}<0\leq t^{i}}\varrho(0)w^{\prime}(X(0;t^{i},x^{i},v^{i}),V(0;t^{i},x^{i},v^{i}))f(0,X,V)\Big\}\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Sigma}_{i}

(6.2)

\displaystyle+w^{\prime}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{1},v_{\mathbf{b}})\int_{\prod_{j=1}^{i}\mathcal{V}_{j}}\sum\limits^{k-1}_{i=1}\mathbf{1}_{0\leq t^{i}}\Big\{\int^{t^{i}}_{\max(0,t^{i+1})}\varrho^{\prime}(s)w^{\prime}(X(s;t^{i},x^{i},v^{i}),V(s;t^{i},x^{i},v^{i}))

\displaystyle\hskip 227.62204pt\times f(s,X(s;t^{i},x^{i},v^{i}),V(s;t^{i},x^{i},v^{i}))\mathrm{d}s\Big\}\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Sigma}_{i}

(6.3)

\displaystyle+w^{\prime}\mu_{\Theta}(x^{1},v_{\mathbf{b}})\int_{\prod_{j=1}^{k}\mathcal{V}_{j}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{k}\geq 0}\ \varrho(t^{k})w^{\prime}f(t^{k},x^{k},v^{k})\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Sigma}_{k},

(6.4)

where

\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Sigma}_{i}:=\frac{\mathrm{d}\sigma_{i}}{\mu_{\Theta}(x^{i+1},v^{i})w^{\prime}(x^{i},v^{i})}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{i-1}\cdots\mathrm{d}\sigma_{1}
.
Here,
(X,V)
in (
1.1
) (resp. (
1.2
)).

6.1.
Gravitational case

Proof of Theorem
2
.

We start to prove (
0.12
), and pick
\varrho(t)=t+1
to utilize the
L^{1}
-decay of Theorem
7
. Then we work on the stochastic cycle representation of
\varrho(t)w^{\prime}(x,v)f(t,x,v)
in (
6.1
)-(
6.4
).

For the contribution of (
6.1
), since
t^{1}<0
, and
w^{\prime},f
are constant along the characteristic trajectory. Thus we deduce that

w^{\prime}(x,v)f(t,x,v)=w^{\prime}(X(0;t,x,v),V(0;t,x,v))f(0,X,V)\leq\|w^{\prime}f(0)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}.

(6.5)

Now we bound the contribution of (
6.2
). Since

0<n(x)\cdot v\lesssim w^{\prime}(x,v)<\mu^{-1}(x,v)
and applying the uniform bound on residual measure in Proposition
11
, we derive

\begin{split}\frac{1}{\varrho(t)}|\eqref{expand_k2}|&\lesssim\frac{k}{\varrho(t)}\bigg(\sup_{i}\int_{\prod_{j=1}^{k}\mathcal{V}_{j}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{i+1}<0\leq t^{i}}\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Sigma}_{i}\bigg)\varrho(0)\|w^{\prime}f(0)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\\
&\lesssim\frac{k}{\varrho(t)}\bigg(\int_{n(x^{j})\cdot v^{j}>0}\frac{|n(x^{j})\cdot v^{j}|}{w^{\prime}(x^{j},v^{j})}\mathrm{d}v^{j}\bigg)\|w^{\prime}f(0)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\\
&\lesssim\frac{k}{\varrho(t)}\|w^{\prime}f(0)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}.\end{split}

(6.6)

Applying Lemma
27
, Proposition
11
and Theorem
7
, we bound the contribution of (
6.3
). Since

0<n(x)\cdot v\lesssim w^{\prime}(x,v)<\mu_{\Theta}^{-1}(x,v)
and
\varrho^{\prime}=1
, we have

\begin{split}\frac{1}{\varrho(t)}|\eqref{expand_k3}|&\lesssim\frac{k}{\varrho(t)}\sup_{i}\int_{\prod_{j=1}^{i}\mathcal{V}_{j}}\mathbf{1}_{0\leq t^{i}}\int^{t^{i}}_{\max(0,t^{i+1})}w^{\prime}(X(s;t^{i},x^{i},v^{i}),V(s;t^{i},x^{i},v^{i}))f(s,X,V)\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Sigma}_{i}\\
&\lesssim\frac{k}{\varrho(t)}\int^{t}_{0}\|f(s)\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}\mathrm{d}s\lesssim\frac{k}{\varrho(t)}\times\|w^{\prime}f(0)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}.\end{split}

(6.7)

where the last inequality holds from
w^{\prime}(x,v)=w^{\prime}(x_{\mathbf{b}},v_{\mathbf{b}})
and
|v_{\mathbf{b}}|\gtrsim t_{\mathbf{f}}
.

Lastly we bound the contribution of (
6.4
). From Lemma
9
, Proposition
11
and
0<n(x^{2})\cdot v^{2}\lesssim w(x^{2},v^{2})<w^{\prime}(x^{2},v^{2})
, we get

\begin{split}\frac{1}{\varrho(t)}|\eqref{expand_k4}|&\lesssim\frac{\varrho(t^{k})}{\varrho(t)}\sup_{(x,v)\in\bar{\Omega}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Big(\int_{\prod_{j=1}^{k-1}\mathcal{V}_{j}}\mathbf{1}_{t_{k}(t,x,v,v^{1},\cdots,v^{k-1})\geq 0}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{1}\cdots\mathrm{d}\sigma_{k-1}\Big)\|w^{\prime}f(t_{k})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\\
&\lesssim e^{-t}\sup_{t\geq s\geq 0}\|w^{\prime}f(s)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}.\end{split}

(6.8)

Collecting estimates from (
6.5
)-(
6.8
) and using
k\lesssim t
, we derive

(1-e^{-t})\sup_{t\geq 0}\|w^{\prime}f(t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\lesssim(1+\frac{k}{\varrho(t)})\times\|w^{\prime}f(0)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}.

(6.9)

Therefore, we prove (
0.12
).

Next, to prove (
0.13
) and show the decay of exponential moments and again utilize the
L^{1}
-decay, we set a new weight function

\varrho(t):=e^{\Lambda t}.

(6.10)

Clearly we have
\varrho^{\prime}(t)\lesssim e^{\Lambda t}
.

From Lemma
17
, we derive the form of
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}w(x,v)|f(t,x,v)|\mathrm{d}v
. First we split
|v|\geq t/2
and
t_{1}\leq 3t/4
case to get (
6.11
)-(
6.12
). Next, for
t_{1}\geq 3t/4
case, we follow along the stochastic cycles twice with
k=2
and
t_{*}=t/2
and get (
6.13
) and (
6.14
).

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}w(x,v)

\displaystyle|f(t,x,v)|\mathrm{d}v\leq\int_{|v|\geq t/2}w(x,v)|f(t,x,v)|\mathrm{d}v

(6.11)

\displaystyle+\int_{|v|\leq t/2}\mathbf{1}_{t^{1}\leq 3t/4}w(x,v)|f(3t/4,X(3t/4;t,x,v),V(3t/4;t,x,v))|\mathrm{d}v

(6.12)

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{1}\geq 3t/4}w(x,v)\mu_{\Theta}(x^{1},v_{\mathbf{b}})\int_{\prod^{2}_{j=1}\mathcal{V}_{j}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{2}<t/2<t^{1}}w(x^{1},v^{1})|f(t^{1},x^{1},v^{1})|\mathrm{d}\Sigma_{1}^{2}\mathrm{d}v

(6.13)

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{1}\geq 3t/4}w(x,v)\mu_{\Theta}(x^{1},v_{\mathbf{b}})\Big|\int_{\prod^{2}_{j=1}\mathcal{V}_{j}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{2}\geq t/2}w(x^{2},v^{2})f(t^{2},x^{2},v^{2})\mathrm{d}\Sigma_{2}^{2}\Big|\mathrm{d}v,

(6.14)

where
\mathrm{d}{\Sigma}^{2}_{1}=\mathrm{d}\sigma_{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}\sigma_{1}}{\mu_{\Theta}(x^{2},v^{1})w(x^{1},v^{1})}
and
\mathrm{d}{\Sigma}^{2}_{2}=\frac{\mathrm{d}\sigma_{2}}{\mu_{\Theta}(x^{3},v^{2})w(x^{2},v^{2})}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{1}
,
and

\mathrm{d}\sigma_{j}=\mu_{\Theta}(x^{j+1},v^{j})\{n(x^{j})\cdot v^{j}\}\mathrm{d}v^{j}
in (
0.16
).

For (
6.11
),
from
|v|\geq t/2
, the
L^{\infty}
-boundedness and
0<w<w^{\prime}
, we derive that

\begin{split}\int_{|v|\geq t/2}w(x,v)|f(t,x,v)|\mathrm{d}v&\leq\int_{|v|\geq t/2}\frac{w(x,v)}{w^{\prime}(x,v)}\mathrm{d}v\|w^{\prime}f(0)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\\
&\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{\theta^{\prime}-\theta}}e^{-\frac{(\theta^{\prime}-\theta)t^{2}}{4}}\|w^{\prime}f(0)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}.\end{split}

(6.15)

For (
6.12
), from
|v|\leq t/2
and
t^{1}\leq 3t/4
, we have

|V(3t/4;t,x,v)|\gtrsim|v_{3}+g(t-3t/4)|\geq 2t\geq|v|+t.

(6.16)

Recall
\Phi(x)
defined in (
0.5
), we derive

\begin{split}\Phi(x)&=gx_{3}+\int^{x_{3}}_{0}\Big\{\int^{s}_{0}\nabla_{x_{3}}\nabla_{x_{3}}\phi(x_{1},x_{2},\alpha)\mathrm{d}\alpha+\nabla_{x_{3}}\phi(x_{1},x_{2},0)\Big\}\mathrm{d}s+\phi(x_{1},x_{2},0)\\
&\geq gx_{3}-\frac{1}{\varrho_{1}}\varrho_{2}x_{3}-\varrho_{3}x_{3}\gtrsim gx_{3}\geq 0.\end{split}

(6.17)

From Lemma
3
and
\Phi(x)|_{x_{3}=0}=0
, we have

\frac{|V(s;t,x,v)|^{2}}{2}+\Phi(X(s;t,x,v))=\frac{|v|^{2}}{2}=\frac{|v_{\mathbf{b}}|^{2}}{2}.

(6.18)

Then, from the
L^{\infty}
-boundedness, (
6.16
) and
0<w<w^{\prime}
, we deduce that

\begin{split}\eqref{first team in f_exp}&\lesssim\int_{|v|\leq t/2}\frac{w(x,v)}{w^{\prime}(X(3t/4;t,x,v),V(3t/4;t,x,v))}\mathrm{d}v\|w^{\prime}f(0)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\\
&\leq\int_{|v|\leq t/2}e^{(\theta-\theta^{\prime})(|V(3t/4;t,x,v)|^{2}+\Phi(X))}\mathrm{d}v\|w^{\prime}f(0)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\\
&\leq\int_{|v|\leq t/2}e^{(\theta-\theta^{\prime})(|v|^{2}+t^{2})}\mathrm{d}v\|w^{\prime}f(0)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{\theta^{\prime}-\theta}}e^{-(\theta^{\prime}-\theta)t^{2}}\|w^{\prime}f(0)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}.\end{split}

(6.19)

Next, we bound
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}w(x,v)\mu_{\Theta}(x^{1},v_{\mathbf{b}})\mathrm{d}v
shown in (
6.13
) and (
6.14
).
Note that from (
6.17
) and (
6.18
), we have
|v|=|v_{\mathbf{b}}|\geq|V(s;t,x,v)|
. Thus, we derive

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}w(x,v)\mu_{\Theta}(x^{1},v_{\mathbf{b}})\mathrm{d}v\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{w(x,v)}{w^{\prime}(x,v)}\mathrm{d}v\lesssim 1.

(6.20)

For (
6.13
), from (
6.20
) and
\int_{\mathcal{V}_{2}}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{2}
is bounded, we have

\eqref{second team in f_exp}\lesssim\int_{\mathcal{V}_{1}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{2}<t/2<t^{1}}|f(t^{1},x^{1},v^{1})|\{n(x^{1})\cdot v^{1}\}\mathrm{d}v^{1}.

(6.21)

Since
t^{1}\geq 3t/4
and
t^{2}<t/2
, we get
t/4\leq t^{1}-t^{2}
. From (
1.17
), we have
|v^{1}|\geq|n(x^{1})\cdot v^{1}|\gtrsim gt/8
. Again, from the
L^{\infty}
-boundedness,
t\gg 1
and

,
we derive

\begin{split}\eqref{first step in second part in wf}&\lesssim\int_{|v^{1}|\gtrsim gt/8}w(x^{1},v^{1})|f(t^{1},x^{1},v^{1})|\mathrm{d}v^{1}\\
&\lesssim\int_{|v^{1}|\gtrsim gt/8}\frac{w(x^{1},v^{1})}{w^{\prime}(x^{1},v^{1})}\mathrm{d}v^{1}\|w^{\prime}f(0)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{\theta^{\prime}-\theta}}e^{-\frac{(\theta^{\prime}-\theta)t^{2}}{4}}\|w^{\prime}f(0)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}.\end{split}

(6.22)

Now we only need to bound (
6.14
). Since
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}w(x,v)\mu_{\Theta}(x^{1},v_{\mathbf{b}})\mathrm{d}v\lesssim 1
and
\int_{\mathcal{V}_{1}}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{1}
is bounded, it suffices to prove the decay of

\sup_{v\in\mathbb{R}^{3},v^{1}\in\mathcal{V}_{1}}\Big|\int_{\mathcal{V}_{2}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{2}\geq t/2}f(t^{2},x^{2},v^{2})\{n(x^{2})\cdot v^{2}\}\mathrm{d}v^{2}\Big|.

(6.23)

Here we define
g(t,x,v):=\varrho(t)w(x,v)f(t,x,v)
, and note that

\frac{1}{\varrho(t^{2})}\int_{\mathcal{V}_{2}}\frac{|n(x^{2})\cdot v^{2}|}{w(x^{2},v^{2})}g(t^{2},x^{2},v^{2})\mathrm{d}v^{2}=\int_{\mathcal{V}_{2}}f(t^{2},x^{2},v^{2})\{n(x^{2})\cdot v^{2}\}\mathrm{d}v^{2}.

Therefore, it suffices to show the decay of
\big|\frac{1}{\varrho(t^{2})}\int_{\mathcal{V}_{2}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{2}\geq t/2}\frac{|n(x^{2})\cdot v^{2}|}{w(x^{2},v^{2})}g(t^{2},x^{2},v^{2})\mathrm{d}v^{2}\big|
.

Applying Lemma
17
with
w(x,v)=e^{\theta(|v|^{2}+2\Phi(x))}
, and
\varrho(t)
in (
6.10
), and choosing
t_{*}=0
,
k\geq\mathfrak{C}t
as in Lemma
9
, we obtain the following stochastic cycle representation of

g(t^{2},x^{2},v^{2})=\varrho(t^{2})w(x^{2},v^{2})f(t^{2},x^{2},v^{2})
:

\displaystyle g(t^{2},x^{2},v^{2})=

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}_{t^{3}<0}\ \varrho(0)w(x^{2},v^{2})f(0,X(0;t^{2},x^{2},v^{2}),V(0;t^{2},x^{2},v^{2}))

(6.24)

\displaystyle+w(x^{2},v^{2})\int^{t^{2}}_{\max(0,t^{3})}\varrho^{\prime}(s)f(s,X(s;t^{2},x^{2},v^{2}),V(s;t^{2},x^{2},v^{2}))\mathrm{d}s

(6.25)

\displaystyle+w\mu_{\Theta}(x^{3},v^{2}_{\mathbf{b}})\int_{\prod_{j=3}^{i}\mathcal{V}_{j}}\sum\limits^{k-1}_{i=3}\Big\{\mathbf{1}_{t^{i+1}<0\leq t^{i}}\varrho(0)w(x^{i},v^{i})

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt\times f(0,X(0;t^{i},x^{i},v^{i}),V(0;t^{i},x^{i},v^{i}))\Big\}\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Sigma}_{i}

(6.26)

\displaystyle+w\mu_{\Theta}(x^{3},v^{2}_{\mathbf{b}})\int_{\prod_{j=3}^{i}\mathcal{V}_{j}}\sum\limits^{k-1}_{i=3}\mathbf{1}_{0\leq t^{i}}\Big\{\int^{t^{i}}_{\max(0,t^{i+1})}\varrho^{\prime}(s)w(x^{i},v^{i})

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt\times f(s,X(s;t^{i},x^{i},v^{i}),V(s;t^{i},x^{i},v^{i}))\mathrm{d}s\Big\}\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Sigma}_{i}

(6.27)

\displaystyle+w\mu_{\Theta}(x^{3},v^{2}_{\mathbf{b}})\int_{\prod_{j=3}^{k}\mathcal{V}_{j}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{k}\geq 0}\ g(t^{k},x^{k},v^{k})\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Sigma}_{k},

(6.28)

where

\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Sigma}_{i}:=\frac{\mathrm{d}\sigma_{i}}{\mu_{\Theta}(x^{i+1},v^{i})w(x^{i},v^{i})}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{i-1}\cdots\mathrm{d}\sigma_{3}
with
3\leq i\leq k
.
Here, we regard
t^{2},x^{2},v^{2}
as free parameters and from Lemma
3
, we have

\mu_{\Theta}(x^{3},v^{2}_{\mathbf{b}})=\mu_{\Theta}(x^{3},v^{2})
.

Next we estimate the contribution of (
6.24
)-(
6.28
) in

\frac{1}{\varrho(t^{2})}\int_{\mathcal{V}_{2}}\frac{|n(x^{2})\cdot v^{2}|}{w(x^{2},v^{2})}g(t^{2},x^{2},v^{2})\mathrm{d}v^{2}

term by term.

For the contribution of (
6.24
),
from
t^{2}\geq t/2
,
t^{3}\leq 0
and (
1.17
), we have
n(x^{2})\cdot v^{2}\gtrsim\frac{gt}{4}
. From the
L^{\infty}
-boundedness,
t\gg 1
and
0<n(x^{2})\cdot v^{2}\lesssim w(x^{2},v^{2})<w^{\prime}(x^{2},v^{2})
,
we deduce that

\begin{split}\frac{1}{\varrho(t^{2})}\int_{\mathcal{V}_{2}}\frac{|n(x^{2})\cdot v^{2}|}{w(x^{2},v^{2})}|\eqref{expand_g1}|\mathrm{d}v^{2}&=\frac{1}{\varrho(t^{2})}\int_{\mathcal{V}_{2}}|n(x^{2})\cdot v^{2}||\varrho(0)f(t^{2},x^{2},v^{2})|\mathrm{d}v^{2}\\
&\lesssim\frac{1}{\varrho(t^{2})}\int_{n(x^{2})\cdot v^{2}\gtrsim\frac{gt}{4}}\frac{|n(x^{2})\cdot v^{2}|}{w(x^{2},v^{2})}\varrho(0)\mathrm{d}v^{2}\|wf(0)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\\
&\lesssim\frac{1}{\varrho(t)}\varrho(0)\|wf(0)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\lesssim\frac{1}{\varrho(t)}\|w^{\prime}f(0)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}.\end{split}

(6.29)

Now we bound the contribution of (
6.25
). Recall Theorem
7
with
\varrho^{\prime}(t)\lesssim e^{\Lambda t}
and Lemma
27
, we get

\begin{split}\frac{1}{\varrho(t^{2})}\int_{\mathcal{V}_{2}}\frac{|n(x^{2})\cdot v^{2}|}{w(x^{2},v^{2})}|\eqref{expand_g2}|\mathrm{d}v^{2}&\lesssim\frac{1}{\varrho(t)}\int^{t}_{0}\|\varrho^{\prime}(s)f(s)\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}\mathrm{d}s\\
&\lesssim\frac{1}{\varrho(t)}\int^{t}_{0}\|e^{\Lambda s}f(s)\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}\mathrm{d}s\\
&\lesssim\frac{t}{\varrho(t)}\times\{\|w^{\prime}f(0)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}+\|\varphi\big((|v|^{2}+2\Phi(x))^{1/2}\big)f_{0}\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}\}.\end{split}

(6.30)

Next, we bound the contribution of (
6.26
). From the
L^{\infty}
-boundedness,

0<n(x^{2})\cdot v^{2}\lesssim w(x^{2},v^{2})<w^{\prime}(x^{2},v^{2})<\mu_{\Theta}^{-1}(x^{3},v^{2})
and
f(0,X(0;t^{i},x^{i},v^{i}),V(0;t^{i},x^{i},v^{i}))=f(t^{i},x^{i},v^{i})
, we derive

\begin{split}\frac{1}{\varrho(t^{2})}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{|n(x^{2})\cdot v^{2}|}{w(x^{2},v^{2})}|\eqref{expand_g3}|\mathrm{d}v^{2}&\lesssim\frac{k}{\varrho(t)}\bigg(\sup_{i}\int_{\prod_{j=3}^{i}\mathcal{V}_{j}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{i+1}<0\leq t^{i}}\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Sigma}_{i}\bigg)\varrho(0)\|wf(0)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\\
&\lesssim\frac{k}{\varrho(t)}\bigg(\int_{n(x^{i})\cdot v^{i}>0}\frac{|n(x^{i})\cdot v^{i}|}{w(x^{i},v^{i})}\mathrm{d}v^{i}\bigg)\|wf(0)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\\
&\lesssim\frac{k}{\varrho(t)}\|w^{\prime}f(0)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}},\end{split}

(6.31)

where the second line follows from Proposition
11
.

Again using Lemma
27
, Proposition
11
and Theorem
7
, we bound the contribution of (
6.27
). From
0<n(x^{2})\cdot v^{2}\lesssim\mu_{\Theta}^{-1}(x^{3},v^{2})
and
\varrho^{\prime}(t)\lesssim e^{\Lambda t}
, we have

\begin{split}&\ \ \ \ \frac{1}{\varrho(t^{2})}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{|n(x^{2})\cdot v^{2}|}{w(x^{2},v^{2})}|\eqref{expand_g4}|\mathrm{d}v^{2}\\
&\lesssim\frac{k}{\varrho(t)}\times\sup_{i}\int_{\prod_{j=3}^{i}\mathcal{V}_{j}}\mathbf{1}_{0\leq t^{i}}\int^{t^{i}}_{\max(0,t^{i+1})}w(x^{i},v^{i})\varrho^{\prime}(s)f(s,X(s;t^{i},x^{i},v^{i}),V(s;t^{i},x^{i},v^{i}))\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Sigma}_{i}\\
&\lesssim\frac{k}{\varrho(t)}\int^{t}_{0}\|\varrho^{\prime}(s)f(s)\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}\mathrm{d}s\lesssim\frac{k}{\varrho(t)}\int^{t}_{0}\|e^{\Lambda s}f(s)\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}\mathrm{d}s\\
&\lesssim\frac{kt}{\varrho(t)}\times\{\|w^{\prime}f(0)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}+\|\varphi\big((|v|^{2}+2\Phi(x))^{1/2}\big)f_{0}\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}\}.\end{split}

(6.32)

Lastly we bound the contribution of (
6.28
). From Lemma
9
, Proposition
11
and
0<n(x^{2})\cdot v^{2}\lesssim w(x^{2},v^{2})<w^{\prime}(x^{2},v^{2})
, we get

\begin{split}&\ \ \ \ \frac{1}{\varrho(t^{2})}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{|n(x^{2})\cdot v^{2}|}{w(x^{2},v^{2})}|\eqref{expand_g5}|\mathrm{d}v^{2}\\
&\lesssim\frac{\varrho(t^{k})}{\varrho(t^{2})}\sup_{(x,v)\in\bar{\Omega}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Big(\int_{\prod_{j=3}^{k-1}\mathcal{V}_{j}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{k}(t^{2},x^{2},v^{2},\cdots,v^{k-1})\geq 0}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{3}\cdots\mathrm{d}\sigma_{k-1}\Big)\sup_{t_{k}\geq 0}\|wf(t^{k})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\\
&\lesssim e^{-t}\|wf(0)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\lesssim e^{-t}\|w^{\prime}f(0)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}.\end{split}

(6.33)

Collecting estimates from (
6.29
)-(
6.33
) and using
k\lesssim t
, we derive

\big|\frac{1}{\varrho(t^{2})}\int_{\mathcal{V}_{2}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{2}\geq t/2}\frac{|n(x^{2})\cdot v^{2}|}{w(x^{2},v^{2})}g(t^{2},x^{2},v^{2})\mathrm{d}v^{2}\big|\leq\max\{\frac{1}{\varrho(t)},\frac{(k+1)t}{\varrho(t)},e^{-t}\}\lesssim\frac{\langle t\rangle^{2}}{\varrho(t)}.

(6.34)

Using
\varrho(t)=e^{\Lambda t}
,
0<w(x,v)<\mu_{\Theta}^{-1}(x,v)
and (
6.34
), we conclude

\eqref{f_exp2}\lesssim\frac{\langle t\rangle^{2}}{\varrho(t)}\lesssim e^{-\Lambda t}.

The above estimate, together with (
6.15
), (
6.19
) and (
6.22
), we prove (
0.13
).
∎

6.2.
With Magnet field

Proof of Theorem
2
of the case (
0.6
)
.

We first show (
0.12
). Setting
\varrho(t)=t^{6}+1
with
\varrho^{\prime}(t)\lesssim t^{5}+1
, we work on the stochastic cycle representation of
\varrho(t)w^{\prime}(x,v)f(t,x,v)
in (
6.1
)-(
6.4
).

For the contribution of (
6.1
), since
t^{1}<0
, and
w^{\prime},f
are constant along the characteristic trajectory, we deduce

\frac{1}{\varrho(t)}|\eqref{expand_k1}|=w^{\prime}(x,v)f(t,x,v)=w^{\prime}(X(0;t,x,v),V(0;t,x,v))f(0,X,V)\leq\|w^{\prime}f(0)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}.

(6.35)

Now we bound the contribution of (
6.2
). Using

0<n(x)\cdot v\lesssim w^{\prime}(x,v)<\mu^{-1}(x,v)
and Proposition
11
, we derive

\begin{split}\frac{1}{\varrho(t)}|\eqref{expand_k2}|\lesssim\frac{k}{\varrho(t)}\bigg(\sup_{i}\int_{\prod_{j=1}^{k}\mathcal{V}_{j}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{i+1}<0\leq t^{i}}\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Sigma}_{i}\bigg)\varrho(0)\|w^{\prime}f(0)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\lesssim\frac{k}{\varrho(t)}\|w^{\prime}f(0)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}.\end{split}

(6.36)

Next we bound the contribution of (
6.3
).
Following (
6.7
) and Lemma
28
with
\varepsilon=t^{-2}
, we have

\begin{split}&\ \ \ \ \frac{1}{\varrho(t)}|\eqref{expand_k3}|\\
&\lesssim\frac{k}{\varrho(t)}\sup_{1\leq i\leq k-1}\int_{\prod_{j=1}^{i}\mathcal{V}_{j}}\mathbf{1}_{0\leq t^{i}}\int^{t^{i}}_{\max(0,t^{i+1})}\varrho^{\prime}(s)w^{\prime}(X(s;t^{i},x^{i},v^{i}),V(s;t^{i},x^{i},v^{i}))f(s,X,V)\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Sigma}_{i}\\
&\lesssim\frac{k}{\varrho(t)}\big(\varepsilon^{i-1}\int_{\mathcal{V}_{i}}\mathbf{1}_{0\leq t^{i}}\ \varrho(t^{i})f(t^{i},x^{i},v^{i})\{n(x^{i})\cdot v^{i}\}\mathrm{d}v^{i}+(i-1)\varepsilon^{-2}\int^{t}_{0}\|\varrho^{\prime}(s)f(s)\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}\mathrm{d}s\big)\\
&\lesssim\frac{k}{\varrho(t)}\times\big(\varepsilon\sup_{t\geq 0}\|w^{\prime}f(t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\int_{\mathcal{V}_{i}}\mathbf{1}_{0\leq t^{i}}\frac{\varrho(t^{i})}{w^{\prime}(x^{i},v^{i})}\{n(x^{i})\cdot v^{i}\}\mathrm{d}v^{i}+k\varepsilon^{-2}\|w^{\prime}f(0)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\big)\\
&\lesssim\frac{k}{t^{2}}\times\sup_{t\geq 0}\|w^{\prime}f(t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}+\frac{k^{2}t^{4}}{\varrho(t)}\times\|w^{\prime}f(0)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}.\end{split}

(6.37)

Lastly we bound the contribution of (
6.4
).
Following from (
6.8
), Lemma
14
and Proposition
11
, we get

\begin{split}\frac{1}{\varrho(t)}|\eqref{expand_k4}|&\lesssim\frac{\varrho(t^{k})}{\varrho(t)}\sup_{(x,v)\in\bar{\Omega}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Big(\int_{\prod_{j=1}^{k-1}\mathcal{V}_{j}}\mathbf{1}_{t_{k}(t,x,v,v^{1},\cdots,v^{k-1})\geq 0}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{1}\cdots\mathrm{d}\sigma_{k-1}\Big)\|w^{\prime}f(t_{k})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\\
&\lesssim e^{-t}\sup_{t\geq s\geq 0}\|w^{\prime}f(s)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}.\end{split}

(6.38)

Collecting estimates from (
6.35
)-(
6.38
), and picking
k\lesssim t
and
0<\varepsilon
, we derive

(1-\frac{k}{t^{2}}-e^{-t})\sup_{t\geq 0}\|w^{\prime}f(t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\lesssim(1+\frac{k}{\varrho(t)}+\frac{k^{2}t^{4}}{\varrho(t)})\times\|w^{\prime}f(0)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}.

(6.39)

Therefore, we prove (
0.12
).

Next, we prove (
0.13
). To show the decay of exponential moments and again utilize the
L^{1}
-decay, we set the weight function
\varrho(t):=e^{\Lambda t}
such that
\varrho^{\prime}(t)\lesssim e^{\Lambda t}
.

From Lemma
17
, we derive the form of
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}w(x,v)|f(t,x,v)|\mathrm{d}v
. First we split out the case when
|v|\geq t/2
and
t_{1}\leq 3t/4
, and get (
6.40
)-(
6.41
). Next, for
t_{1}\geq 3t/4
, we follow along the stochastic cycles twice with
k=2
and
t_{*}=t/2
and get (
6.42
) and (
6.43
).

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}w(x,v)|f(t,x,v)|\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle\leq\int_{|v|\geq t/2}w(x,v)|f(t,x,v)|\mathrm{d}v

(6.40)

\displaystyle+\int_{|v|\leq t/2}\mathbf{1}_{t^{1}\leq 3t/4}w(x,v)|f(3t/4,X(3t/4;t,x,v),V(3t/4;t,x,v))|\mathrm{d}v

(6.41)

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{1}\geq 3t/4}w(x,v)\mu_{\Theta}(x^{1},v_{\mathbf{b}})\int_{\prod^{2}_{j=1}\mathcal{V}_{j}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{2}<t/2<t^{1}}w(x^{1},v^{1})|f(t^{1},x^{1},v^{1})|\mathrm{d}\Sigma_{1}^{2}\mathrm{d}v

(6.42)

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{1}\geq 3t/4}w(x,v)\mu_{\Theta}(x^{1},v_{\mathbf{b}})\Big|\int_{\prod^{2}_{j=1}\mathcal{V}_{j}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{2}\geq t/2}w(x^{2},v^{2})f(t^{2},x^{2},v^{2})\mathrm{d}\Sigma_{2}^{2}\Big|\mathrm{d}v,

(6.43)

where
\mathrm{d}{\Sigma}^{2}_{1}=\mathrm{d}\sigma_{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}\sigma_{1}}{\mu_{\Theta}(x^{2},v^{1})w(x^{1},v^{1})}
and
\mathrm{d}{\Sigma}^{2}_{2}=\frac{\mathrm{d}\sigma_{2}}{\mu_{\Theta}(x^{3},v^{2})w(x^{2},v^{2})}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{1}
with

\mathrm{d}\sigma_{j}=\mu_{\Theta}(x^{j+1},v^{j})\{n(x^{j})\cdot v^{j}\}\mathrm{d}v^{j}
for
j=1,2
.

For (
6.40
),
following from
|v|\geq t/2
and (
6.15
) we derive that

\begin{split}\int_{|v|\geq t/2}w(x,v)|f(t,x,v)|\mathrm{d}v&\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{\theta^{\prime}-\theta}}e^{-\frac{(\theta^{\prime}-\theta)t^{2}}{4}}\|w^{\prime}f(0)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}.\end{split}

(6.44)

For (
6.41
), from
|v|\leq t/2
and
t^{1}\leq 3t/4
, we have

|V(3t/4;t,x,v)|\gtrsim|v_{3}+g(t-3t/4)|\geq 2t\geq|v|+t.

(6.45)

Then, from the
L^{\infty}
-boundedness, (
6.45
) and
0<w<w^{\prime}
, we deduce that

\begin{split}\eqref{first team in f_exp mag}&\lesssim\int_{|v|\leq t/2}\mathbf{1}_{t^{1}\leq 3t/4}\frac{w(x,v)}{w^{\prime}(X(3t/4;t,x,v),V(3t/4;t,x,v))}\mathrm{d}v\|w^{\prime}f(0)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\\
&\leq\int_{|v|\leq t/2}\mathbf{1}_{t^{1}\leq 3t/4}e^{(\theta-\theta^{\prime})(|V(3t/4;t,x,v)|^{2}+\Phi(X))}\mathrm{d}v\|w^{\prime}f(0)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\\
&\leq\int_{|v|\leq t/2}e^{(\theta-\theta^{\prime})(|v|^{2}+t^{2})}\mathrm{d}v\|w^{\prime}f(0)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{\theta^{\prime}-\theta}}e^{-(\theta^{\prime}-\theta)t^{2}}\|w^{\prime}f(0)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}.\end{split}

(6.46)

Next, we bound
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}w(x,v)\mu_{\Theta}(x^{1},v_{\mathbf{b}})\mathrm{d}v
shown in (
6.42
) and (
6.43
).
From
|v|=|v_{\mathbf{b}}|\geq|V(s;t,x,v)|
, we derive

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}w(x,v)\mu_{\Theta}(x^{1},v_{\mathbf{b}})\mathrm{d}v\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{w(x,v)}{w^{\prime}(x,v)}\mathrm{d}v\lesssim 1.

(6.47)

For (
6.42
), from (
6.47
), (
6.22
) and
\int_{\mathcal{V}_{2}}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{2}
is bounded, we have

\begin{split}\eqref{second team in f_exp mag}&\lesssim\int_{|v^{1}|\geq 5t/4}w(x^{1},v^{1})|f(t^{1},x^{1},v^{1})|\mathrm{d}v^{1}\\
&\lesssim\int_{|v^{1}|\geq 5t/4}w(x^{1},v^{1})|f(t^{1},x^{1},v^{1})|\mathrm{d}v^{1}\\
&\lesssim\int_{|v^{1}|\geq 5t/4}\frac{w(x^{1},v^{1})}{w^{\prime}(x^{1},v^{1})}\mathrm{d}v^{1}\|w^{\prime}f(0)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{\theta^{\prime}-\theta}}e^{-\frac{(\theta^{\prime}-\theta)t^{2}}{4}}\|w^{\prime}f(0)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}.\end{split}

(6.48)

Now we only need to bound (
6.43
). Since
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}w(x,v)\mu_{\Theta}(x^{1},v_{\mathbf{b}})\mathrm{d}v\lesssim 1
and
\int_{\mathcal{V}_{1}}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{1}
is bounded, it suffices to prove the decay of

\sup_{v\in\mathbb{R}^{3},v^{1}\in\mathcal{V}_{1}}\Big|\int_{\mathcal{V}_{2}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{2}\geq t/2}f(t^{2},x^{2},v^{2})\{n(x^{2})\cdot v^{2}\}\mathrm{d}v^{2}\Big|.

(6.49)

Here we define
g(t,x,v):=\varrho(t)w(x,v)f(t,x,v)
, and note that

\frac{1}{\varrho(t^{2})}\int_{\mathcal{V}_{2}}\frac{|n(x^{2})\cdot v^{2}|}{w(x^{2},v^{2})}g(t^{2},x^{2},v^{2})\mathrm{d}v^{2}=\int_{\mathcal{V}_{2}}f(t^{2},x^{2},v^{2})\{n(x^{2})\cdot v^{2}\}\mathrm{d}v^{2}.

Therefore, it suffices to show the decay of
\big|\frac{1}{\varrho(t^{2})}\int_{\mathcal{V}_{2}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{2}\geq t/2}\frac{|n(x^{2})\cdot v^{2}|}{w(x^{2},v^{2})}g(t^{2},x^{2},v^{2})\mathrm{d}v^{2}\big|
.

Applying Lemma
17
with
w(x,v)
and
\varrho(t)
,
we choose
t_{*}=0
,
k\geq\mathfrak{C}t
, and obtain the following stochastic cycle representation of

g(t^{2},x^{2},v^{2})=\varrho(t^{2})w(x^{2},v^{2})f(t^{2},x^{2},v^{2})
:

\displaystyle g(t^{2},x^{2},v^{2})=

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}_{t^{3}<0}\ \varrho(0)w(x^{2},v^{2})f(0,X(0;t^{2},x^{2},v^{2}),V(0;t^{2},x^{2},v^{2}))

(6.50)

\displaystyle+w(x^{2},v^{2})\int^{t^{2}}_{\max(0,t^{3})}\varrho^{\prime}(s)f(s,X(s;t^{2},x^{2},v^{2}),V(s;t^{2},x^{2},v^{2}))\mathrm{d}s

(6.51)

\displaystyle+w\mu_{\Theta}(x^{3},v^{2}_{\mathbf{b}})\int_{\prod_{j=3}^{i}\mathcal{V}_{j}}\sum\limits^{k-1}_{i=3}\Big\{\mathbf{1}_{t^{i+1}<0\leq t^{i}}\varrho(0)w(x^{i},v^{i})

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt\times f(0,X(0;t^{i},x^{i},v^{i}),V(0;t^{i},x^{i},v^{i}))\Big\}\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Sigma}_{i}

(6.52)

\displaystyle+w\mu_{\Theta}(x^{3},v^{2}_{\mathbf{b}})\int_{\prod_{j=3}^{i}\mathcal{V}_{j}}\sum\limits^{k-1}_{i=3}\mathbf{1}_{0\leq t^{i}}\Big\{\int^{t^{i}}_{\max(0,t^{i+1})}\varrho^{\prime}(s)w(x^{i},v^{i})

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt\times f(s,X(s;t^{i},x^{i},v^{i}),V(s;t^{i},x^{i},v^{i}))\mathrm{d}s\Big\}\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Sigma}_{i}

(6.53)

\displaystyle+w\mu_{\Theta}(x^{3},v^{2}_{\mathbf{b}})\int_{\prod_{j=3}^{k}\mathcal{V}_{j}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{k}\geq 0}\ g(t^{k},x^{k},v^{k})\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Sigma}_{k},

(6.54)

where

\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Sigma}_{i}:=\frac{\mathrm{d}\sigma_{i}}{\mu_{\Theta}(x^{i+1},v^{i})w(x^{i},v^{i})}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{i-1}\cdots\mathrm{d}\sigma_{3}
with
3\leq i\leq k
.
Here, we regard
t^{2},x^{2},v^{2}
as free parameters and from Lemma
5
, we have

\mu_{\Theta}(x^{3},v^{2}_{\mathbf{b}})=\mu_{\Theta}(x^{3},v^{2})
.

Next we estimate the contribution of (
6.50
)-(
6.54
) in

\frac{1}{\varrho(t^{2})}\int_{\mathcal{V}_{2}}\frac{|n(x^{2})\cdot v^{2}|}{w(x^{2},v^{2})}g(t^{2},x^{2},v^{2})\mathrm{d}v^{2}

term by term.

For the contribution of (
6.50
),
from
t^{2}\geq t/2
,
t^{3}\leq 0
, we have
n(x^{2})\cdot v^{2}\gtrsim\frac{gt}{4}
. From the
L^{\infty}
-boundedness,
t\gg 1
and
0<n(x^{2})\cdot v^{2}\lesssim w(x^{2},v^{2})<w^{\prime}(x^{2},v^{2})
, we deduce that

\begin{split}\frac{1}{\varrho(t^{2})}\int_{\mathcal{V}_{2}}\frac{|n(x^{2})\cdot v^{2}|}{w(x^{2},v^{2})}|\eqref{expand_g1 mag}|\mathrm{d}v^{2}&=\frac{1}{\varrho(t^{2})}\int_{\mathcal{V}_{2}}|n(x^{2})\cdot v^{2}||\varrho(0)f(t^{2},x^{2},v^{2})|\mathrm{d}v^{2}\\
&\lesssim\frac{\varrho(0)}{\varrho(t)}\|wf(0)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\lesssim\frac{1}{\varrho(t)}\|w^{\prime}f(0)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}.\end{split}

(6.55)

Now we bound the contribution of (
6.51
). Following (
6.30
) and Lemma
28
with
\varepsilon=\varrho^{-\frac{1}{3}}(t^{2})
, we get

\begin{split}&\ \ \ \ \frac{1}{\varrho(t^{2})}\int_{\mathcal{V}_{2}}\frac{|n(x^{2})\cdot v^{2}|}{w(x^{2},v^{2})}|\eqref{expand_g2 mag}|\mathrm{d}v^{2}\\
&\lesssim\frac{1}{\varrho(t^{2})}\int_{\mathcal{V}_{2}}\frac{|n(x^{2})\cdot v^{2}|}{w(x^{2},v^{2})}w(x^{2},v^{2})\int^{t^{2}}_{\max(0,t^{3})}\varrho^{\prime}(s)f(t^{2},x^{2},v^{2})\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}v^{2}\\
&\lesssim\frac{1}{\varrho(t^{2})}\times\big(\varepsilon\int_{\mathcal{V}_{i}}\mathbf{1}_{0\leq t^{2}}\ \varrho(t^{2})f(t^{2},x^{2},v^{2})\{n(x^{2})\cdot v^{2}\}\mathrm{d}v^{2}+\varepsilon^{-2}\int^{t}_{0}\|\varrho^{\prime}(s)f(s)\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}\mathrm{d}s\big)\\
&\lesssim\frac{1}{\varrho(t)}\times\|w^{\prime}f(0)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}.\end{split}

(6.56)

Next, we bound the contribution of (
6.52
).
Following from (
6.31
) and Proposition
11
, we derive

\begin{split}\frac{1}{\varrho(t^{2})}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{|n(x^{2})\cdot v^{2}|}{w(x^{2},v^{2})}|\eqref{expand_g3 mag}|\mathrm{d}v^{2}&\lesssim\frac{k}{\varrho(t)}\bigg(\sup_{i}\int_{\prod_{j=3}^{i}\mathcal{V}_{j}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{i+1}<0\leq t^{i}}\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Sigma}_{i}\bigg)\varrho(0)\|wf(0)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\\
&\lesssim\frac{k}{\varrho(t)}\|w^{\prime}f(0)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}.\end{split}

(6.57)

Using Lemma
28
with
\varepsilon=\varrho^{-\frac{1}{3}}(t^{2})
, Proposition
11
and Theorem
7
, we bound the contribution of (
6.53
) as

\begin{split}&\ \ \ \ \frac{1}{\varrho(t^{2})}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{|n(x^{2})\cdot v^{2}|}{w(x^{2},v^{2})}|\eqref{expand_g4 mag}|\mathrm{d}v^{2}\\
&\lesssim\frac{k}{\varrho(t)}\times\sup_{i}\int_{\prod_{j=3}^{i}\mathcal{V}_{j}}\mathbf{1}_{0\leq t^{i}}\int^{t^{i}}_{\max(0,t^{i+1})}w(x^{i},v^{i})\varrho^{\prime}(s)f(s,X(s;t^{i},x^{i},v^{i}),V(s;t^{i},x^{i},v^{i}))\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Sigma}_{i}\\
&\lesssim\frac{k}{\varrho(t)}\times\big(\varepsilon^{i-1}\int_{\mathcal{V}_{i}}\mathbf{1}_{0\leq t^{i}}\ \varrho(t^{i})f(t^{i},x^{i},v^{i})\{n(x^{i})\cdot v^{i}\}\mathrm{d}v^{i}+(i-1)\varepsilon^{-2}\int^{t}_{0}\|\varrho^{\prime}(s)f(s)\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}\mathrm{d}s\big)\\
&\lesssim\frac{k}{\varrho(t)}\times\big(\varepsilon^{i-1}\|w^{\prime}f(t^{i})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\int_{\mathcal{V}_{i}}\mathbf{1}_{0\leq t^{i}}\frac{\varrho(t^{i})}{w^{\prime}(x^{i},v^{i})}\{n(x^{i})\cdot v^{i}\}\mathrm{d}v^{i}+k\varepsilon^{-2}\int^{t}_{0}\|\varrho^{\prime}(s)f(s)\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}\mathrm{d}s\big)\\
&\lesssim\frac{k}{\varrho(t)}\times\big(\varepsilon\|w^{\prime}f(0)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}+k\varepsilon^{-2}\int^{t}_{0}\|e^{\Lambda s}f(s)\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}\mathrm{d}s\big)\\
&\lesssim\frac{k^{2}t}{\varrho(t)}\times\{\|w^{\prime}f(0)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}+\|\varphi\big((|v|^{2}+2\Phi(x))^{1/2}\big)f_{0}\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}\}.\end{split}

(6.58)

Lastly we bound the contribution of (
6.54
).
Following (
6.33
) and Lemma
14
, we get

\begin{split}&\ \ \ \ \frac{1}{\varrho(t^{2})}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{|n(x^{2})\cdot v^{2}|}{w(x^{2},v^{2})}|\eqref{expand_g5 mag}|\mathrm{d}v^{2}\\
&\lesssim\frac{\varrho(t^{k})}{\varrho(t^{2})}\sup_{(x,v)\in\bar{\Omega}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Big(\int_{\prod_{j=3}^{k-1}\mathcal{V}_{j}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{k}(t^{2},x^{2},v^{2},\cdots,v^{k-1})\geq 0}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{3}\cdots\mathrm{d}\sigma_{k-1}\Big)\sup_{t_{k}\geq 0}\|wf(t^{k})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\\
&\lesssim e^{-t}\|w^{\prime}f(0)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}.\end{split}

(6.59)

Collecting estimates from (
6.55
)-(
6.59
) and using
k\lesssim t
, we derive

\big|\frac{1}{\varrho(t^{2})}\int_{\mathcal{V}_{2}}\mathbf{1}_{t^{2}\geq t/2}\frac{|n(x^{2})\cdot v^{2}|}{w(x^{2},v^{2})}g(t^{2},x^{2},v^{2})\mathrm{d}v^{2}\big|\leq\max\{\frac{1}{\varrho(t)},\frac{(k^{2}+1)t}{\varrho(t)},e^{-t}\}\lesssim\frac{\langle t\rangle^{3}}{\varrho(t)}.

(6.60)

Using
\varrho(t)=e^{\Lambda t}
,
0<w(x,v)<\mu_{\Theta}^{-1}(x,v)
and (
6.60
), we conclude
\eqref{f_exp2 mag}\lesssim\frac{\langle t\rangle^{3}}{\varrho(t)}\lesssim e^{-\Lambda t}.
The above estimate, together with (
6.44
), (
6.46
) and (
6.48
), we prove (
0.13
).
∎
